$\mathcal C(c)=(K,\ell,E,H,R,D,B)$.  The certificate may be read as saying that a
candidate is admitted to the publication-facing package only when the promotion
field $H$ records the human editorial act and the corresponding route-gate pass
record in $R$ names the active-paper marker surface required by the promotion
gate.  A record containing only a human-promotion note but no active-paper marker
surface does not certify daemon visibility, and a record containing only a
daemon-visible \path{2026_*} marker surface but no human-promotion field does not
certify editorial promotion.  In particular, the intake-isolation row $a_3$ of
Tables~\ref{tab:current-acceptance-ledger} and~\ref{tab:current-certificate-ledger}
supports exactly the bounded lesson that the intake packet remains outside
daemon-visible publication work until both sides of the promotion interface are
recorded; it does not identify human promotion with daemon enumeration.
\end{corollary}

\begin{proof}
Clause A4 of Definition~\ref{def:certificate-schema-acceptance-table} assigns the
route-boundary information to the certificate coordinate $H$.  The route-gate
part of Definition~\ref{def:operational-acceptance-interface} is a different
finite coordinate: its pass record belongs to $R$ and its evidence surface is the
active-paper marker surface, namely a human-promotion act producing a
\path{2026_*} directory with \path{main.tex} and \path{PIPELINE.md}.  Thus the
schema checks two typed fields, not one untyped publication-status fact.  By
Theorem~\ref{thm:record-acceptance-exactness}, the finite verifier accepts a
route-boundary certificate exactly when all required finite schema predicates
hold.  If the active-paper marker surface is missing, then the route-gate pass
record lacks its required evidence pointer in $R$; by
Theorem~\ref{thm:mixed-claim-projection-obstruction} this is an
inclusion-minimal bad subrecord for the daemon-visible reading.  If the
human-promotion field is missing, then A4 fails for the route-boundary crossing;
this is a distinct missing-coordinate obstruction in $H$.  Proposition~\ref{prop:gate-interface-separation}
prevents repairing either bad subrecord by passage through overlap,
submission-state, scheduler, discovery, artifact, or theorem-content gates.

For row $a_3$, the acceptance ledger names the intake seed and active-paper
detector paths, while the certificate ledger records the marker that the intake
seed does not cross into publication work before promotion.  The case-witness
table states the same failed active-paper invariant and detector block.  Applying
the preceding paragraph to these finite coordinates gives the advertised bounded
reading: inactive intake is separated from publication work by the promotion
interface, but the certificate does not collapse the editorial act and daemon
marker enumeration into a single equivalent notion.\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[Scheduler selection is advisory before discovery gating]
\label{prop:scheduler-selection-advisory}
Let $s$ be a scheduler or critical-path score attached to a candidate claim
$c$.  If $s$ recommends selecting $c$, then the recommendation is a
work-selection claim and by itself can only pass the scheduler gate.  If $c$ is
also asserted to be a discovery or classifier-shifting contribution in a
record-gate-sound pipeline, then the accepted mixed claim must pass both the scheduler
gate and the discovery gate.
Consequently, critical-path scores may prioritize review order, and the imported
classifier-shift selection rule may break ties among otherwise equal discovery
potential coordinates, but neither score nor recommendation accepts a
classifier-shift discovery unless the discovery gate also supplies the required
certificate, ledger, and lineage witnesses.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
By the definition of $\Gamma$, a scheduler recommendation has claim kind
work-selection, so its atomic gate is the scheduler gate.  The scheduler gate's
evidence surface records a score, backlog window, or analogous critical-path
priority; none of these fields is a semantic naming certificate, a ledger
witness, a DNA-complete theorem genome, a score certificate for a discovery
claim, a lineage witness, or a not-claimed boundary.  Hence scheduler passage
alone records only that $c$ is an eligible or prioritized work item.

Now suppose that the same recorded assertion also says that $c$ is a discovery
or classifier-shifting contribution.  Then the assertion is mixed: it has a
work-selection component and a discovery component.  Proposition~\ref{prop:claim-kind-dispatch}
therefore requires passage through every gate in the union assigned by $\Gamma$,
namely the scheduler gate for the work-selection component and the pinned
discovery gate for the discovery component.  The source-interface record in
Table~\ref{tab:source-interface-record} includes the imported classifier-change
selection theorem only in its bounded form: with positive classifier-change
weight and all other coordinates fixed, such a selection operator favors the
classifier-changing candidate.  The same source-interface record also includes
pipeline safety, which requires certificate, ledger, genome, score, lineage, and
boundary witnesses for accepted discovery.  Combining these two imported
interfaces gives the stated conclusion: scheduling and classifier-shift scoring
can advise selection order, but discovery acceptance still occurs only through
the discovery gate.\end{proof}

The next result is the concrete verifier/interface contribution for the workshop
route.  It constructs a finite certificate from submitted rows, proves that the
certificate is exact for the acceptance relation above, and classifies all
properly stronger readings by the preorder of
Definition~\ref{def:stronger-reading-preorder}.  Its sharpness is the finite
extension-budget normal form of
Theorem~\ref{thm:finite-extension-budget-normal-form}, not an
implementation-soundness or semantic-correctness theorem for the underlying
tools.  The load-bearing trust boundary is hypothesis~(2), record-gate
soundness: without the finite predicate $\mathsf{RGS}_{S}(P,L,\Gamma)$ tying
ledger entries to assigned passing rows and submitted evidence pointers, the
theorem has no certificate constructor to apply.  The schema-relative exactness
and finite-coordinate classifier of
Theorems~\ref{thm:record-acceptance-exactness},
\ref{thm:finite-coordinate-determination-boundary}, and
\ref{thm:record-interface-rigidity} then ensure that the resulting certificate
proves only the finite-record claim it records and remains machine-readable only
at a common refreshed support-surface fixed point.

\label{tab:publication-safety-dependency-map}

\begin{proposition}[Minimal bad-record witness for command-run escalation]
\label{prop:publication-safety-minimal-bad-record-witness}
In the operational interface of
Definition~\ref{def:operational-acceptance-interface}, let $r$ be a submitted
claim record whose claim-kind set is \{route\}, whose evidence level is declared
to be command-run, and whose boundary field contains no command-run tuple
$(\text{command},\text{source state},\text{environment},\text{exit code},
\text{log path})$.  Suppose all other route coordinates of $r$ are filled:
there is an evidence pointer, a human-promotion marker at the declared boundary,
and pass rows for the promotion, overlap, and submission-state gates.  Then the
schema verifier rejects $r$, and the inclusion-minimal bad subrecord is exactly
the pair consisting of the evidence-level declaration ``command-run'' and the
boundary field missing the five command-run coordinates.  In particular, adding
more prose, advisory scores, route history, or path-verified evidence surfaces
cannot repair the rejected stronger reading unless the missing command-run tuple
is added as a finite coordinate.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
For a route claim, the dispatch table in
Definition~\ref{def:operational-acceptance-interface} requires the promotion,
overlap, and submission-state gates.  By hypothesis those pass rows and the
route-boundary marker are already present, so clauses A1--A4 and A6 of
Definition~\ref{def:certificate-schema-acceptance-table} have no missing route,
evidence-surface, gate-coverage, or promotion coordinate to report.  The only
remaining clause that reads the declared evidence level is the command-run part
of the boundary condition: at command-run strength the boundary field must
contain the command, immutable source state, environment, exit code, and log
path.  These five fields are absent by hypothesis, so the verifier rejects.

The bad subrecord is inclusion-minimal.  If the evidence-level declaration is
removed, the command-run boundary obligation is no longer triggered by the
record.  If the boundary field is replaced by one containing the five command-run
coordinates, the displayed obstruction disappears and the record must be tested
against the remaining clauses, which were assumed filled.  Deleting any unrelated
prose, advisory score, route-history note, or path-verified surface does not add
the missing five fields and therefore cannot change the failed command-run
clause.  Thus the rejected stronger reading is witnessed by a finite bad record,
not by a semantic judgment about the surrounding pipeline.  This is the concrete
negative instance used by Theorem~\ref{thm:publication-safety-interface},
Proposition~\ref{prop:publication-safety-maximality}, and
Theorem~\ref{thm:finite-extension-budget-normal-form} when a path-verified route
or submission row is silently upgraded to command-run strength.
\end{proof}
\begin{theorem}[Bounded software-audit interface under record-gate soundness]
\label{thm:publication-safety-interface}
Consider a theory-to-paper pipeline with the operational interface above and a
finite submitted record surface consisting only of the manuscript source, printed
tables, submitted manifests, uploaded review-bundle records, recorded logs, and
explicitly declared historical boundaries.  Assume the following four distinct
finite-record hypotheses.
\begin{enumerate}
\item \emph{Acceptance entries}: every load-bearing claim in an active manuscript
is either rejected or has an acceptance entry with an evidence surface inside
this finite submitted record surface.
\item \emph{Record-gate soundness}: writing $S$ for this finite submitted record
surface and $L$ for the acceptance ledger, the package satisfies the finite
predicate $\mathsf{RGS}_{S}(P,L,\Gamma)$ of
Definitions~\ref{def:operational-acceptance-interface} and
\ref{def:record-gate-soundness-external-interface} as proved equivalent in
Proposition~\ref{prop:record-gate-soundness-external-interface-exactness} and as
delimited by Theorem~\ref{thm:assigned-gate-soundness-audit-boundary}: every recorded
acceptance entry has the assigned passing rows and submitted evidence pointers,
every advisory output is demoted unless the deterministic gate set $\Gamma$ for
its claim kind passes, and no independent implementation-soundness,
fresh-execution, or semantic-correctness claim about the gates is inferred unless
the package also contains the corresponding source path, command, source state,
environment, exit code, and log path.
\item \emph{Discovery certificate input}: discovery claims are checked through
the pinned discovery-gate interface certified by
Lemma~\ref{lem:source-interface-extraction-correctness} and
Propositions~\ref{prop:reviewable-source-snapshot-interface},
\ref{prop:source-interface-record-file-consistency}, and
\ref{prop:source-interface-evidence-level-separation}, in particular the
separated manual/path-verified and executable finite-record levels for the
pipeline safety and main pipeline theorem labels.
\item \emph{Promotion and verifier boundary}: no inactive intake seed is treated
as publication work without human promotion, and the constructed tuple below is
submitted to the finite schema verifier rather than being identified with the
acceptance entry itself.
\end{enumerate}
Then, and only under these finite-record hypotheses, the package checker has the
following bounded software-audit invariant.  The checker input is the submitted surface
$S$, the ledger $L$, the dispatch table $\Gamma$, and the schema files and bundle
records named in Proposition~\ref{prop:finite-certificate-schema-verifier}.  Its
output is the finite accepted set
\[
\mathsf{Out}_{\Gamma,S}(L)=
\{(c,\mathsf{Cert}_{\Gamma}(c)):\ V(c,\mathsf{Cert}_{\Gamma}(c))=1\}
\]
together with the finite list of rejected bad subrecords.  For every accepted
load-bearing claim $c$ it constructs, from the acceptance entry and the submitted
finite evidence surfaces, a record
\[
\mathsf{Cert}_{\Gamma}(c)=\mathcal C(c)=(K,\ell,E,H,R,D,B)
\]
accepted by the finite schema verifier of
Proposition~\ref{prop:finite-certificate-schema-verifier} with the input--predicate--output
boundary of Theorem~\ref{thm:verifier-input-output-contract}; equivalently, by the
schema-relative finite-record exactness of Theorem~\ref{thm:record-acceptance-exactness}, the
constructed record satisfies the schema-relative record-acceptance semantics for
the dispatch table $\Gamma$.  Consequently, within the submitted finite records
and under the stated explicit record-gate-soundness hypothesis, $c$ has a recorded evidence
surface, a declared evidence boundary, and a prior pass record for every gate
required for its claim kind.  Accepted discovery claims additionally inherit the
bounded pinned source-interface consequences that statement-only, code-only,
mechanical-expansion, and compression-only outputs are not accepted as discovery
without the required certificate, ledger, and lineage witnesses.  Moreover, the
constructed record is axis-and-level invariant and sharp for the stronger-reading
preorder: if $c\preceq_{\Gamma}c^{+}$ is a properly stronger reading that moves
a load-bearing consequence from one architecture axis of
Definition~\ref{def:architecture-axis-map} to another, raises the evidence level
from path-verified to command-run strength, adds an implementation, artifact,
venue, or semantic-correctness assertion, or adds a theorem-obligation
completeness assertion, then $c^{+}$ is accepted by the same interface if and only
if the submitted record surface contains the corresponding finite extension
coordinates and those records pass the assigned gates.  If those coordinates are
absent, the accepted certificate is maximal below $c^{+}$: it still accepts $c$
and rejects the stronger reading by a finite bad subrecord.

The dependency order for this sharpness assertion is made explicit in
Proposition~\ref{prop:publication-safety-dependency-normal-form}: construction
and schema-relative exactness first, extension-budget necessity and sufficiency
second, and maximality third.  The maximality of this constructed certificate is
stated separately in Proposition~\ref{prop:publication-safety-maximality}, and
the exact finite price of any genuine strengthening is classified by
Theorem~\ref{thm:finite-extension-budget-normal-form}; the current-package
instantiation is stated separately in
Corollary~\ref{cor:publication-safety-current-package-instance} and refined by
Theorem~\ref{thm:current-package-certificate-exactness}.  The concrete boundary
readings most likely to be mistaken for stronger workflow, script, source,
artifact, inventory, or support claims are classified uniformly in
Theorem~\ref{thm:named-boundary-positive-coordinate-classification}, and the
current five-coordinate skeleton is proved not to glue into implementation,
semantic, artifact, proof-assistant, daemon, or venue soundness by
Theorem~\ref{thm:current-boundary-skeleton-non-gluing}.

The conclusion is therefore an audit-interface theorem rather than an end-to-end
pipeline-correctness theorem.  It proves only that the finite submitted records
determine the accepted certificate tuples and the finite bad-subrecord reports
under the declared record-gate predicate.  It does not prove, unless separately
added as extension coordinates, gate implementation soundness, Lean/BEDC rebuild
correctness, artifact semantics, daemon behavior, upload, archive availability,
venue compliance at upload time, or venue acceptance.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
We verify the obligations in the order advertised in the statement.

\emph{Package-checker construction.}  The checker first reads the finite record
surface $S$ and ledger $L$; no prose paragraph or live daemon state is queried.
For each ledger row it forms the candidate tuple
$(K,\ell,E,H,R,D,B)$, runs the six predicates $P_1,\ldots,P_6$, appends
$(c,\mathsf{Cert}_{\Gamma}(c))$ to $\mathsf{Out}_{\Gamma,S}(L)$ on acceptance,
and otherwise appends the least failed predicate together with the fields read by
that predicate to the rejected-subrecord list.  Let $c$ be an accepted
load-bearing claim in the manuscript.  The acceptance-entry hypothesis supplies
the claim-kind set $K$, evidence level $\ell$, and evidence surface $E$, so the
decision is attached to a source path, artifact path, command log, inventory, or
witness record rather than to generated prose alone.  Record-gate-soundness
supplies the pass-record field $R$ for every gate assigned by $\Gamma$.  If
accepting $c$ crosses from intake or source material into daemon-visible
publication work, the promotion assumption supplies the human-promotion field
$H$; otherwise $H$ records that no such boundary is crossed.  The evidence-level
definition supplies the boundary field $B$: either the command-run fields are
present or the record explicitly remains path-verified.  If $c$ is a discovery
claim, the discovery-routing assumption supplies $D$ from the pinned
source-interface rows; otherwise $D$ is absent as required by the certificate
schema.  Thus the constructor
\(\mathsf{Cert}_{\Gamma}(c)=\mathcal C(c)=(K,\ell,E,H,R,D,B)\) is determined by
finite submitted records and is locally checkable whenever its cited paths and
manifest rows lie in the submitted artifact, review bundle, or explicit
historical boundary.  The output invariant follows from the same case split and
from Theorem~\ref{thm:verifier-input-output-contract}: accepted rows are exactly
those for which all six predicates hold, and rejected rows carry the finite bad
subrecord defined by the first failed predicate, with no output channel for
external gate implementation soundness, end-to-end semantic correctness,
proof-assistant correctness, artifact semantic validation, fresh case reruns,
archive availability, or venue acceptance.  The same construction verifies
$\mathsf{Acc}_{\Gamma}(c,\mathcal C)$, so
\emph{Record exactness.}  The schema-relative finite-record exactness of
Theorem~\ref{thm:record-acceptance-exactness} identifies the constructed
certificate with the paper's independently stated record-acceptance semantics,
not merely with an informal unpacking of the tuple.

\emph{Axis and evidence-level invariant.}  The record just constructed exposes
exactly the axes and levels on which $c$ is allowed to rely.  If a proposed
consequence of $c$ needs evidence on a different architecture axis,
Proposition~\ref{prop:architecture-axis-non-escalation} forces a separate
submitted coordinate for that axis and a pass record for the gates assigned to
that coordinate.  If the proposed consequence instead raises the evidence level
from path-verified to command-run strength, clause~A6 of
Definition~\ref{def:certificate-schema-acceptance-table} forces the command,
source state, environment, exit code, and log path into $B$.  In both cases,
Theorem~\ref{thm:finite-extension-budget-normal-form} identifies the added
finite coordinates as the positive extension budget for the stronger reading.
If those coordinates are absent, Definition~\ref{def:stronger-reading-preorder}
identifies the proposed assertion as a properly stronger reading without the
extension coordinates required for acceptance.  Theorem~\ref{thm:record-acceptance-exactness}
then returns the same original certificate and no cross-axis, cross-level,
implementation, artifact-semantic, venue, or theorem-completeness consequence is
certified.  The failed coordinate is a finite bad subrecord because it is either a
missing kind in $K$, a missing gate record in $R$, a missing command tuple in $B$,
or an evidence item outside the submitted surface $E$.

\emph{Gate coverage.}  Propositions~\ref{prop:claim-kind-dispatch} and
\ref{prop:gate-interface-separation} identify the required finite gate set:
formal-source claims use build and axiom gates, traceability claims use inventory
and marker gates, route claims use promotion, overlap, and submission-state
gates, artifact claims use status and limitation-ledger gates, submission claims
use submission-pack gates, extension-style discovery claims use the
theorem-content delta gate together with the pinned discovery gate,
work-selection claims use the scheduler gate, and mixed claims use the union.
Thus $c$ has a prior pass record for the gates required by its kind.  The
advisory-output assumption prevents an agent draft, review packet, score, or
repair proposal from being promoted by prose alone: without the relevant pass
records it remains demoted.  The promotion assumption similarly prevents an
inactive intake seed from becoming publication work merely because a local source
path exists.

\emph{Discovery boundary.}  If $c$ is a discovery claim, the discovery-routing assumption sends it through
the pinned discovery-gate interface.  Proposition~\ref{prop:reviewable-source-snapshot-interface}
supplies the corresponding reviewer-facing snapshot interface, so this routing
does not depend on a reviewer's access to the author's absolute source path.  The
record-file consistency certificate in
Proposition~\ref{prop:source-interface-record-file-consistency} further shows, at
the finite record-comparison level, that the manuscript table, JSON mirror,
review-bundle manifest, digest, line count, and bundled source snapshot name the
same finite imported interface.  The
source-interface table records the imported statements used here:
statement-only, code-only, mechanical-expansion, and compression-only records do
not become accepted discovery claims without the required certificate, ledger,
and lineage witnesses, and classifier-shift selection remains bounded by the
discovery gate as shown in Proposition~\ref{prop:scheduler-selection-advisory}.
Finally, the constructed tuple is accepted by
Proposition~\ref{prop:finite-certificate-schema-verifier}; equivalently,
Theorem~\ref{thm:record-acceptance-exactness} identifies it with
$\mathsf{Acc}_{\Gamma}$.  The stronger non-escalation and maximality statement is
Proposition~\ref{prop:publication-safety-maximality}; the finite extension-budget
normal form in Theorem~\ref{thm:finite-extension-budget-normal-form} identifies
exactly which additional finite coordinates a stronger reading would have to add.
The current theorem is therefore the finite certificate-construction component of
the bounded software-audit interface, with a separately proved budget boundary
for extensions.  Since every step above factors through the displayed finite
schema and the external predicate $\mathsf{RGS}_{S}(P,L,\Gamma)$, the proof never
establishes the soundness of gate implementations, Lean/BEDC rebuilding,
artifact evaluators, daemons, uploads, archives, or venue decisions; those are
precisely the stronger readings handled only by the extension-budget theorems.
No current-package conclusion is used in this proof; the package instantiation
appears only later, after its support-surface hypotheses have been stated.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Finite-record certificate rigidity]
\label{thm:finite-record-certificate-rigidity}
Fix the finite submitted record surface $S$, acceptance ledger $L$, dispatch table
$\Gamma$, and schema verifier $V$ used in
Theorem~\ref{thm:publication-safety-interface}.  Assume the same record-gate
soundness and source-interface hypotheses as that theorem.  For every accepted
load-bearing claim $c$ with certificate
\(\mathcal C(c)=(K,\ell,E,H,R,D,B)\), the following are equivalent for a
properly stronger reading $c^{+}$ in the preorder of
Definition~\ref{def:stronger-reading-preorder}.
\begin{enumerate}
\item The same interface accepts $c^{+}$ over $S$.
\item The submitted surface contains exactly the finite extension coordinates
required by the normal form of
Theorem~\ref{thm:finite-extension-budget-normal-form} for the changed axis,
evidence level, discovery component, artifact-semantic component,
implementation-soundness component, theorem-completeness component, command-run
component, support-surface component, or venue component, and those added
coordinates pass the gates assigned by $\Gamma$.
\end{enumerate}
If these coordinates are absent, $c$ remains accepted but $c^{+}$ is rejected by
an inclusion-minimal bad subrecord of one of the types classified in
Theorem~\ref{thm:finite-certificate-obstruction-skeleton}.  Consequently the
publication-safety theorem is a finite-record certificate invariant under
record-gate soundness: it is invariant under changes to unsubmitted
implementations, Lean states, daemons, artifact reruns, venue states, archive
locators, and source checkouts that leave the finite tuple
\((K,\ell,E,H,R,D,B),S,L,\Gamma)\) unchanged, and it certifies no stronger fact
than that tuple records.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
The proof is a finite-record case analysis.  By
Theorem~\ref{thm:publication-safety-interface}, the accepted information about
$c$ is exhausted by the tuple \(\mathcal C(c)\) together with the submitted
surface, ledger, dispatch table, and schema verifier.  The verifier reads only
those finite coordinates by Proposition~\ref{prop:finite-certificate-schema-verifier}
and Theorem~\ref{thm:verifier-input-output-contract}; it does not inspect a live
implementation, a proof-assistant state, a daemon trace, an artifact rerun, a
venue web form, an unsubmitted archive, or an unsubmitted source checkout.

Suppose first that the finite extension coordinates required for $c^{+}$ are
present and pass the gates assigned by $\Gamma$.  Form the enlarged certificate
by adjoining those coordinates to the appropriate fields of
\(\mathcal C(c)\): new claim kinds are added to $K$, new submitted paths or logs
to $E$, new promotion or venue records to $H$, new pass rows to $R$, new pinned
source-interface rows to $D$, and new command-run or limitation-boundary data to
$B$.  The six clauses of
Definition~\ref{def:certificate-schema-acceptance-table} are then satisfied for
the enlarged tuple, because each clause reads exactly one of these finite fields
and the corresponding added coordinate has been supplied.  Therefore
Theorem~\ref{thm:record-acceptance-exactness} identifies the enlarged tuple with
the schema-relative accepted record semantics, and the interface accepts
$c^{+}$.

Conversely suppose the same interface accepts $c^{+}$.  Since $c^{+}$ is a
properly stronger reading, Definition~\ref{def:stronger-reading-preorder} says
that at least one finite coordinate has been strengthened: an architecture axis
has changed, the evidence level has increased, a discovery or theorem-content
claim has been added, an implementation- or artifact-semantic claim has been
added, a command-run reading has been asserted, a support-surface locator has
been required, or a venue conclusion has been asserted.  The exact budget normal
form of Theorem~\ref{thm:finite-extension-budget-normal-form} lists the finite
coordinate required for each such strengthening.  Acceptance by the schema means
that the relevant clause of
Definition~\ref{def:certificate-schema-acceptance-table} did not fail; hence the
required coordinate is present and its assigned gate record passes.  Thus the
normal-form extension coordinates are necessary.

If a required extension coordinate is absent, the failed schema clause supplies a
finite rejected subrecord: a missing submitted evidence surface, missing assigned
gate row, missing promotion or venue boundary, missing discovery-interface row,
or missing command-run boundary tuple.  Theorem~\ref{thm:finite-certificate-obstruction-skeleton}
classifies this rejected subrecord and gives inclusion-minimality after removing
irrelevant prose and fields not read by the failed clause.  Therefore the
original certificate for $c$ is rigid below the stronger reading: it remains
accepted exactly at its declared finite-record level, while $c^{+}$ is rejected.
The final invariance statement follows because two surrounding states that agree
on \((K,\ell,E,H,R,D,B),S,L,\Gamma)\) give the same verifier input and hence the
same accepted and rejected finite records.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Boundary-axis exact non-escalation]
\label{thm:boundary-axis-non-escalation-exactness}
Assume the hypotheses of
Theorem~\ref{thm:publication-safety-interface}, and let
\(c\) be any load-bearing claim accepted by the finite certificate constructed
there.  The following five stronger readings of \(c\), when they are not already
record-equivalent to \(c\), are accepted by the same publication-safety interface
if and only if the submitted finite support surface contains the indicated
positive extension coordinates and the added coordinates pass the gates assigned
by \(\Gamma\):
\begin{enumerate}
\item An \emph{external implementation-soundness reading} is accepted exactly when
the package adds implementation-soundness records for the relevant external gate:
the implemented gate or checker identity, its source state or version, the
specification it is asserted to implement, and either a pass row for the
implementation-soundness gate or a command-run tuple with command, source state,
environment, exit code, and log path.
\item An \emph{exact source-theorem import reading} is accepted exactly when the
package adds an exact source-statement or formal-rebuild coordinate: the pinned
source path and commit, the exact statement text or formal object being imported,
and a checked exact-statement appendix, Lean/BEDC rebuild record, or equivalent
command-run record whose pass rows are part of the submitted surface.  In
particular, the manual line-range extraction of
Lemma~\ref{lem:source-interface-extraction-correctness}, even when strengthened
by the executable table/JSON/snapshot comparison of
Proposition~\ref{prop:source-interface-record-file-consistency}, pays only for
bounded interface use and cannot by itself pay this exact-import budget.
\item A \emph{semantic source-validation reading of the source-interface record
comparison} is accepted exactly when, in addition to table/JSON/snapshot
consistency, the package adds the semantic source-validation coordinate from the
previous item for the source theorem whose truth is being used.
\item An \emph{artifact-semantic reading of a finite-witness row} is accepted
exactly when the package adds the evaluator contract or artifact-semantic pass row
for the asserted Rule110/GroundCompiler property, or a fresh evaluator command-run
tuple tied by manifest row to the submitted artifact.
\item A \emph{syntactic theorem-environment coverage reading of a
theorem-obligation ledger} is accepted exactly when the package adds a
deterministic theorem extractor coordinate: extractor identity and source state,
the source files it scans, the extraction command, environment, exit code, log
path, and the equality or inclusion certificate relating the extractor output to
the submitted theorem inventory.  This coordinate pays only for coverage of
labelled theorem-like environments in the declared syntactic domain; it does not
pay for proof correctness, theorem adequacy, Lean/BEDC rebuild, or semantic
completeness.
\end{enumerate}
If the corresponding positive coordinates are absent, the original claim \(c\)
may still be accepted at its declared finite-record level, but the stronger
reading is rejected by an inclusion-minimal finite bad subrecord.  Hence the main
safety interface, the manual source-interface extraction lemma, the executable
source-interface comparison, the finite-witness manifest theorem, and the
submitted theorem-obligation ledger theorem are jointly rigid: none can be used to
derive implementation soundness, exact source-theorem truth, semantic source
validation, dynamic artifact semantics, proof correctness, theorem adequacy, or
semantic theorem-inventory completeness without paying the matching finite
extension budget.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
Fix the accepted certificate
\(\mathcal C(c)=(K,\ell,E,H,R,D,B)\).  Each of the five displayed readings is a
properly stronger reading in the sense of
Definition~\ref{def:stronger-reading-preorder} unless its asserted extra content
is already one of the finite fields of \(\mathcal C(c)\).  Indeed, an
implementation-soundness reading adds either a new claim-kind component or a new
gate-pass family and command boundary; an exact source import adds source text,
formal-build, or discovery-source coordinates; semantic source validation adds
the semantic coordinate not read by the finite table/JSON/snapshot comparison;
artifact semantics adds an artifact-semantic surface, evaluator pass row, or
command-run boundary; and syntactic theorem-environment coverage adds an
extractor surface and command-boundary record.  A further proof-correctness,
theorem-adequacy, or semantic-completeness reading would add still another
formal-source or semantic checker coordinate not included in the syntactic
extractor record.  Thus every non-equivalent case changes at least one finite
coordinate read by the schema.

Theorem~\ref{thm:finite-extension-budget-normal-form} gives the exact normal form
for accepting any such changed reading: collect the additional kind components in
\(\Delta K\), the submitted surfaces in \(\Delta E\), the added pass rows in
\(\Delta R\), any new external or route boundary in \(\Delta H\), any exact or
semantic discovery-source rows in \(\Delta D\), and any command-run or limitation
boundary in \(\Delta B\).  In the exact-source case, the manual extraction
certificate supplies only the finite row fields listed in
Lemma~\ref{lem:source-interface-extraction-correctness}; the exact source theorem
text, a formal rebuild record, an axiom-audit pass row, or a semantic validation
coordinate is therefore a nonempty component of the required extension record,
not a consequence of the bounded extraction row.  Specialising that normal form
to the five cases gives precisely the five positive coordinate lists in the
statement.  If those
coordinates are present and their assigned gate rows pass, the enlarged
certificate
\[
(K\cup\Delta K,\ell,E\cup\Delta E,H\cup\Delta H,
R\cup\Delta R,D\cup\Delta D,B\cup\Delta B)
\]
satisfies the six clauses of
Definition~\ref{def:certificate-schema-acceptance-table}.  By
Theorem~\ref{thm:record-acceptance-exactness}, the publication-safety interface
therefore accepts the stronger reading.

Conversely, suppose one of the five stronger readings is accepted.  Since it is
not record-equivalent to \(c\),
Theorem~\ref{thm:finite-coordinate-determination-boundary} says that some finite
coordinate read by the verifier differs from the coordinate of
\(\mathcal C(c)\).  Acceptance forces the changed coordinate to satisfy its
corresponding schema clause, so the changed coordinate must be present in the
submitted support surface and must have the assigned pass rows or command-boundary
tuple required by \(\Gamma\).  Reading the changed coordinate by cases recovers
exactly the implementation-soundness, exact-source, semantic-source,
artifact-semantic, or syntactic theorem-extractor coordinate listed above.  Thus
the listed coordinates are necessary as well as sufficient for those bounded
readings.  For the source-interface item this necessity is sharp: table/JSON
equality, line-range containment, and snapshot digest agreement make the bounded
interface reproducible, but they do not supply the exact source theorem text, a
fresh formal build, an axiom-purity pass record, or semantic source validation.
They are therefore insufficient for a separate semantic proof-checking reading
unless that separate reading supplies its own finite coordinate.

If a listed coordinate is absent, the first schema clause that tries to read it
fails.  The failure is one of the obstruction skeletons of
Theorem~\ref{thm:finite-certificate-obstruction-skeleton}: a missing submitted
evidence surface, a missing assigned gate row, a missing discovery-source row, or
a missing command-run boundary tuple.  That theorem also gives
inclusion-minimality after deleting fields not read by the failed clause.  The
original certificate for \(c\) is unaffected, because its accepted finite fields
are unchanged; only the unpaid stronger reading is rejected.  Applying this case
analysis to Theorem~\ref{thm:publication-safety-interface},
Lemma~\ref{lem:source-interface-extraction-correctness},
Proposition~\ref{prop:source-interface-record-file-consistency},
Theorem~\ref{thm:finite-witness-manifest-record-interface}, and
Theorem~\ref{thm:theorem-obligation-ledger-boundary} gives the stated joint
rigidity of the five boundary axes.  The same argument also shows that the
theorem-extractor coordinate is only a syntactic coverage coordinate: it cannot
be reinterpreted as a proof checker because the proof-checking fields are not
read by, or present in, the extractor tuple.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Mixed-interface overclaim skeleton]
\label{thm:mixed-interface-overclaim-skeleton}
Assume the hypotheses of
Theorem~\ref{thm:publication-safety-interface}.  Let $c$ be an accepted
load-bearing claim whose proof uses one or more of the following interface
families: record-gate passage, bounded source-interface import, current-package
ledger rows, manifest-bounded finite-witness rows, or theorem-obligation
inventory rows.  Let $c^{+}$ be a proposed reading obtained from $c$ by adding
any finite nonempty set $J$ of stronger demands from the following five axes:
\begin{enumerate}
\item \emph{gate implementation soundness}: soundness of an actual automath or
newmath gate implementation beyond the external predicate
$\mathsf{RGS}_{S}(P,L,\Gamma)$;
\item \emph{exact or semantic source import}: exact source theorem statements,
fresh BEDC/Lean rebuilds, axiom-purity, or semantic truth of the imported source
theorems beyond the bounded source-interface rows;
\item \emph{row-frozen package completeness}: extractor completeness for every
possible prose claim in the supplement, or any extension beyond the explicitly
enumerated current-package ledger rows, without adding corresponding rows and
rerunning the finite checks;
\item \emph{artifact semantics}: dynamic Rule110/GroundCompiler validity,
evaluator semantics, or fresh artifact-suite outcomes beyond the submitted
manifest, snapshot, status, and limitation rows; and
\item \emph{semantic theorem-inventory completeness}: proof correctness,
theorem adequacy, semantic proof checking, Lean/BEDC rebuild, or theorem-like
material outside the declared syntactic extractor domain $\mathcal E$.
\end{enumerate}
For each axis $a\in J$, let $B_{a}$ be the finite extension budget required for
that stronger demand by Theorems~\ref{thm:finite-extension-budget-normal-form}
and~\ref{thm:boundary-axis-non-escalation-exactness}, with the row-frozen package
axis using the same normal form applied to the current-package rows of
Theorem~\ref{thm:current-package-certificate-exactness} and the ledger boundary
of Theorem~\ref{thm:manual-ledger-dependence-boundary}.  Then $c^{+}$ is accepted
by the same finite-record publication interface if and only if every coordinate
in the union $\bigcup_{a\in J}B_a$ is present in the submitted support surface and
passes its assigned gate.  If the unpaid set
\[
M=\{a\in J: B_a\text{ is not fully present with passing assigned gates}\}
\]
is nonempty, then $c^{+}$ has an inclusion-minimal finite bad subrecord whose
projection lies in one of the unpaid axes in $M$, and adding records only on axes
outside $M$ cannot make $c^{+}$ accepted.  Consequently no support coordinate in
one of the five families can be used as a substitute for a missing coordinate in
another family: the record-gate, source-interface, row-frozen ledger,
manifest-witness, and theorem-inventory boundaries are jointly confined by a
finite extension-budget lower bound.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
The proof is the bad-record skeleton argument applied simultaneously to the five
listed axes.  Start with the accepted finite certificate
$\mathcal C(c)=(K,\ell,E,H,R,D,B)$ supplied by
Theorem~\ref{thm:publication-safety-interface}.  Since $c^{+}$ is obtained by
adding stronger demands, each added demand is a stronger reading in the preorder
of Definition~\ref{def:stronger-reading-preorder} unless its content is already
record-equivalent to $c$.  Record-equivalent additions have empty budget and may
be deleted from $J$ without changing the claim.  Thus every remaining axis in
$J$ is a genuine strengthening.

For each genuine strengthening, Theorem~\ref{thm:finite-extension-budget-normal-form}
identifies the necessary and sufficient finite extension tuple
$(\Delta K,\Delta E,\Delta H,\Delta R,\Delta D,\Delta B)$.  Specialising that
normal form gives the five budgets in the statement.  Gate implementation
soundness requires implementation identity, specification, source-state or
version, and a matching implementation-soundness pass row or command-run tuple.
Exact or semantic source import requires exact source text, a formal-rebuild or
equivalent command-run coordinate, or a semantic source-validation coordinate.
Row-frozen package completeness requires new explicit ledger rows and the finite
verifier records that read those rows, because
Theorems~\ref{thm:current-package-certificate-exactness} and
\ref{thm:manual-ledger-dependence-boundary} identify only the enumerated ledger
surface.  Artifact semantics requires an evaluator contract, artifact-semantic
pass row, or fresh evaluator command-run tuple tied to the manifest row.  Semantic
theorem-inventory completeness requires a proof-checking, theorem-adequacy,
formal-rebuild, or out-of-domain extractor coordinate; the syntactic extractor of
Theorem~\ref{thm:deterministic-theorem-environment-extractor-boundary} pays only
for label coverage inside $\mathcal E$.

If every coordinate in $\bigcup_{a\in J}B_a$ is present and each assigned gate
passes, enlarge $\mathcal C(c)$ by adjoining exactly those finite coordinates.
Each clause of Definition~\ref{def:certificate-schema-acceptance-table} then reads
a present coordinate: claim kinds and evidence levels are in the enlarged $K$ and
$\ell$, evidence surfaces in $E$, assigned gate rows in $R$, route or external
boundaries in $H$, discovery/source rows in $D$, and command or limitation data in
$B$.  Theorem~\ref{thm:record-acceptance-exactness} therefore accepts the enlarged
certificate, so $c^{+}$ is accepted at precisely the paid evidence level.

Conversely suppose that some axis in $J$ is unpaid.  By the same normal-form
theorem, acceptance of $c^{+}$ would force the missing coordinate of $B_a$ to be
present with its assigned pass row.  Since it is absent or nonpassing, the first
schema clause that reads it fails.  Theorem~\ref{thm:finite-certificate-obstruction-skeleton}
then produces an inclusion-minimal bad subrecord: a missing evidence surface,
missing assigned-gate row, missing promotion or external boundary, missing
source/discovery row, or unsupported command-run escalation.  That subrecord is
contained in the finite budget of an unpaid axis, so its projection lies in $M$.

Finally, adding records only on axes outside $M$ leaves at least one unpaid
budget $B_a$ with $a\in M$ unchanged.  The same failed schema clause and the same
minimal bad-subrecord classification remain.  Hence cross-axis substitution is
impossible: a source snapshot cannot prove gate implementation soundness, a
manifest row cannot prove artifact semantics without evaluator coordinates, a
theorem-inventory label check cannot prove proof correctness, and a row-frozen
package ledger cannot become semantically complete without fresh rows and checks.
This proves the stated joint confinement and lower bound.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Package-local boundary normal form for the presentation package]
\label{thm:presentation-scoped-boundary-normal-form}
Assume the submitted package is evaluated only through the finite-record
interface of Theorem~\ref{thm:publication-safety-interface}.  Then every accepted
load-bearing claim using one of the five interface components below has the stated
maximal bounded reading, and every strictly stronger reading has the indicated
unpaid obstruction unless the submitted surface is enlarged by a fresh finite
coordinate and its assigned pass record.
\begin{enumerate}
\item \emph{Publication safety}: the accepted reading is conditional finite-record
safety under the external record-gate-soundness predicate
\(\mathsf{RGS}_{S}(P,L,\Gamma)\).  An implementation-soundness reading of the
record gates is obstructed by a missing implementation-specification,
source-state, and implementation-soundness pass or command-run coordinate.
\item \emph{Imported source interface}: the accepted reading of
Lemma~\ref{lem:source-interface-extraction-correctness} is the path-verified
bounded source-interface record consisting of path, commit, label, line range,
extraction date, bounded summary, and mirror file.  A fresh Lean/BEDC rebuild,
exact source-theorem import, axiom-purity statement, or semantic source-theorem
truth reading is obstructed by a missing formal-source or command-run coordinate.
\item \emph{Finite witnesses}: the accepted reading of
Theorem~\ref{thm:finite-witness-manifest-record-interface} is manifest-bounded:
manifest rows, evaluator-facing outcomes explicitly recorded in those rows,
reviewable snapshots, status pass records, and limitation-ledger records.  Dynamic
Rule110/GroundCompiler semantic validation is obstructed by a missing evaluator
contract, artifact-semantic pass row, or fresh evaluator command-run coordinate.
\item \emph{Primary-claim inventory}: the accepted reading of
Proposition~\ref{prop:primary-claim-row-classification} and
Proposition~\ref{prop:primary-inventory-freshness-criterion} is manual
claim-audit completeness plus row-span and certificate-field freshness for the
specific primary source being audited.  A semantic extractor for arbitrary later
prose edits is obstructed by a missing fresh manual audit of the edited primary
source and a missing fresh verifier record for the edited inventory fields.
\item \emph{Theorem inventory}: the accepted reading of
Theorem~\ref{thm:deterministic-theorem-environment-extractor-boundary} and
Theorem~\ref{prop:manual-theorem-inventory-boundary} is syntactic
environment-label coverage for the declared theorem-like environment domain
\(\mathcal E\).  Proof correctness, theorem adequacy, semantic completeness, Lean
or BEDC rebuild, and artifact validation are obstructed by missing formal-source,
proof-checking, semantic-checking, or artifact-validation coordinates.
\end{enumerate}
Consequently the current package has a sharp finite-record skeleton for the
presentation-only route: the accepted claims are exactly these bounded readings
together with the certificates cited by their statements, and the five stronger
families above cannot be inferred from the manuscript by prose escalation.  This
normal form is package-local: it applies only to packages using the same finite
schema, submitted support-surface convention, dispatch table, and five-component
boundary design, and it asserts no classification theorem for AI-assisted
mathematics pipelines with different schemas or evidence surfaces.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
Let \(c\) be an accepted load-bearing claim using one of the five listed interface
components, and let \(\mathcal C(c)=(K,\ell,E,H,R,D,B)\) be its accepted
certificate from Theorem~\ref{thm:publication-safety-interface}.  By
Theorem~\ref{thm:finite-record-certificate-rigidity}, the value of any accepted
consequence is determined by the finite submitted coordinates read by
\(\mathcal C(c)\), the submitted surface \(S\), the ledger \(L\), and the dispatch
table \(\Gamma\).  Hence two surrounding states that agree on those coordinates
but differ in a live gate implementation, source checkout, evaluator run,
primary-prose edit, proof-checker state, or artifact semantics must give the same
accepted certificate and the same rejected bad subrecords.  Any claimed
difference depending on one of those unsubmitted states is therefore not a
consequence of the accepted finite record.

For item~1, the statement of Theorem~\ref{thm:publication-safety-interface}
explicitly assumes \(\mathsf{RGS}_{S}(P,L,\Gamma)\) and states that no independent
implementation-soundness, fresh-execution, or semantic-correctness claim about the
gates is inferred without source path, command, source state, environment, exit
code, and log path.  Thus an implementation-soundness upgrade changes the
implementation or command-boundary fields and is governed by
Theorem~\ref{thm:boundary-axis-non-escalation-exactness}.

For item~2, Lemma~\ref{lem:source-interface-extraction-correctness} identifies the
complete imported interface as the manually audited path, commit, line range,
bounded summary, extraction date, and mirror file, and
Proposition~\ref{prop:manual-source-interface-non-escalation} proves that exact
source-statement import, rebuild, axiom-purity, and semantic validation require
additional source-statement, formal-build, or command-run coordinates.  Therefore
the path-verified bounded reading is maximal below those upgrades.

For item~3, Theorem~\ref{thm:finite-witness-manifest-record-interface} lists the
coordinate set \(\mathcal M_{a_6}\) read for finite-witness claims, and
Corollary~\ref{cor:artifact-semantic-validity-boundary} proves that unbounded
Rule110/GroundCompiler semantics require an evaluator contract, artifact pass
record, or evaluator command-run tuple.  Agreement on \(\mathcal M_{a_6}\) is
therefore sufficient for the accepted manifest-bounded reading and insufficient
for dynamic semantic validation.

For item~4, Proposition~\ref{prop:primary-claim-row-classification} is proved by a
finite manual inspection of the current primary-claim rows, while
Proposition~\ref{prop:primary-inventory-freshness-criterion} states the three
conditions needed for an edited source: a fresh manual audit list, live pointers
to submitted certificate surfaces, and evidence levels bounded by those
certificates.  Its command-run paragraph is limited to row-span and
certificate-field freshness.  Thus a later arbitrary prose edit that lacks a new
manual audit or a refreshed verifier record differs in an unsubmitted coordinate
and is rejected as an unclassified-row, stale-pointer, or evidence-level
obstruction.

For item~5, Theorem~\ref{thm:deterministic-theorem-environment-extractor-boundary}
states that the extractor scans only the five syntactic environment names in
\(\mathcal E\), compares source labels with inventory labels, and does not parse
mathematical statements, check proofs, invoke Lean, consult the daemon, or
validate artifacts.  Theorem~\ref{prop:manual-theorem-inventory-boundary} therefore
accepts only obligation-ledger and syntactic label-coverage claims.  Any proof
correctness, adequacy, semantic-completeness, rebuild, or artifact-validation
upgrade changes a finite coordinate outside the extractor tuple and is rejected
until the corresponding formal-source, semantic-checking, or artifact-validation
record is supplied.

The five cases exhaust the scoped weak points named in this presentation package
under the stated finite schema and support-surface convention.
In each case, Theorem~\ref{thm:finite-extension-budget-normal-form} supplies the
positive extension budget for a genuine upgrade, and
Theorem~\ref{thm:finite-certificate-obstruction-skeleton} supplies the
inclusion-minimal bad subrecord when that budget is absent.  This proves both the
maximal bounded readings and the non-escalation conclusion.
\end{proof}
\begin{theorem}[Package-local five-axis upgrade obstruction]
\label{thm:five-axis-upgrade-obstruction}
Assume the hypotheses of
Theorem~\ref{thm:publication-safety-interface}.  Let $c$ be an accepted
load-bearing claim in this presentation package, evaluated with this paper's
finite schema, submitted support surface, dispatch table $\Gamma$, and boundary
axis map, whose proof uses at least one of the following bounded interfaces:
\[
\text{record-gate},\quad \text{source-interface},\quad
\text{finite-witness},\quad \text{primary-inventory},\quad
\text{theorem-inventory}.
\]
For each such interface there is a finite upgrade coordinate
\(U_j(c)\subseteq \Delta(c,c^{+})\) with the following exact form.
\begin{enumerate}
\item For a record-gate implementation-soundness reading,
\(U_1(c)\) consists of an implementation object or source state, the gate
specification being checked, and an implementation-soundness pass row or the full
command-run tuple in the boundary field.
\item For an exact or semantic source-theory reading of an imported
source-interface row, \(U_2(c)\) consists of either a submitted extractor/rebuild
artifact for the cited Lean/BEDC source state or exact source-theorem import rows,
together with the assigned pass record.
\item For dynamic artifact or Rule110/GroundCompiler semantic validation,
\(U_3(c)\) consists of an artifact-semantic evaluator contract, the evaluator
input/output surface, and a status pass row or fresh evaluator command-run tuple.
\item For semantic completeness of the primary-claim inventory beyond the twelve
manual rows, \(U_4(c)\) consists of a fresh audit of the edited primary source,
row-span and certificate-field comparison data for that audit, and its verifier
pass record.
\item For proof-dependency assurance or theorem semantic completeness beyond the
declared environment domain $\mathcal E$, \(U_5(c)\) consists of a dependency graph
or semantic proof-review/checker surface, the source-to-output binding for the
covered theorem-like material, and its pass record.
\end{enumerate}
Let $J(c,c^{+})$ be the set of axes on which a proposed stronger reading
$c^{+}$ strengthens $c$.  Then $c^{+}$ is accepted by the same finite-record
safety interface if and only if, for every $j\in J(c,c^{+})$, the submitted
surface contains $U_j(c)$ and the rows in $U_j(c)$ pass the gates assigned by
$\Gamma$.  If any $U_j(c)$ is absent, $c^{+}$ has an inclusion-minimal bad
subrecord on that axis and no combination of the other four bounded interfaces
can discharge the missing coordinate.  Thus the five weak presentation-package
points are not merely warnings inside this schema: each is a separate
finite-budget lower bound for upgrading the main safety theorem beyond its
conditional, path-verified, manifest-bounded, manual-inventory, or
syntactic-inventory reading.  No conclusion is asserted for a system that lacks
these five interfaces or replaces the finite support-surface design by a
different audit schema.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
Let \(\mathcal C(c)=(K,\ell,E,H,R,D,B)\) be the certificate accepted for $c$ by
Theorem~\ref{thm:publication-safety-interface}.  The six schema clauses of
Definition~\ref{def:certificate-schema-acceptance-table} read only the finite
fields \(K,\ell,E,H,R,D,B\) and the pass rows assigned by $\Gamma$.  Therefore a
proper stronger reading must differ from $c$ in at least one finite coordinate of
the extension normal form of
Theorem~\ref{thm:finite-extension-budget-normal-form}; otherwise
Theorem~\ref{thm:finite-coordinate-determination-boundary} makes it
record-equivalent to $c$.

The five displayed coordinates are obtained by specializing that normal form to
the five scoped interfaces of this package.  A record-gate implementation claim changes the
implementation or command-boundary fields and hence lies in \(\Delta E\),
\(\Delta R\), and \(\Delta B\).  An exact source import, source-theory semantic
claim, or Lean/BEDC rebuild changes the discovery/source-interface surface and
its pass records, hence lies in \(\Delta E\), \(\Delta R\), and \(\Delta D\).  An
artifact-semantic validation changes the artifact evidence surface, the assigned
status/evaluator rows, or the command-boundary field, hence lies in
\(\Delta E\), \(\Delta R\), and \(\Delta B\).  A semantic completeness claim for
the primary inventory changes the manual audit row set and its comparison
records, hence lies in \(\Delta E\), \(\Delta R\), and, when the changed row is a
discovery or source-interface row, \(\Delta D\).  A proof-dependency or semantic
theorem-inventory claim changes the theorem-checking surface and its pass
record, and may also change the command-boundary field; it is not contained in
the syntactic label tuple scanned over $\mathcal E$.  These are exactly the
coordinates listed as \(U_1(c),\ldots,U_5(c)\).

If every coordinate \(U_j(c)\) for the strengthened axes is present and passes
the gates assigned by $\Gamma$, adjoining their finite rows to
\(\mathcal C(c)\) gives the enlarged certificate of
Theorem~\ref{thm:finite-extension-budget-normal-form}.  The six schema clauses
then hold for $c^{+}$, so
Theorem~\ref{thm:record-acceptance-exactness} accepts the stronger reading.
Conversely, if $c^{+}$ is accepted, each changed coordinate must be present and
must satisfy the corresponding schema clause; reading the changed coordinates by
the five cases recovers the required \(U_j(c)\) for every strengthened axis.

Suppose finally that one required \(U_j(c)\) is absent.  The first schema clause
that reads a field of that coordinate fails, and
Theorem~\ref{thm:finite-certificate-obstruction-skeleton} extracts an
inclusion-minimal bad subrecord.  The other bounded interfaces cannot repair this
failure because Theorem~\ref{thm:mixed-interface-overclaim-skeleton} proves
cross-axis substitution impossible: each axis has its own evidence surface, pass
row, discovery/source field, or command-boundary field.  Hence the missing
upgrade coordinate is a genuine finite-budget lower bound, not a prose boundary
that can be discharged by relabelling another record.\end{proof}

Together, Theorem~\ref{thm:presentation-scoped-boundary-normal-form} and
Theorem~\ref{thm:five-axis-upgrade-obstruction} are the supplement's bounded
replacement for a stronger article-level claim.  They keep the headline safety
theorem conditional on record-gate soundness, identify the manual/path-verified
status of the imported source interface, confine finite-witness rows to bounded
artifact evidence, confine the primary inventory to the twelve audited rows, and
confine the theorem inventory to syntactic labelled-environment coverage.  Any
future paper that wants unconditional gate implementation soundness, complete
source-theory extraction, dynamic artifact validation, semantic primary-claim
extraction, or proof-dependency assurance must add precisely the corresponding
finite upgrade coordinate above.

\begin{theorem}[Current package-local boundary skeleton and non-gluing lower bound]
\label{thm:current-boundary-skeleton-non-gluing}
Assume the support-surface hypotheses used by the current-package instantiation,
including the six-row state $P^{+}_{6}$ of
Theorem~\ref{thm:current-package-certificate-exactness}, the finite-script
projection records of
Theorem~\ref{thm:concrete-finite-script-implementation-soundness}, the
manifest-bounded artifact coordinate $\mathcal M_{a_6}$ of
Theorem~\ref{thm:finite-witness-manifest-record-interface}, and the syntactic
theorem-environment domain $\mathcal E$ of
Theorem~\ref{thm:deterministic-theorem-environment-extractor-boundary}.  Let
$F_{\mathrm{cur}}$ be the finite union of those submitted coordinates together
with the finite record-gate surface used by
Theorem~\ref{thm:publication-safety-interface}.  Then the maximal information
about the current package determined by $F_{\mathrm{cur}}$ is exactly the
following five-coordinate skeleton.  The word ``current'' is load-bearing: the
claim is only about this presentation package with this finite schema and this
support-surface design, and it does not classify packages with different gate
interfaces, artifact semantics, theorem inventories, or venue workflows.
\begin{enumerate}
\item The publication-safety coordinate is conditional finite-record
non-escalation under the explicit predicate
$\mathsf{RGS}_{S}(P,L,\Gamma)$; it is not independent gate implementation
soundness, fresh daemon execution, proof-assistant correctness, artifact
semantics, or venue acceptance.
\item The finite-script coordinate is projection soundness for the printed
finite predicates on the parsed finite input records of the named scripts; it is
not end-to-end correctness of the Python implementation, filesystem model,
runtime, verifier source, or external gate semantics.
\item The current-package certificate coordinate is the row-frozen six-row map
for $a_1,\ldots,a_6$ and the path-verified instantiation that cites those rows.
Theorem-environment scope coverage is supplied, when cited, only by the separate
Stage-A theorem-inventory/extractor coordinate, not by completeness of the
six-row certificate map for every theorem-like environment.
\item The finite-witness coordinate is the manifest-bounded row $a_6$ interface
$\mathcal M_{a_6}$; it is not unbounded Rule110/GroundCompiler semantic
validity or fresh dynamic evaluator validation.
\item The theorem-inventory coordinate is syntactic label coverage for labelled
environments in the declared domain $\mathcal E$; it is not semantic proof
completeness, proof-dependency assurance, Lean/BEDC rebuild, or artifact
validation.
\end{enumerate}
Moreover these five coordinates do not glue into any stronger conclusion: for
any proposed strengthened reading $c^{+}$ that changes one or more of the five
axes, acceptance of $c^{+}$ in the finite-record schema requires the corresponding
finite upgrade coordinates $U_j(c)$ of
Theorem~\ref{thm:five-axis-upgrade-obstruction}.  If one such coordinate is
absent, the obstruction is an inclusion-minimal bad subrecord on that axis, and
agreement on all other coordinates of $F_{\mathrm{cur}}$ cannot repair it.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
Each item is a specialization of a labelled theorem already in the finite-record
chain, but the point here is the joint skeleton.  Item~1 is exactly the
conditional hypothesis and boundary of
Theorem~\ref{thm:publication-safety-interface}: the theorem reads the submitted
record surface, ledger, dispatch table, and certificate schema, and its statement
excludes unrecorded implementation, execution, semantic, artifact, and venue
claims.  Item~2 follows from
Theorem~\ref{thm:concrete-finite-script-implementation-soundness}, whose proof
identifies the finite input projection for each named script and proves only the
corresponding printed predicate on that projection.  Item~3 follows from
Theorem~\ref{thm:current-package-certificate-exactness} and
Corollary~\ref{cor:current-package-path-verified-instantiation}: those results
read the selected six rows $a_1,\ldots,a_6$ and the path catalogue fixed by the
current support surface.  They do not read the semantic content of every
theorem-like environment; syntactic theorem-environment coverage is introduced
separately by
Theorem~\ref{thm:deterministic-theorem-environment-extractor-boundary} and
bounded by Theorem~\ref{prop:manual-theorem-inventory-boundary}.  Item~4 is the
manifest-record invariance statement proved in
Theorem~\ref{thm:finite-witness-manifest-record-interface} and sharpened in
Corollary~\ref{cor:artifact-semantic-validity-boundary}.  Item~5 is the exact
input/output boundary of
Theorem~\ref{thm:deterministic-theorem-environment-extractor-boundary} together
with the semantic separation in
Theorem~\ref{prop:manual-theorem-inventory-boundary}.

It remains to prove non-gluing.  Let two possible ambient states agree on
$F_{\mathrm{cur}}$ and differ only in one unsubmitted fact, such as live gate
implementation correctness, verifier-source correctness beyond the parsed finite
projection, a certificate row outside $a_1,\ldots,a_6$, dynamic artifact
semantics, or proof completeness beyond the syntactic extractor domain.  By
Theorem~\ref{thm:finite-coordinate-determination-boundary}, the finite verifier
and certificate constructor return the same accepted certificates and the same
bad-subrecord reports on these two states.  Hence any assertion whose truth
changes between them is not determined by the current finite record.  By
Theorem~\ref{thm:five-axis-upgrade-obstruction}, making that assertion
load-bearing requires the appropriate finite upgrade coordinate $U_j(c)$ and its
assigned pass row.  If it is absent,
Theorem~\ref{thm:finite-certificate-obstruction-skeleton} returns an
inclusion-minimal bad subrecord, and
Theorem~\ref{thm:mixed-interface-overclaim-skeleton} rules out substituting pass
records from another axis.  Therefore the five bounded current-package
coordinates remain separate lower bounds inside this package-local schema, and
their union does not certify any of the stronger readings listed in the
statement.\end{proof}

For reviewer reproducibility, the constructor and extractor used in
Theorem~\ref{thm:publication-safety-interface} can be written as the following
finite algorithms.  The constructor \(\mathsf{Cert}_{\Gamma}\) reads, in order,
the claim-kind coordinates from the acceptance entry, the declared evidence level,
the cited submitted surfaces, the pass rows assigned by \(\Gamma\), the route
promotion field, the pinned discovery rows when the kind set contains discovery,
and the command-boundary field.  The bad-record extractor \(\mathsf{Bad}_{\Gamma}\)
applies the six schema predicates in the order of
Theorem~\ref{thm:verifier-input-output-contract} and returns the fields read by
the first false predicate.  Thus positive certificates and negative certificates
are built from the same finite coordinates; neither construction consults live
daemon state, proof-assistant semantics, artifact semantics, or venue state.

\begin{proposition}[Certificate constructor and bad-subrecord extractor]
\label{prop:certificate-constructor-bad-subrecord-extractor}
Assume the four finite-record hypotheses of
Theorem~\ref{thm:publication-safety-interface}.  For every ledger row \(c\) there
is a deterministic partial constructor
\[
\mathsf{Cert}_{\Gamma}(c)=(K,\ell,E,H,R,D,B)
\]
and a deterministic extractor \(\mathsf{Bad}_{\Gamma}(c)\) with the following
properties.
\begin{enumerate}
\item \(\mathsf{Cert}_{\Gamma}(c)\) is defined and accepted if and only if the
six-clause predicate \(\mathsf{Acc}_{\Gamma}(c,\mathsf{Cert}_{\Gamma}(c))\) holds.
\item If \(\mathsf{Cert}_{\Gamma}(c)\) is rejected, then
\(\mathsf{Bad}_{\Gamma}(c)\) is an inclusion-minimal finite bad subrecord of one
of the five obstruction skeletons in
Theorem~\ref{thm:finite-certificate-obstruction-skeleton}: missing submitted
evidence, missing assigned gate record, missing route or external decision field,
missing pinned discovery row, or unsupported command-run boundary.
\item For every properly stronger reading \(c^{+}\) of an accepted row, the same
extractor rejects \(c^{+}\) exactly at the first unpaid positive extension
coordinate of Theorem~\ref{thm:finite-extension-budget-normal-form}; if no such
coordinate is added, \(c^{+}\) is not distinct from \(c\) in the finite-record
preorder.
\end{enumerate}
Consequently the publication-safety theorem has both a positive certificate
construction algorithm and a negative bad-subrecord extraction algorithm, each
bounded by the submitted schema fields.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
The constructor is obtained by the ``Package-checker construction'' paragraph of
Theorem~\ref{thm:publication-safety-interface}: from the acceptance entry it reads
\(K\), \(\ell\), and \(E\); from record-gate soundness it reads the gate-pass rows
\(R\) assigned by \(\Gamma\); from the promotion hypothesis it reads \(H\) or the
no-crossing marker; from discovery routing it reads \(D\) exactly for discovery
claims; and from the evidence-level definition it reads \(B\), either the
path-verified boundary or the five command-run fields.  These are finite fields
on the submitted surface, so the constructor is deterministic and partial only
when a required field is absent.  By
Theorem~\ref{thm:record-acceptance-exactness}, acceptance of the constructed tuple
is equivalent to \(\mathsf{Acc}_{\Gamma}\), proving the first item.

For rejection, run the predicates \(P_1,\ldots,P_6\) of
Theorem~\ref{thm:verifier-input-output-contract} in order and return the finite
fields read by the first false predicate.  If the failure is not merely an
out-of-domain kind or level, it is one of the five substantive coordinates
\(E,R,H,D,B\).  The exhaustion argument of
Theorem~\ref{thm:finite-certificate-obstruction-skeleton} proves that these are
exactly the five listed obstruction skeletons, and deleting unread coordinates
gives inclusion-minimality.  If the failure is an out-of-domain kind or level,
the same theorem reduces it to the corresponding assigned-gate or evidence-level
boundary obstruction, since \(K\) determines the finite gate set and \(\ell\)
determines the boundary obligation.

Finally let \(c^{+}\) be a proper stronger reading of an accepted row.  By
Theorem~\ref{thm:finite-extension-budget-normal-form}, such a reading changes at
least one finite coordinate unless it is record-equivalent to \(c\).  The first
changed coordinate not supplied on the submitted support surface is read by one
of the six predicates and is therefore returned by the extractor.  If all changed
coordinates are supplied and pass their assigned gates, the enlarged tuple is a
new accepted certificate for the enlarged claim; if none are changed, the finite
preorder has not distinguished \(c^{+}\) from \(c\).\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[Finite-record maximality]
\label{prop:publication-safety-maximality}
Under the hypotheses of Theorem~\ref{thm:publication-safety-interface}, the
constructed certificate is maximal with respect to the finite submitted record
surface, in the dependency order isolated in
Proposition~\ref{prop:publication-safety-dependency-normal-form}.  More
precisely, let $c$ be an accepted load-bearing claim and let
$\mathsf{Cert}_{\Gamma}(c)=(K,\ell,E,H,R,D,B)$ be the certificate constructed in
that theorem.  If a stronger reading $c^{+}$ asserts any external end-to-end
compiler correctness, current execution of unrecorded gates, proof-assistant
correctness, current formal-source currency, dynamic artifact semantics, or venue
acceptance, then either $c^{+}$ is not a distinct finite-record consequence of
$\mathsf{Cert}_{\Gamma}(c)$, or its certificate must strictly enlarge one of
$K,\ell,E,H,R,D,B$ by adding the corresponding claim kind, evidence surface,
gate pass record, promotion or venue field, discovery-interface row, or
command-run tuple.  In the latter case, if the enlargement is absent, a finite
bad subrecord is returned by Theorem~\ref{thm:record-acceptance-exactness} or by
one of the obstruction types of
Theorem~\ref{thm:finite-certificate-obstruction-skeleton}.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
The schema verifier used in Theorem~\ref{thm:publication-safety-interface} reads
only the tuple \((K,\ell,E,H,R,D,B)\), the dispatch table $\Gamma$, and the cited
submitted finite surfaces.  This is exactly the finite-coordinate determination
boundary of Theorem~\ref{thm:finite-coordinate-determination-boundary}.  Hence two
surrounding implementations, formal-source trees, artifact runs, or venue states
that agree on those coordinates obtain the same verifier decision.  A purported
stronger reading which changes none of the coordinates is therefore invisible to
the certificate and cannot become a new accepted finite-record conclusion.

If the stronger reading is made load-bearing, it changes a coordinate.  External
compiler, proof-assistant, formal-source-currency, or artifact-semantics claims
add claim kinds and assigned gate records; fresh executions raise the evidence
boundary to command-run data; route or venue assertions add promotion or venue
surfaces; discovery-strengthening adds source-interface rows.  These are exactly
the finite fields tested by the clauses of
Definition~\ref{def:certificate-schema-acceptance-table}.  When the required
field is missing, Theorem~\ref{thm:record-acceptance-exactness} supplies the
failed acceptance clause, and
Theorem~\ref{thm:finite-certificate-obstruction-skeleton} classifies the failure
as a missing evidence surface, gate record, promotion field, discovery-interface
row, or command-run escalation.\end{proof}

\begin{corollary}[Current-package bounded source-level audit instance]
\label{cor:publication-safety-current-package-instance}
For the current package, assume the support-surface condition later closed by
Theorem~\ref{thm:final-support-surface-closure}: the exact supplemental
\path{review_bundle/} directory is uploaded with the submission, or an
archive-equivalent replacement with matching final digest records is supplied.
Under that explicit availability condition, the finite pass-row components of
the hypotheses of Theorem~\ref{thm:publication-safety-interface} are witnessed
by the machine-readable evidence map of
Proposition~\ref{prop:machine-readable-certificate-records}, the acceptance
ledger of
Proposition~\ref{prop:current-package-acceptance-certificate-ledger}, the
assigned-gate boundary of
Theorem~\ref{thm:assigned-gate-soundness-audit-boundary}, and the four
path-complete case witnesses of
Proposition~\ref{prop:path-complete-case-witnesses}.  Therefore the current
package instantiates the construction theorem as a bounded software-audit
certificate-construction and certificate-checking result for the finite records
actually supplied, with the bounded schema-relative package certificate exactness
isolated in Theorem~\ref{thm:current-package-certificate-exactness}.  It is not,
without the separate extension coordinates listed in the boundary theorems, an
instance of gate implementation soundness, Lean/BEDC rebuild correctness,
artifact semantic validity, daemon behavior, upload, archive replacement,
upload-time venue compliance, or venue acceptance.
\end{corollary}

\begin{proof}
Under the stated support-surface condition, each cited current-package result
supplies one finite hypothesis of the abstract interface.  Proposition~\ref{prop:current-package-acceptance-certificate-ledger}
lists the accepted rows and their evidence surfaces.  The assigned-gate boundary
theorem gives the finite-record form of record-gate soundness and blocks an
unrecorded implementation-soundness reading.  The machine-readable certificate
records supply the schema mirror, pass-record rows, interface map, inventory,
verifier script, positive verifier log, negative fixture, and negative verifier
log when the exact review-bundle support surface is submitted with matching final
digests.  The positive log records the invocation
\path{python review_bundle/verify_certificate_records.py} and exit code
\path{0}; the negative log records the invocation
\path{python review_bundle/verify_certificate_records.py --negative-example review_bundle/negative_certificate_record_example.json}
and the expected finite rejection \path{neg1: command-run fields incomplete}.
The case-witness proposition supplies
the finite case-study paths used by the accepted lesson rows.  Applying
Theorem~\ref{thm:publication-safety-interface} to these records gives the stated
bounded audit construction; Theorem~\ref{thm:current-package-certificate-exactness}
is the row-frozen schema-relative exactness specialization.  The exclusions in
the statement follow because none of the cited current-package records contains
the extension coordinates required by
Theorem~\ref{thm:finite-extension-budget-normal-form}.\end{proof}

\begin{corollary}[Finite-record boundary for external gate soundness]
\label{cor:finite-record-gate-soundness-boundary}
Under the hypotheses of the finite-record interface theorem, Theorem~\ref{thm:publication-safety-interface}, the
publication-safety conclusion is invariant under replacement of the surrounding
implementation by any other implementation with the same submitted acceptance
entries, evidence surfaces, promotion fields, discovery-interface rows, and
evidence-level boundaries.  Therefore the theorem cannot be used, without an
additional submitted record-gate-soundness certificate, to infer external workflow
correctness, current execution of unrecorded gates, Lean correctness, artifact
semantic validity, or venue acceptance.  Conversely, any manuscript assertion
that tries to promote the finite certificate constructor to one of those stronger
implementation claims has a finite overclaim witness: either the required field
is absent from \(\mathcal C(c)\), or the row's declared evidence level exceeds
the cited certificate surface.
\end{corollary}

\begin{proof}
The constructor in the finite-record interface theorem, Theorem~\ref{thm:publication-safety-interface}, reads only the
finite tuple \(\mathcal C(c)=(K,\ell,E,H,R,D,B)\).  Here \(K\) is the claim-kind
set assigned by the dispatch table, \(\ell\) is the declared evidence level,
\(E\) is the submitted evidence surface, \(H\) is the human-promotion field,
\(R\) is the finite list of recorded pass rows, \(D\) is the pinned
discovery-interface field when the claim has a discovery component, and \(B\) is
the evidence boundary.  If another implementation supplies the same values for
these fields, the same finite schema verifier receives the same input record and
therefore gives the same certificate-construction conclusion by
Proposition~\ref{prop:finite-certificate-schema-verifier} and
the schema-relative finite-record exactness theorem, Theorem~\ref{thm:record-acceptance-exactness}.  No step of the proof inspected an
unsubmitted daemon trace, reran a proof assistant, reran a finite-witness
artifact, or queried a venue form after the recorded venue check; those facts are
not coordinates of \(\mathcal C(c)\).

Now suppose the manuscript asserted one of the stronger implementation claims
without adding a new accepted record.  External workflow correctness and current
execution of unrecorded gates require pass data not present in \(R\).  Lean
correctness requires a formal-source certificate rather than the bounded
contextual rows allowed by Proposition~\ref{prop:formal-source-gate-instantiation}.
Artifact semantic validity requires a stronger artifact row than the bounded
manifest records of Theorem~\ref{thm:finite-witness-manifest-record-interface}.
Venue acceptance requires a venue decision rather than the submission-pack
readiness row of Proposition~\ref{audit:venue-submission-pack-gate}.  In each
case, either the needed finite field is missing or the row is read at a stronger
evidence level than its certificate permits.  The obstruction is therefore a
finite subrecord of the kind classified by
Theorem~\ref{thm:finite-certificate-obstruction-skeleton} and
Theorem~\ref{thm:evidence-level-overclaim-obstruction}.\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Boundary extension criterion for implementation-soundness readings]
\label{thm:implementation-soundness-extension-criterion}
Fix the dispatch table $\Gamma$ and the finite certificate schema of
Proposition~\ref{prop:finite-certificate-schema-verifier}.  A reading of a
load-bearing package claim as an implementation-soundness claim is accepted by
the same publication-safety interface if and only if the submitted finite record
surface is enlarged by an implementation-soundness extension record
\[
\mathsf{Impl}(c)=(K^{\mathrm{impl}},E^{\mathrm{impl}},R^{\mathrm{impl}},B^{\mathrm{impl}})
\]
with the following properties.
\begin{enumerate}
\item $K^{\mathrm{impl}}$ contains the additional implementation claim kind or
