claim-kind component being asserted, rather than merely reusing the original
finite-record claim kind of $c$.
\item $E^{\mathrm{impl}}$ is a submitted finite evidence surface naming the
implementation object being certified: for example the relevant source path,
version or commit, configuration, executable verifier, and implementation-log
surface.
\item $R^{\mathrm{impl}}$ contains a pass record, with evidence pointer inside
$E^{\mathrm{impl}}$, for every gate assigned by $\Gamma$ to the enlarged claim
kind $K\cup K^{\mathrm{impl}}$.
\item $B^{\mathrm{impl}}$ declares the evidence level of the implementation
claim and contains the command-run tuple whenever the implementation claim is
asserted at command-run strength.
\end{enumerate}
When such an extension record is present, the finite certificate constructor of
Theorem~\ref{thm:publication-safety-interface} accepts the enlarged claim exactly
at the evidence level declared by $B^{\mathrm{impl}}$.  When it is absent, every
implementation-soundness reading of the same row has a finite obstruction of one
of the five kinds in Theorem~\ref{thm:finite-certificate-obstruction-skeleton}.
The current package supplies no record of the displayed form
$\mathsf{Impl}(c)$ for the package verifiers or gates.  Thus the theorem is a
future-facing exact criterion rather than an implementation-soundness result for
this submission: it says precisely what finite record must be added before a
later package may make that stronger claim.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
First suppose the extension record is present.  Replace the original row for
$c$ by the enlarged row whose claim-kind coordinate is $K\cup K^{\mathrm{impl}}$,
whose evidence surface is $E\cup E^{\mathrm{impl}}$, whose pass-record field is
$R\cup R^{\mathrm{impl}}$, and whose boundary field is the original boundary
together with $B^{\mathrm{impl}}$.  The first condition makes the stronger
reading visible to the dispatch table rather than hiding it in prose.  The second
condition supplies a finite submitted surface for the implementation object.  The
third condition supplies every gate record required by $\Gamma$ for the enlarged
claim-kind set, and the fourth condition prevents a path-verified implementation
record from being read as a command-run implementation certificate without the
command, source-state, environment, exit-code, and log-path fields.  Therefore
the tuple has exactly the fields checked by
Proposition~\ref{prop:finite-certificate-schema-verifier}.  Applying
Theorem~\ref{thm:publication-safety-interface} to the enlarged row constructs an
accepted finite certificate for the implementation-soundness reading, and the
accepted strength is precisely the declared evidence level because the schema
reads no unsubmitted evidence-level coordinate.

Conversely, suppose a manuscript row is read as an implementation-soundness claim
but no extension record with the four displayed properties is submitted.  If the
additional implementation claim kind is not present in the claim-kind coordinate,
then the stronger reading has no assigned implementation gate and is an
unsupported escalation of the original row.  If the kind is present but
$E^{\mathrm{impl}}$ is absent, the claim lacks a submitted evidence surface for
the implementation object, giving obstruction type~1 of
Theorem~\ref{thm:finite-certificate-obstruction-skeleton}.  If the evidence
surface is present but some gate assigned by $\Gamma$ has no corresponding pass
record in $R^{\mathrm{impl}}$, the bad subrecord is type~2.  If the
implementation reading crosses an intake, source, route, or venue boundary with
no required promotion or decision field, the bad subrecord is type~3.  If the
implementation reading is also a discovery or classifier-shift reading without
the pinned discovery-interface rows, the bad subrecord is type~4.  Finally, if
the row asserts command-run implementation strength without the command-run tuple
inside $B^{\mathrm{impl}}$, the bad subrecord is type~5.  These cases exhaust the
finite schema fields by the obstruction-skeleton theorem, so absence of the
extension record is exactly absence of finite support for the stronger reading.
Inspecting Table~\ref{tab:current-certificate-ledger} and
Proposition~\ref{prop:machine-readable-certificate-records}, the current package
records path-verified certificate rows $a_1,\ldots,a_7$ and command-boundary
inventory rows for bounded verifier invocations, but it does not add a new
implementation claim kind, implementation evidence surface, implementation gate
pass family, and implementation boundary tuple as a single
$\mathsf{Impl}(c)$ extension record.  Therefore this theorem is not instantiated
as an implementation-soundness theorem here; it remains an exact finite-record
extension test for a later package.
\end{proof}

\begin{corollary}[Submitted gate-implementation contract dichotomy]
\label{cor:submitted-gate-implementation-contract-dichotomy}
Fix the package ledger $L$, support surface $S$, and dispatch table $\Gamma$ of
the publication-safety interface.  A claim that the actual gate-producing
daemons or human workflow of the package satisfy record-gate soundness is
accepted by the theorem chain of this paper only in the following submitted-form
case: the package includes a finite implementation contract
\[
\mathsf{GateImpl}(P)=(I,E_I,R_I,B_I)
\]
where $I$ names the gate-producing implementations and human boundary
procedures, $E_I\subseteq S$ names their source, configuration, replay inputs,
and produced-row surfaces, $R_I$ contains a submitted pass record for every
implementation or human-boundary gate assigned by $\Gamma$ to that stronger
claim, and $B_I$ contains the command-run tuple whenever the contract is read at
command-run strength.  If no such finite tuple is submitted, then every system
soundness reading of Theorem~\ref{thm:publication-safety-interface} is rejected:
the theorem remains strictly conditional on the submitted gate-passage records
in $L$ and asserts no independent soundness of the daemons, scripts, or human
workflow that produced those rows.
\end{corollary}

\begin{proof}
The claim that the actual daemons, scripts, or human workflow satisfy
record-gate soundness is an implementation-soundness reading of the finite
record row.  Apply Theorem~\ref{thm:implementation-soundness-extension-criterion}
with $K^{\mathrm{impl}}$ equal to the additional implementation component,
$E^{\mathrm{impl}}=E_I$, $R^{\mathrm{impl}}=R_I$, and
$B^{\mathrm{impl}}=B_I$.  If the displayed contract is present, the enlarged row
has an explicit implementation kind, a submitted evidence surface for the
implementation objects and human boundary procedures, all assigned pass rows,
and the declared evidence-level boundary.  The finite certificate constructor
therefore accepts exactly that enlarged finite-record claim and no stronger one.

Conversely, suppose the manuscript is read as asserting such implementation
soundness while no tuple $\mathsf{GateImpl}(P)$ is submitted.  If $I$ or the
corresponding implementation claim kind is missing, the stronger reading is
invisible to $\Gamma$; if $E_I$ is missing, there is no submitted implementation
surface; if $R_I$ is missing, some assigned implementation or human-boundary gate
has no submitted pass row; and if $B_I$ is missing at command-run strength, the
evidence-level boundary is incomplete.  These are exactly the finite obstruction
cases classified by Theorem~\ref{thm:finite-certificate-obstruction-skeleton}
and by Theorem~\ref{thm:implementation-soundness-extension-criterion}.  Hence,
without the displayed tuple, the only remaining hypothesis available to
Theorem~\ref{thm:publication-safety-interface} is the conditional finite
predicate $\mathsf{RGS}_{S}(P,L,\Gamma)$ over the submitted gate-passage records;
no independent implementation-level soundness follows.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[External-interface projection and no-free-implementation upgrade]
\label{thm:external-interface-projection-no-free-upgrade}
Fix a package $P$, submitted surface $S$, acceptance ledger $L$, and dispatch
table $\Gamma$.  Let $\pi(P,S,L,\Gamma)$ be the finite record projection that
retains only the fields read by the certificate schema: claim kinds, evidence
levels, submitted evidence pointers, human-promotion fields, assigned passing
gate rows, discovery-interface rows, and command-boundary tuples.  Then the
following are equivalent for the publication-safety conclusion of
Theorem~\ref{thm:publication-safety-interface}.
\begin{enumerate}
\item The theorem constructs the accepted publication certificates for the
load-bearing rows of $L$.
\item The projected finite record $\pi(P,S,L,\Gamma)$ satisfies
$\mathsf{RGS}_{S}(P,L,\Gamma)$ and the remaining finite hypotheses of
Theorem~\ref{thm:publication-safety-interface}.
\end{enumerate}
Consequently any two gate-producing implementations, daemon histories, or human
workflows with the same projection $\pi(P,S,L,\Gamma)$ are indistinguishable by
the theorem: they yield the same accepted certificates and the same finite bad
subrecords.  A stronger reading asserting independent soundness of the gates,
scripts, daemons, or human workflow is accepted if and only if the projection is
strictly enlarged by an implementation-soundness extension record of the form
specified in Theorem~\ref{thm:implementation-soundness-extension-criterion}; in
particular, when a command-run implementation reading is asserted, that extension
must include command, source state, environment, exit code, and log path.  Without
that enlargement the stronger implementation reading is rejected while the
finite-record interface certificate, if otherwise valid, remains accepted at its
declared finite evidence level.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
The verifier predicates $P_1,\ldots,P_6$ in the certificate schema read exactly
the fields retained by $\pi$: the claim-kind coordinate, the evidence-level and
evidence-surface coordinates, the assigned gate rows, the human-promotion field,
the discovery-interface field, and the command-boundary field.  Therefore
replacing $(P,S,L,\Gamma)$ by another package with the same projection gives the
same input to Proposition~\ref{prop:finite-certificate-schema-verifier}.  The
schema-relative exactness theorem, Theorem~\ref{thm:record-acceptance-exactness},
then gives the same acceptance value and the same rejected bad subrecord for each
load-bearing row.  Proposition~\ref{prop:record-gate-soundness-external-interface-exactness}
identifies this projected record condition with the finite predicate
$\mathsf{RGS}_{S}(P,L,\Gamma)$ used by
Theorem~\ref{thm:publication-safety-interface}.  Thus the theorem's certificate
construction depends on the package only through $\pi$ and the explicitly stated
finite hypotheses, proving the equivalence and the indistinguishability claim.

It remains to characterize stronger implementation readings.  Such a reading
adds an implementation claim-kind component or raises the evidence level of an
existing gate row beyond the submitted row-and-pointer record.  By
Theorem~\ref{thm:implementation-soundness-extension-criterion}, acceptance of
that stronger reading is equivalent to adding the four implementation extension
coordinates $K^{\mathrm{impl}}$, $E^{\mathrm{impl}}$, $R^{\mathrm{impl}}$, and
$B^{\mathrm{impl}}$; at command-run strength the fourth coordinate contains the
command, source state, environment, exit code, and log path.  If the extension is
present, the enlarged projection is checked by the same schema and the stronger
finite-record claim is accepted at its declared evidence level.  If the extension
is absent, one of the finite coordinates required by the schema is missing, so
Theorem~\ref{thm:finite-certificate-obstruction-skeleton} supplies the finite bad
subrecord.  The original finite-record certificate is unaffected because its
projection has not changed.
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[Current-package acceptance and certificate ledger]
\label{prop:current-package-acceptance-certificate-ledger}
Let $P^{+}$ be the finite current package consisting of the primary source
\path{submission_abstract.tex}, this supplement \path{main.tex}, and, under the
exact-or-archive-equivalent support-surface condition of
Proposition~\ref{audit:review-bundle-availability-condition},
\path{review_bundle/REVIEW_BUNDLE_MANIFEST.json}, together with
\path{review_bundle/certificate_schema.json},
\path{review_bundle/current_package_pass_records.json},
\path{review_bundle/submission_interface_map.json},
\path{review_bundle/primary_claim_inventory.json},
\path{review_bundle/verify_certificate_records.py},
\path{review_bundle/certificate_verification_run.log}, and the explicit
historical paths named in the case catalogue.  Root-level diagnostics such as
\path{PIPELINE.md}, \path{SOURCE_MAP.md}, \path{ARTIFACT_INVENTORY.md}, and
\path{source_interface_record.json} may explain local provenance or path-verified
history, but they are not finite package coordinates for this proposition unless
their contents are represented in \path{main.tex} or in the submitted support
surface.  The TeX source fixes the displayed six-row ledger.  This state is denoted
$P^{+}_{6}$ in Theorem~\ref{thm:nested-package-states}: it is the row-frozen
certificate state for $a_1,\ldots,a_6$, before the submitted theorem-ledger
coordinate $a_7$ is adjoined.  The review-bundle files in this definition are
supplemental-record components: claims that depend on them are conditional on
those files being submitted with matching paths and records, or on an archival
equivalent satisfying Proposition~\ref{audit:review-bundle-availability-condition}.
Tables~\ref{tab:current-acceptance-ledger}
and~\ref{tab:current-certificate-ledger} form a finite submission-local
acceptance-entry ledger for the load-bearing current-package claims used in
Corollary~\ref{cor:current-package-path-verified-instantiation}.  For each entry
$a_i$, the two tables determine the certificate fields
\[
\mathcal C(a_i)=(K_i,\ell_i,E_i,H_i,R_i,D_i,B_i)
\]
and each such certificate is locally checkable at the path-verified evidence
level.  No load-bearing claim outside these six manually selected entries is
accepted by the current-package instantiation.  This is completeness of the
declared six-row package ledger only; it is not a deterministic extraction or
completeness assertion for all theorem-like environments or all possible claims
in \path{main.tex}.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
The acceptance-entry ledger is finite because Table~\ref{tab:current-acceptance-ledger}
has six rows.  Its rows enumerate exactly the assertions on which the current
package corollary depends: the bounded imported source interface, the existence
of the presentation-only package, and the four case-study lessons for intake
isolation, overlap blocking, fake-extension rejection, and artifact limitation.
The third column assigns the claim-kind set $K_i$ for each accepted assertion.
These kinds all lie in the seven-kind domain of the dispatch function $\Gamma$.

For each row, Table~\ref{tab:current-certificate-ledger} supplies the remaining
certificate fields.  The declared evidence level $\ell_i$ is path-verified in
every row.  The durable manuscript contribution is the finite row text printed in
the tables and the proof that these fields have the required schema shape.  The
matching assertions about JSON mirrors, pass-record files, manifest entries, and
verifier logs are supplemental-record assertions and require the named
\path{review_bundle/} files to be present.  The evidence surface $E_i$ is a finite
list of submission-local files, review-bundle records, pinned source-interface
records, or explicit historical paths.  The promotion field $H_i$ is either the current active-paper marker
\path{main.tex} together with \path{PIPELINE.md}, or the explicit marker that the
case row does not itself cross a new route boundary.  The pass-record field
$R_i$ names the finite gate records used by the row: source-interface extraction
and consistency for $a_1$, package and submission-pack records for $a_2$,
promotion for $a_3$, overlap and submission-state for $a_4$, theorem-content and
discovery gating for $a_5$, and artifact status plus limitation-ledger gating for
$a_6$.

The discovery field behaves exactly as required by the certificate schema.  It is
present only for $a_5$, the row whose accepted claim has discovery kind, and it
names the pinned source-interface rows that supply the certificate-and-ledger,
demotion, lineage, pipeline-safety, and main-pipeline witnesses.  For the other
rows the claim-kind set does not contain discovery, so $D_i$ is absent.  Finally,
the boundary field $B_i$ records the same limitation in every row: the package is
using path-verified evidence, not command-run evidence, unless the command,
source commit, environment, exit code, and log path are explicitly recorded.
Consequently the ledger is historical and path-verified; it does not assert a
fresh Lean build, daemon pass, full-source rerun, Rule110 or GroundCompiler
rerun, artifact-suite validation, or external venue acceptance.  Thus each row
determines a locally checkable publication certificate
\(\mathcal C(a_i)\).

It remains only to see that the ledger is complete for the declared
current-package instantiation.  Corollary~\ref{cor:current-package-path-verified-instantiation}
uses no unlisted positive claim: its source-interface premise is $a_1$, its
package premise is $a_2$, and its four bounded case-study premises are
$a_3$--$a_6$.  The proof does not claim that these six rows were produced by a
complete theorem-environment extractor or that they enumerate every theorem-like
obligation in this source; that stronger reading is isolated in
Theorem~\ref{thm:manual-ledger-dependence-boundary}.  Any proposed stronger
reading, such as a fresh full-source rebuild,
publication-daemon run, Rule110 dynamic rerun, artifact-suite semantic
validation, unledgered discovery claim, or external venue acceptance, has no
acceptance entry in Table~\ref{tab:current-acceptance-ledger} and also violates
the boundary field in Table~\ref{tab:current-certificate-ledger}.  By
Theorems~\ref{thm:finite-certificate-obstruction-skeleton} and
\ref{thm:evidence-level-overclaim-obstruction}, such a reading is therefore
rejected by a finite obstruction subrecord rather than accepted by this
package.\end{proof}

\subsection{Current-package instantiation}

\noindent\emph{Six-row and seven-row states.}
The current-package certificate state in this subsection is the six-row state
$P^{+}_{6}$ with acceptance rows \path{a1}--\path{a6}.  The later
theorem-obligation integration in
Theorem~\ref{thm:theorem-obligation-ledger-boundary} is the seven-row extension
$P^{+}_{7}$, obtained from $P^{+}_{6}$ by adding exactly the manual submitted
theorem-ledger coordinate \path{a7}.  Thus the two theorems describe nested support states,
not competing current-package mirrors.

\begin{theorem}[Current-package bounded schema-relative certificate exactness]
\label{thm:current-package-certificate-exactness}
For the six-row finite current-package state $P^{+}_{6}$ of
Proposition~\ref{prop:current-package-acceptance-certificate-ledger}, assume the
support-surface availability condition of
Theorem~\ref{thm:final-support-surface-closure}: the submitted supplemental
material contains the exact \path{review_bundle/} directory with
\path{review_bundle/FINAL_DIGESTS_SHA256.md}, with refreshed digest rows and
rerun finite-verifier logs for every named bundle coordinate regenerated after
the digest surface was written.  Equivalently, in the sense of
Theorem~\ref{thm:record-interface-rigidity}, the theorem-environment extractor,
certificate verifier, source-interface verifier, primary-claim verifier, and
final digest surface must be a common finite support-surface fixed point after
any regenerated inventory, log, or digest coordinate.  With that support
surface present, the six ledger entries $a_1,\ldots,a_6$ as printed in
Tables~\ref{tab:current-acceptance-ledger} and~\ref{tab:current-certificate-ledger}
and as checked by \path{review_bundle/certificate_verification_run.log} are
exactly the accepted schema-relative finite-record instantiation of the finite-record interface theorem,
Theorem~\ref{thm:publication-safety-interface}.  More precisely, the package map
\[
a_i\longmapsto \mathcal C(a_i)=(K_i,\ell_i,E_i,H_i,R_i,D_i,B_i)
\]
is a bijection from the accepted current-package claim rows printed in this
source to the locally checkable certificates accepted by the submitted schema
verifier at the declared path-verified evidence level, relative only to the
finite fields read by that schema and to the manually declared six-row ledger
classified in Theorem~\ref{thm:manual-ledger-dependence-boundary}.  Thus the
exactness is a row-frozen audit relation between six printed rows and six
finite certificates, not a claim that the surrounding workflow, gate
implementations, source tree, artifact evaluators, daemons, upload mechanisms,
archives, or venues are correct.  The assigned-gate rows are sound only in the
finite-record sense of
Theorem~\ref{thm:assigned-gate-soundness-audit-boundary}: they certify assigned
gate coverage and declared evidence pointers, while any stronger assertion of
independent gate implementation soundness would require source paths, commands,
source states, environments, exit codes, and log paths for those gates.  Without
that support surface, the theorem retains only the printed source-level inventory
of six intended certificate rows
and not the machine-readable current-package acceptance claim.  The durable TeX source
fixes the six row statements and their negative boundaries; the supplemental
record supplies the machine-readable mirror and verifier log only if it travels
with the submission or with an archival replacement satisfying
the final-support closure condition just stated.  Every
current-package reading requiring a seventh accepted row, a different claim kind,
a stronger evidence level, an additional gate pass record, a fresh command-run
tuple, an extractor-completeness assertion for the theorem inventory, or an
external venue decision is outside this bijection and is rejected by
the finite obstruction skeleton and, more sharply, by the positive extension-budget
criterion of Theorem~\ref{thm:finite-extension-budget-normal-form}.  The exactness
is therefore row-frozen, finite-record, and verifier-log bounded: editing any of
the six rows or any machine-readable mirror changes the finite package unless the
dependent extractor, verifier logs, and digest surface are rerun or regenerated
into the common fixed point required by
Theorem~\ref{thm:record-interface-rigidity}, and the displayed tables and logs
are updated together.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
\emph{Construction from the displayed rows.}
Proposition~\ref{prop:current-package-acceptance-certificate-ledger} constructs,
for each row $a_i$ of Tables~\ref{tab:current-acceptance-ledger}
and~\ref{tab:current-certificate-ledger}, a certificate
\(\mathcal C(a_i)=(K_i,\ell_i,E_i,H_i,R_i,D_i,B_i)\).  The row set is finite and
has exactly six elements.  The construction is injective because the row
identifier, accepted claim text, and evidence surface in
Table~\ref{tab:current-acceptance-ledger} are part of the displayed record data;
two different rows cannot have the same accepted current-package claim row.  It
is surjective onto the accepted current-package certificates because the same
proposition states that no load-bearing claim outside these six entries is
accepted by the current-package instantiation.

\emph{Support-surface instantiation.}
Conditioned on the supplemental records being present, matching the displayed
paths, and matching the final digest manifest
\path{review_bundle/FINAL_DIGESTS_SHA256.md},
Proposition~\ref{prop:machine-readable-certificate-records} supplies the finite
schema instance over \path{review_bundle/certificate_schema.json},
\path{review_bundle/current_package_pass_records.json},
\path{review_bundle/submission_interface_map.json},
\path{review_bundle/primary_claim_inventory.json}, the verifier script, and the
recorded verifier log.  The refreshed recorded log states command
\path{python review_bundle/verify_certificate_records.py}, source commit
\path{current local automath checkout plus final package digest manifest review_bundle/FINAL_DIGESTS_SHA256.md}, final package digest manifest
\path{review_bundle/FINAL_DIGESTS_SHA256.md}, environment \path{Python 3.10.11 on Windows 10}, exit code \path{0}, and log path
\path{review_bundle/certificate_verification_run.log}.  The companion logs
\path{review_bundle/source_interface_verification_run.log} and
\path{review_bundle/primary_claim_inventory_freshness_2026-06-07.log} likewise
record exit code \path{0} against the same final digest manifest.  That instance checks kind-domain membership,
path-verified evidence-level membership, assigned-gate coverage, the five
pass-record fields, discovery-field discipline, and the path-verified command
boundary for entries $a_1,\ldots,a_6$.  Hence every certificate in the image of
the displayed package map is accepted by the submitted schema verifier at exactly
the declared path-verified level when that supplemental record surface is part of
the review package.  The support surface is finite:
Theorem~\ref{thm:final-support-surface-closure} later proves that the bundle-backed
component for this package is admissible exactly when the exact
\path{review_bundle/} directory is submitted with its final digest manifest.
Without that finite surface,
the TeX proof still supplies the finite schema classification of the displayed
rows, but not the additional claim that the submitted executable mirror and log
were available to the reviewer.  By
Theorem~\ref{thm:assigned-gate-soundness-audit-boundary}, the same verification
does not audit the implementation internals of the named gates: it verifies the
presence and local evidence surfaces of the assigned finite pass rows, and a
fresh implementation-soundness reading would be a command-result escalation not
present in these six certificates.

\emph{Maximality and non-escalation.}
Conversely, a stronger reading changes at least one finite component of the
record: it adds a row, changes $K_i$, changes $\ell_i$, adds a required gate in
$R_i$, replaces the path-verified boundary $B_i$ by a command-run tuple, or adds
an external venue-decision surface.  Such a record is not in the six-row image
just identified.  By Theorem~\ref{thm:finite-extension-budget-normal-form}, a
genuine strengthening must pay a positive finite extension budget in added
coordinates, and by Theorem~\ref{thm:finite-certificate-obstruction-skeleton},
the first unpaid coordinate gives a bad subrecord: a missing evidence surface,
missing gate record, missing promotion or venue field, missing discovery-source
row, or unsupported command-run escalation.  Thus the six certificates are not a
sample of an unrecorded full-pipeline execution; they are exactly the row-frozen,
finite, path-verified package instantiation of
the finite-record interface theorem, Theorem~\ref{thm:publication-safety-interface}.\end{proof}


\begin{theorem}[Ledger dependence and semantic extractor-completeness boundary]
\label{thm:manual-ledger-dependence-boundary}
For the current package, the exactness assertions in
Proposition~\ref{prop:current-package-acceptance-certificate-ledger},
Theorem~\ref{thm:current-package-certificate-exactness}, and
Theorem~\ref{thm:theorem-obligation-ledger-boundary} are exactly finite-record
claims over the displayed ledger rows, the submitted theorem-obligation
inventory, and the syntactic theorem-environment extractor certificate of
Theorem~\ref{thm:deterministic-theorem-environment-extractor-boundary}.  They
therefore imply deterministic label coverage for the five environment classes in
$\mathcal E$, but they imply neither proof semantics nor completeness for
material outside that syntactic domain.  A stronger current-package claim of
semantic extractor completeness is accepted by the same certificate calculus if
and only if the support surface is enlarged by a finite semantic-completeness
coordinate consisting of: the semantic extractor or proof-checking command,
source commit, environment, exit code, log path, exact source file read, produced
inventory file, digest binding between the source and produced inventory, and a
gate pass row assigning that semantic record to the theorem-ledger or
traceability claim.  In the present package that semantic coordinate is absent;
therefore the accepted content remains the row-frozen certificate surface plus
syntactic label coverage, and every semantic extractor-completeness reading has a
finite bad subrecord.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
Let \(M_6\) be the finite set of six displayed current-package ledger rows
\(a_1,\ldots,a_6\), and let \(M_7=M_6\cup\{a_7\}\) be the seven-row surface used by
Theorem~\ref{thm:theorem-obligation-ledger-boundary}.  By
Proposition~\ref{prop:current-package-acceptance-certificate-ledger}, the
accepted current-package rows are constructed by reading the displayed row
identifier, accepted claim, claim-kind set, evidence level, evidence surfaces,
route boundary, pass records, discovery field, and command-boundary field.  By
Theorem~\ref{thm:current-package-certificate-exactness}, these six displayed rows
are then mapped bijectively to the locally checkable certificates accepted by the
schema verifier, conditioned on the submitted support surface.  These statements
have no input coordinate for semantic proof checking; their inputs are exactly
the finite row surfaces and submitted mirrors just listed.

The theorem-obligation extension adds the coordinate \(a_7\), which names the
submitted theorem-ledger row, the manifest-exposed inventory file, and the
syntactic extractor log.  Its proof checks manifest exposure, gate coverage,
evidence pointers, the extractor's finite label comparison, and the boundary
supplied by Proposition~\ref{prop:manual-theorem-inventory-boundary}.  Those
checks turn a detached local inventory into a submitted finite obligation surface
and eliminate the syntactic bad example of a labelled environment in
$\mathcal E$ absent from the inventory.  They do not parse mathematical
statements, replace the printed TeX proofs, or certify proof semantics.

Now consider a proposed stronger reading \(q\) asserting that the ledger or
inventory is semantically extractor-complete for \path{main.tex}.  This reading
is not record-equivalent to the accepted syntactic reading: it requires, at
minimum, a new command-boundary tuple naming the semantic checker or extractor
invocation, source state, environment, exit code, and log path; a
source-to-inventory digest binding that identifies the TeX bytes read and the
inventory bytes produced; and an assigned gate row saying that this semantic
record is accepted for the theorem-ledger or traceability claim.  These are
finite coordinates of the same kind as the extension coordinates classified by
Theorem~\ref{thm:finite-extension-budget-normal-form}.  If they are present and
pass their assigned gates, the enlarged tuple satisfies the six schema clauses
A1--A6 and is accepted by Theorem~\ref{thm:record-acceptance-exactness}.
Conversely, if the stronger reading is accepted,
Theorem~\ref{thm:finite-coordinate-determination-boundary} forces it to differ
from the syntactic reading in a coordinate read by the verifier; the only
coordinates capable of expressing semantic extractor completeness are precisely
the semantic command, digest, produced-inventory, source-file, and gate-pass
records just listed.

For the current package, no such semantic-completeness coordinate is one of the
six rows of \(M_6\), and the seventh row \(a_7\) supplies only the bounded
theorem-ledger coordinate and syntactic extractor run.  Therefore the
stronger semantic reading fails at the first missing schema coordinate.  By
Theorem~\ref{thm:finite-certificate-obstruction-skeleton}, that failed coordinate
has an inclusion-minimal finite bad subrecord: the missing semantic command-run
tuple, the missing source-to-inventory digest binding, the missing
produced-inventory coordinate, or the missing assigned pass row.  Removing the
stronger reading leaves the accepted finite-record and syntactic label-coverage
claims unchanged.  Thus the boundary is exactly the one stated in the theorem.
\end{proof}

\begin{corollary}[Row-frozen current-package invariance]
\label{cor:row-frozen-current-package-invariance}
Under the support-surface hypotheses of
Theorem~\ref{thm:current-package-certificate-exactness}, the current-package
acceptance decision factors through the six displayed rows
\(a_1,\ldots,a_6\) and their certificate fields only.  Any two ambient repository,
daemon, artifact, or venue states that induce the same six tuples
\(\mathcal C(a_i)=(K_i,\ell_i,E_i,H_i,R_i,D_i,B_i)\), the same submitted support
surface, and the same verifier log path induce the same accepted current-package
certificate set.  Conversely, a reading that depends on a seventh row, a modified
row, a fresh command execution, semantic validity of BEDC/Lean/Rule110 artifacts,
implementation soundness of the verifier or gates, upload status, or venue
decision is not invariant under this six-row projection and is accepted only
after it is added as a finite extension coordinate and rechecked.  Thus the
current-package theorem is exactly a row-frozen finite-record theorem, not a
current-execution reproducibility theorem and not a semantic-validity theorem.
\end{corollary}

\begin{proof}
Theorem~\ref{thm:current-package-certificate-exactness} identifies the accepted
current-package certificates with the bijective image of the six rows
\(a_1,\ldots,a_6\) under the map
\(a_i\mapsto \mathcal C(a_i)\), conditioned on the submitted support surface and
the recorded verifier log.  The verifier then reads only the finite fields of
these tuples, the fixed dispatch table, and the cited submitted surfaces.  By
Theorem~\ref{thm:finite-coordinate-determination-boundary}, two ambient states
with identical values on those finite coordinates have the same verifier
decision and the same finite certificate set.  This proves the stated invariance.

For the converse boundary, each listed stronger reading changes a coordinate not
contained in the six-row projection: a seventh accepted row changes the row set;
a modified row changes one of \(K_i,\ell_i,E_i,H_i,R_i,D_i,B_i\); a fresh command
execution changes the boundary field to command-run evidence; semantic validity
or implementation soundness adds claim kinds, evidence surfaces, and gate rows;
upload or venue status adds external route/submission fields.  By
Theorem~\ref{thm:finite-extension-budget-normal-form}, such a genuine
strengthening has a positive finite extension budget, and by
Theorem~\ref{thm:finite-certificate-obstruction-skeleton} an unpaid coordinate is
reported as a finite bad subrecord.  Hence the six-row exactness result cannot be
read as a claim about fresh reproducibility or semantic validity; those readings
require an enlarged record and a new acceptance check.\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Current-package extension-coordinate rigidity]
\label{thm:current-package-extension-coordinate-rigidity}
Assume the support-surface hypotheses of
Theorem~\ref{thm:current-package-certificate-exactness}.  Let
\(P^{+}_{6}\) be the accepted six-row current-package state and let
\(F_{6}\) be its finite schema projection: the row identifiers
\(a_1,\ldots,a_6\), their tuples
\(\mathcal C(a_i)=(K_i,\ell_i,E_i,H_i,R_i,D_i,B_i)\), the submitted support
surface, the fixed dispatch table, and the recorded verifier log.  Then
\(F_{6}\) is the largest current-package invariant certified by this submission
before adding a new finite coordinate.  More precisely:
\begin{enumerate}
\item any two ambient repository, daemon, implementation, artifact, upload, or
venue states with the same \(F_{6}\) have the same accepted current-package
certificate set;
\item no current-package reading that asserts gate-implementation soundness,
fresh full-source or artifact rerun success, artifact-semantic validity,
semantic theorem-inventory completeness, upload status, or venue acceptance is a
consequence of \(F_{6}\);
\item each such stronger reading is accepted by the publication-safety interface
if and only if the submitted package adds the corresponding positive extension
coordinate from Theorem~\ref{thm:finite-extension-budget-normal-form} and the
enlarged row passes the assigned gates; and
\item in the present package those extension coordinates are absent, so every
listed stronger reading has an inclusion-minimal bad subrecord of the kind
classified by Theorem~\ref{thm:finite-certificate-obstruction-skeleton}.
\end{enumerate}
Consequently Theorems~\ref{thm:publication-safety-interface},
\ref{thm:finite-verifier-normal-form},
\ref{thm:record-acceptance-exactness}, and
\ref{thm:current-package-certificate-exactness} form a current theorem chain only
for row-frozen, schema-relative, finite-record acceptance under the stated
record-gate hypothesis.  The implementation-soundness statement of
Theorem~\ref{thm:implementation-soundness-extension-criterion} is not
instantiated by this package; it is exactly the extension test a later package
must pass after adding implementation-gate and command records.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
The first assertion is precisely the finite-coordinate determination theorem
applied to the current package.  By
Theorem~\ref{thm:current-package-certificate-exactness}, the accepted certificates
are the image of the six displayed rows under
\(a_i\mapsto\mathcal C(a_i)\), conditioned on the submitted support surface and
the recorded verifier log.  By
Theorem~\ref{thm:finite-coordinate-determination-boundary}, a verifier decision is
a function only of the finite projection it reads.  Hence two ambient states with
the same \(F_{6}\) produce the same current-package certificate set, regardless
of unsubmitted implementation states, live daemon states, artifact worktrees,
upload histories, or venue decisions.

Now let \(q\) be one of the stronger readings listed in the statement.  It is
not a function of \(F_{6}\).  Gate-implementation soundness adds an
implementation object, an implementation specification, source or version data,
and implementation-gate or command-run evidence.  A fresh full-source or artifact
rerun adds a command-run boundary tuple and usually an output surface.  Artifact
semantic validity adds an artifact-semantic evidence surface and the pass row or
evaluator command that checks the asserted property.  Semantic theorem-inventory
completeness adds a semantic extractor or checker coordinate, source-to-output
binding data, and its pass record.  Upload status and venue acceptance add
external route, submission, or decision records.  None of these data is among the
six row tuples and support-surface fields constituting \(F_{6}\).

Since \(q\) is not record-equivalent to the accepted finite-record reading,
Theorem~\ref{thm:finite-extension-budget-normal-form} supplies a nonempty normal
form
\(\Delta(a_i,q)=(\Delta K,\Delta E,\Delta H,\Delta R,\Delta D,\Delta B)\)
for the row or rows affected by \(q\).  If the submitted package contains that
extension and the added coordinates pass the gates assigned by \(\Gamma\), the
enlarged tuple satisfies the six schema clauses; by
Theorem~\ref{thm:record-acceptance-exactness} it is accepted exactly as an
enlarged finite-record claim.  Conversely, if the interface accepts \(q\), the
same theorem forces the enlarged tuple to satisfy every clause, so the relevant
positive extension coordinates and pass rows must be present.  This proves the
if-and-only-if criterion.

It remains only to identify the present package.  Inspecting the six-row ledger
used by Theorem~\ref{thm:current-package-certificate-exactness}, each row is
declared at the path-verified finite-record level, with command-boundary fields
only for the bounded verifier logs explicitly named in the support surface.  The
proof of Theorem~\ref{thm:implementation-soundness-extension-criterion} observes
that the current package does not contain a single
\(\mathsf{Impl}(c)=(K^{\mathrm{impl}},E^{\mathrm{impl}},
R^{\mathrm{impl}},B^{\mathrm{impl}})\) extension record for the package gates.
The current package likewise supplies no fresh artifact-semantic evaluator log,
no semantic theorem-inventory completeness coordinate, and no external venue
decision row.  Therefore each listed stronger reading misses at least one of the
normal-form coordinates.  The finite certificate obstruction skeleton classifies
the first missing coordinate as a minimal bad subrecord: missing evidence surface,
missing pass row, missing route or venue boundary, missing discovery/interface
row, or missing command-run tuple.  Removing that stronger reading leaves the
six-row finite certificate set unchanged.\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Current-package semantic non-escalation]
\label{thm:current-package-semantic-non-escalation}
Let $P^{+}_{6}$ be the six-row current-package state of
Theorem~\ref{thm:current-package-certificate-exactness}, and assume the same
support-surface availability condition.  For each accepted row $a_i$ with
certificate
\(\mathcal C(a_i)=(K_i,\ell_i,E_i,H_i,R_i,D_i,B_i)\), the following are
equivalent for any proposed stronger reading $a_i^{+}$ of the same manuscript
row.
\begin{enumerate}
\item $a_i^{+}$ is accepted by the finite-record safety interface for the current
package.
\item The package supplies a positive extension record
\(\Delta(a_i,a_i^{+})\) of the normal form in
Theorem~\ref{thm:finite-extension-budget-normal-form}, and every nonempty
coordinate of that record lies on the submitted support surface and passes its
assigned gates.
\end{enumerate}
Consequently no row of $P^{+}_{6}$ entails, by itself, semantic correctness of
BEDC source theory, Lean, Rule110/GroundCompiler, the certificate-verifier
implementation, upload surfaces, or venue decisions.  In the current submission
those stronger readings are certifiable only as extension claims; when the
extension coordinates are absent they are rejected by a finite bad subrecord, and
the accepted content remains exactly the six row-frozen finite-record certificates
of Theorem~\ref{thm:current-package-certificate-exactness}.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
Fix $i$.  By Theorem~\ref{thm:current-package-certificate-exactness}, the row
$a_i$ is accepted exactly through the finite tuple
\(\mathcal C(a_i)=(K_i,\ell_i,E_i,H_i,R_i,D_i,B_i)\) at the declared
path-verified level, and the theorem identifies no additional accepted
current-package row outside the six-row image.  If $a_i^{+}$ is accepted as a
properly stronger reading, then it is not record-equivalent to $a_i$ in the
preorder of Definition~\ref{def:stronger-reading-preorder}.  Hence
Theorem~\ref{thm:finite-extension-budget-normal-form} collects the finite
differences into a positive extension record
\(\Delta(a_i,a_i^{+})\) consisting of added claim kinds, evidence surfaces,
gate-pass rows, boundary fields, discovery rows, or command-run data.  Acceptance
forces each added coordinate to satisfy the corresponding schema clause A1--A6,
so the extension coordinates must be submitted and gate-passing.  This proves
(1)$\Rightarrow$(2).

Conversely, suppose the positive extension record is supplied on the submitted
surface and every added coordinate passes its assigned gates.  Enlarging
\(\mathcal C(a_i)\) by those coordinates gives the certificate tuple displayed in
Theorem~\ref{thm:finite-extension-budget-normal-form}.  The six schema clauses of
Definition~\ref{def:certificate-schema-acceptance-table} then hold for the
enlarged reading, and Theorem~\ref{thm:record-acceptance-exactness} accepts
$a_i^{+}$ exactly at the declared evidence level.  This proves (2)$\Rightarrow$(1).

The listed semantic readings are all proper strengthenings of a six-row finite
certificate: BEDC and Lean semantic correctness add formal-source or
implementation-soundness coordinates; Rule110/GroundCompiler semantic validity
adds artifact-semantic or evaluator-run coordinates; verifier implementation
soundness adds implementation source, command, and pass records; upload and venue
readings add external support-surface or decision fields.  None of those
coordinates is one of the six row-frozen certificates identified by
Theorem~\ref{thm:current-package-certificate-exactness}.  If the corresponding
extension record is absent,
Theorem~\ref{thm:finite-certificate-obstruction-skeleton} returns the first
missing evidence surface, gate record, external boundary, discovery row, or
command-run tuple as a finite bad subrecord.  Therefore the current-package
exactness theorem is a finite-record certificate theorem and not a semantic
correctness theorem for the named systems or surfaces.\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Named boundary positive-coordinate classification]
\label{thm:named-boundary-positive-coordinate-classification}
Assume the support-surface hypotheses of
Theorem~\ref{thm:current-package-certificate-exactness}.  Let $F_{6}$ be the
finite six-row projection of
Theorem~\ref{thm:current-package-extension-coordinate-rigidity}.  Consider a
proposed strengthened reading of one of the following six boundary interfaces:
\begin{enumerate}
\item reading Theorem~\ref{thm:publication-safety-interface} as end-to-end
correctness of the AI-assisted workflow rather than conditional finite-record
non-escalation;
\item reading Theorem~\ref{thm:concrete-finite-script-implementation-soundness}
or Corollary~\ref{cor:finite-script-rgs-instantiation-boundary} as correctness of
the Python interpreter, filesystem model, verifier implementations, or external
artifacts rather than finite-script projection soundness;
\item reading Lemma~\ref{lem:source-interface-extraction-correctness} or
Proposition~\ref{prop:source-interface-record-file-consistency} as a fresh
full-source rebuild or semantic source-theorem import rather than the manual
path-verified source-interface record;
\item reading Theorem~\ref{thm:deterministic-theorem-environment-extractor-boundary}
or Proposition~\ref{prop:manual-theorem-inventory-boundary} as theorem semantics,
proof correctness, or scope adequacy rather than syntactic theorem-environment
inventory coverage;
\item reading Theorem~\ref{thm:finite-witness-manifest-record-interface} or
Corollary~\ref{cor:artifact-semantic-validity-boundary} as dynamic artifact
validation or artifact-substrate semantic validity rather than a manifest-bounded
record theorem; or
\item reading Theorem~\ref{thm:submission-support-fixed-point-normal-form} or
Theorem~\ref{thm:post-inventory-package-fixed-point} as mathematical-software
correctness rather than byte-coordinate support closure for local support files.
\end{enumerate}
Then exactly one obstruction schema controls all six cases.  Such a strengthened
reading is accepted by the publication-safety interface if and only if the
submitted package adds a positive extension coordinate of the corresponding type:
an implementation-soundness contract for (1) and (2), a deterministic
full-source extractor/rebuild and semantic source-theorem check for (3), a
semantic proof-audit or scope-audit checker for (4), an evaluator specification
and artifact-semantic rerun log for (5), and the external upload/archive,
fresh-command, or semantic-software records claimed by the stronger support
reading for (6).  If the corresponding coordinate is absent, then two ambient
states can agree on $F_{6}$ and disagree on the strengthened reading, and the
attempted strengthening has an inclusion-minimal bad subrecord of one of the
five types classified in
Theorem~\ref{thm:finite-certificate-obstruction-skeleton}.  In the current
package the listed positive extension coordinates are absent, so these six
interfaces contribute only their stated finite-record boundary claims to the
core theorem chain.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
First observe that the six displayed readings have the same formal shape.  Each
starts with a theorem already accepted through the finite coordinates of $F_{6}$
or through a support theorem cited by those coordinates, and each asks to add a
fact not read by those finite coordinates.  In case (1) the added fact is global
workflow correctness beyond record-gate soundness.  In case (2) it is correctness
of the executable substrate or of objects outside the parsed finite projection.
In case (3) it is semantic import of the pinned BEDC/newmath source rather than
line-range and record comparison.  In case (4) it is semantic theorem validity or
adequacy rather than syntactic environment coverage.  In case (5) it is artifact
semantics rather than manifest membership.  In case (6) it is external support or
software correctness rather than byte-coordinate digest closure.  None of these
added facts is a component of $F_{6}$.

For the forward direction, suppose one strengthened reading is accepted.  Since
it is not a function of $F_{6}$, it is a proper stronger reading in the preorder
of Definition~\ref{def:stronger-reading-preorder}.  The finite extension budget
normal form, Theorem~\ref{thm:finite-extension-budget-normal-form}, therefore
assigns it a nonempty extension record consisting of added claim kinds, evidence
surfaces, boundary fields, pass rows, discovery rows, or command-run data.  The
schema exactness theorem,
Theorem~\ref{thm:record-acceptance-exactness}, says that acceptance of the
stronger row forces every such added coordinate to lie on the submitted support
surface and to pass the gates assigned by $\Gamma$.  Inspecting the six cases
identifies the required coordinate types listed in the statement: implementation
and command records for global workflow or script-substrate soundness, extractor
and semantic-source records for source import, semantic proof-audit records for
the theorem inventory, evaluator and rerun records for artifact semantics, and
external-support or fresh-command records for stronger fixed-point support
claims.

For the reverse direction, suppose the corresponding positive extension
coordinate is added and its assigned gates pass.  Enlarge the original finite
certificate row by that coordinate.  The added evidence surface discharges clause
A1 of Definition~\ref{def:certificate-schema-acceptance-table}; the added pass
rows discharge clause A3; any human-promotion, discovery, or command-run fields
introduced by the coordinate discharge clauses A4--A6; and the unchanged original
row continues to discharge the remaining clauses.  Hence
Theorem~\ref{thm:record-acceptance-exactness} accepts the strengthened reading at
exactly the evidence level named by the added coordinate.  This proves the
if-and-only-if classification.

It remains to show minimal obstruction when the coordinate is absent.  By
Theorem~\ref{thm:finite-coordinate-determination-boundary}, verifier acceptance
is a function only of the finite coordinates it reads.  Therefore one may choose
two ambient states with identical $F_{6}$ but different unsubmitted facts for the
relevant strengthened axis: a sound versus unsound gate implementation, a true
versus false semantic theorem import, a complete versus incomplete proof audit,
a valid versus invalid artifact substrate, or an uploaded versus non-uploaded
support surface.  The finite verifier cannot distinguish the pair.  If it
accepted the strengthened reading from $F_{6}$ alone, it would accept it in both
ambient states, contradicting the assumed difference of the unsubmitted fact.
Thus some finite coordinate demanded by the stronger reading is missing.  The
obstruction skeleton theorem returns a minimal bad subrecord: missing evidence
surface, missing assigned pass row, missing promotion or route boundary, missing
discovery/interface row, or missing command-run tuple.  The present package has
only the six row-frozen finite certificates of
Theorem~\ref{thm:current-package-certificate-exactness} and the support-boundary
records explicitly classified later, so the positive extension coordinates just
listed are not instantiated here.\end{proof}

\begin{corollary}[Current package path-verified instantiation]
\label{cor:current-package-path-verified-instantiation}
For the current presentation package, let $P$ be the finite package consisting
of the primary source \path{submission_abstract.tex}, this supplement
\path{main.tex}, and the exact-or-archive-equivalent review-bundle support
surface of Proposition~\ref{audit:review-bundle-availability-condition}, together
with the executable certificate records named in
Proposition~\ref{prop:machine-readable-certificate-records}.  Root-level workflow
diagnostic files are local historical/path-verified context only unless their
records are represented on that submitted surface.  The path catalogue
in $P$, through the exact six-row certificate map of
Theorem~\ref{thm:current-package-certificate-exactness}, instantiates
the finite-record interface theorem, Theorem~\ref{thm:publication-safety-interface}, at the
path-verified evidence level as follows.  The imported discovery-gate interface
is fixed by the pinned newmath commit
\path{3fb3d6a0641767388a401883062aa522ea0b397b}, the source-interface table,
the JSON mirror, and the bundled source snapshot; the four case-study records
are bounded witnesses for promotion, route-overlap/submission-state,
theorem-content, and artifact-limitation gates; and every such case record is
usable only for the bounded manuscript lesson named in its row.  The finite
acceptance entries and certificate fields are exactly the rows of
Tables~\ref{tab:current-acceptance-ledger} and~\ref{tab:current-certificate-ledger}.
Therefore the
current package supports exactly the path-verified publication-safety claim that
its load-bearing case-study assertions are reviewable, gate-typed, and equipped
with locally checkable publication certificates at the path-verified level.  The
corollary is historical and record-level: it instantiates the certificate
constructor using the listed paths, tables, and bundle records, not by rerunning
the whole workflow.  It does not instantiate any stronger claim that the full
newmath source tree, a publication daemon, an artifact validator, or the Rule110
dynamic artifact suite was freshly rerun.
\end{corollary}

\begin{proof}
The package $P$ contains exactly the submission-local source files and manifest
records named in the statement.  Lemma~\ref{lem:source-interface-extraction-correctness}
shows that the source-interface table is the complete imported interface used by
this paper, and Propositions~\ref{prop:reviewable-source-snapshot-interface}
and \ref{prop:source-interface-record-file-consistency} identify the same
interface by pinned commit, JSON mirror, and bundled source snapshot.  Hence the
discovery-gate hypotheses of the finite-record interface theorem, Theorem~\ref{thm:publication-safety-interface} are
not supplied by free prose; they are supplied, at the bounded source-interface
level, by recorded paths and record files in $P$.  Proposition~\ref{prop:current-package-acceptance-certificate-ledger}
adds the submission-local acceptance entries for these premises: the imported
source-interface claim is entry $a_1$, and the route/submission package claim is
entry $a_2$.

For the case-study claims, Lemma~\ref{lem:gate-witness-criterion} states that a
record naming the source or artifact surface, failed invariant, gate decision,
and safe manuscript lesson is a path-verified safety-interface witness and not a
command-run witness unless the extra command-run fields are recorded.
Proposition~\ref{prop:path-complete-case-witnesses} applies this criterion to
the four case kinds used in the paper: promotion, route overlap, theorem-content
growth, and artifact limitation.  Thus the rows later displayed in
Table~\ref{tab:case-witnesses}, together with the manifest file named in $P$,
provide passable path-verified evidence surfaces for exactly those bounded case
claims.  Proposition~\ref{prop:current-package-acceptance-certificate-ledger}
identifies these four case-study claims with entries $a_3$--$a_6$ and records
their fields $K$, $\ell$, $E$, $H$, $R$, $D$, and $B$.  Applying
the finite-record interface theorem, Theorem~\ref{thm:publication-safety-interface}, to these recorded surfaces gives
the stated current-package instantiation: each bounded case-study assertion
obtains a finite certificate whose fields lie in $P$, the review bundle, or the
explicit historical-evidence boundary.  The obstruction
skeleton theorem then shows what a reviewer would have to find to reject any
purported stronger reading: a missing path, missing gate row, missing promotion
record, missing discovery-source row, or unsupported command-run escalation.
Since $P$ records no command, environment, exit code, and log path for a fresh
full-source rebuild, publication-daemon execution, or Rule110 dynamic rerun, and
since Theorem~\ref{thm:verifier-input-output-contract} gives the verifier no
output channel for fresh case-study rerun validity, the definition of command-run
evidence prevents the corollary from asserting any of those stronger claims.\end{proof}

\section{Gate summary}

\subsection{Case-study witness protocol}

Table~\ref{tab:gates} lists the deterministic gates used by
the finite-record interface theorem, Theorem~\ref{thm:publication-safety-interface}.  The rows are operational
interfaces: each gate has an evidence surface and a failure class it is meant
to prevent.

\begin{table}
\caption{Deterministic gate interfaces}
\label{tab:gates}
\small
\begin{tabular}{p{0.23\linewidth}p{0.33\linewidth}p{0.32\linewidth}}
\toprule
Gate & Evidence surface & Failure prevented \\
\midrule
Formal-source gate & Lean build, axiom checks, axiom-purity audits & broken or impure formal targets \\
Traceability gate & declaration inventory, source maps, marker audits & stale paper labels and missing source facts \\
Discovery gate & certificate ledger, demotion, lineage DAG & statement-only, code-only, or mechanical ``discoveries'' \\
Theorem-content delta gate & theorem inventory, phase-D hard lint, delta inventory & file churn or compilation reported as substantive theorem growth \\
Artifact-limitation gate & status files, limitation ledgers, rerun logs when present & drift from bounded artifact evidence to stronger dynamic claims \\
Scheduler gate & critical-path scoring and backlog windows & duplicated or random agent dispatch \\
Promotion gate & active \texttt{2026\_*} directory plus pipeline metadata & intake seeds entering the daemon prematurely \\
Overlap gate & route identity, semantic-overlap ledgers, submitted-sibling paths, board state & duplicate or racing paper routes \\
Submission-state gate & submission markers, venue state, board state, submission metadata & invalid advancement of active or submitted routes \\
Submission-pack gate & review, venue, bibliography, and metadata checks & unreviewed or under-specified submission packs \\
\bottomrule
\end{tabular}
\end{table}

\begin{lemma}[Gate-witness criterion]
\label{lem:gate-witness-criterion}
A case record is a historical path-verified witness for the safety interface
when it names the source or artifact snapshot surface, the invariant that failed,
the gate decision recorded for that snapshot, and the safe manuscript lesson.
Under the finite-record interface theorem,
Theorem~\ref{thm:publication-safety-interface}, such a witness can support a
case-study claim about the recorded historical gate surface, but not a stronger
command-run, benchmark, live-daemon, rebuild, dynamic-artifact, or semantic
validation claim, unless the corresponding command, source state, environment,
exit code, and log path are also recorded.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
The four named fields are exactly the data required by the definition of a
path-verified historical evidence level: they identify the snapshot surface on
which the case is checked, which gate predicate was evaluated, what decision was
recorded, and which bounded claim may be carried into the manuscript.  A live
benchmark, daemon execution, full-source rebuild, dynamic artifact run, or
semantic validator would add an event time, command, source state, environment,
exit code, log path, and usually a new artifact-output surface.  Those fields are
not read by a historical witness.  Therefore a witness record with only the four
path-verified fields supports the bounded case-study claim and cannot be used as
evidence for a fresh command-run assertion.\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[Path-complete case witnesses]
\label{prop:path-complete-case-witnesses}
Suppose a case-study row is used as load-bearing evidence for
the finite-record interface theorem, Theorem~\ref{thm:publication-safety-interface}.  If the row is path-complete in
the sense above, then the row supplies exactly the evidence surface required for
a path-verified historical snapshot case claim and no evidence surface for a
fresh daemon, full-source, theorem-content, artifact-suite, benchmark, or
semantic-substrate rerun.  In fact every accepted path-complete case row factors
through one of four finite witness skeletons---promotion, route/submission
blocking, theorem-content non-growth, or artifact-limitation disclosure---and its
admissible manuscript lesson is the unique bounded lesson attached to that
skeleton.  Replacing that lesson by a fresh experiment, dynamic validation, live
benchmark, or semantic artifact claim changes the skeleton by adding a
command-run or artifact-semantic coordinate, so the original row no longer
certifies the stronger claim.  In
particular, a promotion row is sufficient only
for the bounded claim that an intake packet is not daemon-visible publication
work before human promotion; a route-overlap row is sufficient only for the
bounded claim that the overlap and submission-state gates blocked advancement
until one of the recorded next actions occurs; a theorem-content row is
sufficient only for the bounded claim that file churn or compilation did not
show substantive theorem growth; and an artifact-limitation row is sufficient
only for the bounded claim that the artifact limitation must be disclosed or must
block promotion.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
Let $r$ be a path-complete case row.  By definition, every load-bearing
component of $r$ has a concrete source or artifact snapshot path, directory
manifest, or explicit historical boundary.  The promotion interface names the
candidate surface, human-promotion evidence, active-paper
markers, gate decision, and the bounded manuscript claim; these fields are
precisely the data needed to distinguish inactive intake from publication work.
The route-overlap interface names the active route, the submitted sibling that
creates the chronology constraint, the reason for overlap, the gate decision,
and the finite set of permitted next actions.  These are precisely the data
needed to check whether advancement is allowed without appealing to generated
prose, and the submission-state evidence prevents a route from racing an already
submitted sibling.  The theorem-content interface names the changed surface,
the theorem inventory or hard-lint surface, the substantive-content delta, the
gate decision, and the allowed manuscript claim; these fields are precisely the
data needed to reject extension claims based only on file churn or successful
compilation.  The artifact-limitation interface names the status file, the
limitation ledger, the observed limitation, the gate decision, and the only
manuscript claim that may be carried forward.  These are precisely the data
needed to prevent a failed or bounded artifact check from being promoted into a
stronger dynamic-validation claim.  Lemma~\ref{lem:gate-witness-criterion} then
applies to each such row:
the named paths give the source surface, the interface fields give the failed
invariant and decision, and the last field gives the safe lesson.  The same
lemma also rules out upgrading the row to a command-run claim unless the extra
command-run fields are recorded.  In particular, the proof does not rerun the
publication daemon, overlap scanner, theorem-content detector, artifact suite,
or GroundCompiler evaluator; it proves only that the historical path surfaces are
sufficient for the bounded lessons displayed in the table.

It remains to justify the skeleton and uniqueness assertions.  The operational
interface dispatches each case row by its gate family.  A row whose required gate
is promotion has exactly the promotion skeleton; a row whose required gates are
overlap and submission-state has the route/submission-blocking skeleton; a row
whose required gate is theorem-content has the non-growth skeleton; and a row
whose required gates are status and limitation-ledger has the artifact-limitation
skeleton.  These four skeletons are disjoint because their gate sets differ, and
the row's safe-lesson field is part of the finite record checked by
Lemma~\ref{lem:gate-witness-criterion}.  Hence, once the skeleton is fixed, the
only accepted lesson is the bounded lesson printed in that row.  A fresh
experiment or dynamic validation would require the command-run boundary fields in
Definition~\ref{def:operational-acceptance-interface}; an artifact-semantic claim
would require an additional artifact pass record or semantic evaluator surface.
Those coordinates are not read by the path-complete row and are not present in
the four skeletons.  The stronger reading is therefore rejected by
Theorem~\ref{thm:finite-coordinate-determination-boundary} together with the
finite obstruction skeleton, while the original bounded historical lesson remains
accepted.\end{proof}

\section{Case studies}

Each row of Table~\ref{tab:case-witnesses} is a witness in the sense of
Lemma~\ref{lem:gate-witness-criterion} and
Proposition~\ref{prop:path-complete-case-witnesses}: source or artifact paths,
failed invariant, gate decision, and safe manuscript lesson are all explicit.
The reviewer-facing path catalogue is the finite manifest row
\path{review_bundle/REVIEW_BUNDLE_MANIFEST.json:case_evidence} together with the
snapshots under \path{review_bundle/case_snapshots/}.  Newmath intake isolation
uses the bundled intake-seed manifest and the bundled active-paper-detector
snapshot, copied from the provenance paths
\path{automath/papers/publication/newmath_intake/seeds/bedc_automation_pipeline}
and \path{automath/papers/publication/pipeline_auto.py}.  The upper-fibers
route-overlap record uses bundled snapshots for the route identity, submitted
sibling marker, overlap ledgers \path{semantic_overlap_blockers.json} and
\path{semantic_overlap_blockers.md}, and the program-board state.  The
fake-extension case uses the bundled \path{phase_d_lint.py} snapshot and this
paper's submitted \path{ARTIFACT_INVENTORY.md}.  The Rule110 limitation record
uses bundled snapshots corresponding to \path{rule110/STATUS.md},
\path{rule110/docs/collision_audit_findings.md},
\path{rule110/docs/exhaustiveness_audit_findings.md}, and
\path{rule110/docs/papers_research_report.md}.  Machine-local absolute paths, if
present in the manifest, are provenance fields only; the load-bearing review
surface is the submitted bundle path or the submitted-package path named here.


\paragraph{Case-evidence protocol.}
The four case-study rows below are not additional mathematical theorems; they
are the path-verified protocol records used to instantiate
Lemma~\ref{lem:gate-witness-criterion}.  A row is complete for this purpose when
it names the source or artifact surface, the invariant that failed, the gate
decision, and the safe manuscript lesson.  For the present package the row
protocol is:
\begin{itemize}
\item \emph{Newmath intake isolation}: the intake-seed path and active-paper
detector show that a candidate remains inactive preparation until human
promotion creates \path{2026_*}, \path{main.tex}, and \path{PIPELINE.md}.
\item \emph{Upper-fibers overlap block}: the route identity, submitted sibling,
overlap ledgers, and program-board state show the recorded
\path{deferred_wait_for_prior_submission} decision and the finite next-action
set of closure, merge, supersession, or waiting for prior-route feedback.
\item \emph{Fake-extension block}: the phase-D hard-lint script and
this paper's artifact inventory show a theorem-content-gate rejection of file
churn or compilation that did not add load-bearing theorem content.
\item \emph{Rule110 limitation gate}: the status file, collision-audit ledger,
exhaustiveness ledger, and paper-data reference show a bounded limitation that
must be disclosed or must block artifact claims; they do not show fresh dynamic
validation.
\end{itemize}
Thus the rows prove path completeness and reviewability only in the operational
sense of Lemma~\ref{lem:gate-witness-criterion}; they do not certify fresh
daemon execution, full-source rebuilds, or Rule110 reruns.

\begin{proposition}[Review-bundle case-snapshot coverage]
\label{prop:review-bundle-case-snapshot-coverage}
For the four case-study rows in Table~\ref{tab:case-witnesses}, the review
bundle contains a finite external-review surface for every local case path used
by the paper.  The Newmath block has two local paths, the upper-fibers block has
five local paths, the fake-extension block has two local paths, and the Rule110
block has four local paths, for thirteen local-path strings after resolving this
paper's relative \path{ARTIFACT_INVENTORY.md} to its absolute path in the paper
directory.  The manifest file
\path{review_bundle/REVIEW_BUNDLE_MANIFEST.json} has one
\path{case_evidence} entry for each case row and, inside each entry, a
\path{reviewable_snapshots} list matching those local paths.  File-valued local
surfaces are copied into \path{review_bundle/case_snapshots/}; directory-valued
local surfaces are represented there by directory file-manifests.  Consequently
the case-study claim made by this paper is externally reviewable at the
path-verified snapshot level.  It is not a command-run validation claim, a fresh
full-source rebuild claim, a daemon-execution claim, or a fresh Rule110 dynamic
rerun claim.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
The path catalogue displayed at the beginning of this section is finite and
partitions the case surfaces into the four named rows: two paths for Newmath
intake isolation, five paths for the upper-fibers overlap block, two paths for
the fake-extension block, and four paths for the Rule110 limitation gate.  The
manifest row for each case repeats exactly the same local-path list in its
\path{local_paths} field and attaches a \path{reviewable_snapshots} list.  In
that list the Newmath row names
\path{review_bundle/case_snapshots/newmath_intake_seed_directory_manifest.json}
and \path{review_bundle/case_snapshots/newmath_pipeline_auto.py}; the
upper-fibers row names
\path{review_bundle/case_snapshots/upper_fibers_route_directory_manifest.json},
\path{review_bundle/case_snapshots/upper_fibers_SUBMITTED},
\path{review_bundle/case_snapshots/upper_fibers_semantic_overlap_blockers.json},
\path{review_bundle/case_snapshots/upper_fibers_semantic_overlap_blockers.md},
and \path{review_bundle/case_snapshots/program_board_machine.md}; the
fake-extension row names
\path{review_bundle/case_snapshots/fake_extension_phase_d_lint.py} and
\path{review_bundle/case_snapshots/fake_extension_artifact_inventory.md}; and the
Rule110 row names \path{review_bundle/case_snapshots/rule110_STATUS.md},
\path{review_bundle/case_snapshots/rule110_collision_audit_findings.md},
\path{review_bundle/case_snapshots/rule110_exhaustiveness_audit_findings.md},
and \path{review_bundle/case_snapshots/rule110_papers_research_report.md}.
These thirteen bundle paths are in bijection with the thirteen local-path
strings.  The two directory-valued local paths are not treated as opaque private
folders: their bundle entries are directory file-manifests, so a reviewer can
inspect the directory boundary and file list used by the row.  The remaining
eleven entries are file snapshots.  Thus the review surface no longer depends on
bare absolute paths alone.

The proposition's negative boundary follows from the same manifest entries and
from Lemma~\ref{lem:gate-witness-criterion}.  The \path{boundary} field for each
case records historical or path-verified evidence and explicitly withholds fresh
daemon, full-source command, and Rule110 dynamic-rerun claims.  A directory
manifest or copied file snapshot supplies reviewability of the cited surface; it
does not supply command, commit, environment, exit-code, and log-path fields.
Therefore the conclusion is exactly path-verified snapshot reviewability and not
any stronger execution claim.\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Case-witness skeleton rigidity]
\label{thm:case-witness-skeleton-rigidity}
Let \(r\) be one of the four case-study rows in
Table~\ref{tab:case-witnesses}, and assume the path-completeness and
review-bundle coverage conclusions of
Propositions~\ref{prop:path-complete-case-witnesses} and
\ref{prop:review-bundle-case-snapshot-coverage}.  Then the accepted information
carried by \(r\) is exactly one of the following four skeleton lessons and no
other reproducibility or semantic-validation lesson:
\begin{enumerate}
\item a promotion skeleton: an intake packet is not daemon-visible publication
work before human promotion;
\item a route/submission skeleton: an overlap or submission-state gate blocks
advancement until one of the recorded next actions occurs;
\item a theorem-content skeleton: file churn or compilation does not by itself
show substantive theorem growth or accepted discovery; or
\item an artifact-limitation skeleton: a bounded artifact limitation must be
disclosed or must block the corresponding artifact claim.
\end{enumerate}
Moreover this skeleton classification is rigid.  Any stronger reading of the
same case row as a fresh daemon run, full-source rebuild, theorem-content
rerun, artifact-suite rerun, benchmark, semantic artifact validation, or
Rule110/GroundCompiler dynamic-validation result is accepted by the
publication-safety interface if and only if the submitted package adds the
positive extension coordinates required for that stronger reading: a new claim
kind when needed, a submitted evidence surface for the run or semantic property,
assigned pass rows for the enlarged gate set, and the command-run tuple
containing command, source state, environment, exit code, and log path whenever
fresh execution is asserted.  Without those coordinates, the case row remains
accepted only at the path-verified historical-snapshot level and the stronger
reading has an inclusion-minimal finite bad subrecord.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
By Proposition~\ref{prop:path-complete-case-witnesses}, a path-complete case row
factors through exactly one of the four displayed finite witness skeletons:
promotion, route/submission blocking, theorem-content non-growth, or
artifact-limitation disclosure.  That proposition also identifies the unique
admissible manuscript lesson attached to the skeleton.  By
Proposition~\ref{prop:review-bundle-case-snapshot-coverage}, the local paths used
by the row have a finite review-bundle surface: thirteen total local-path strings
across the four rows, represented by copied snapshots or directory
file-manifests.  Thus the evidence coordinate for \(r\) is a path-verified
historical snapshot surface, not an executable event.  The four alternatives are
exhaustive for the submitted case evidence because the finite-row closure theorem,
Theorem~\ref{thm:finite-row-case-evidence-closure}, maps the finite set of four
case rows to exactly these four skeleton values and proves that no fresh rerun or
semantic-validation value is accepted from the same row data.

Let \(r^{+}\) be a stronger reading of the same row.  If \(r^{+}\) asserts a
fresh daemon run, full-source rebuild, theorem-content rerun, artifact-suite
rerun, benchmark, or dynamic Rule110/GroundCompiler validation, it raises the
evidence level from path-verified snapshot evidence to command-run evidence and
therefore changes the boundary coordinate \(B\).  Clause~A6 of the finite
certificate schema requires the command, source state, environment, exit code,
and log path.  If \(r^{+}\) asserts a semantic artifact property rather than only
a historical limitation lesson, it also adds an artifact-semantic evidence
surface and the assigned pass record for the enlarged artifact claim kind.  If
\(r^{+}\) asserts new theorem-content discovery, it adds the theorem-content or
discovery component and the pinned discovery/interface pass rows.  These are
precisely the positive extension coordinates listed by
Theorem~\ref{thm:finite-extension-budget-normal-form}.

If those coordinates are submitted and pass the gates assigned by \(\Gamma\), the
enlarged tuple satisfies the schema clauses, and
Theorem~\ref{thm:record-acceptance-exactness} accepts the stronger finite-record
reading at the declared level.  Conversely, if the stronger reading is accepted,
the same exactness theorem forces every changed coordinate to be present and to
pass the assigned gate checks.  When a required coordinate is absent, the failed
schema clause supplies the minimal bad subrecord: missing submitted evidence,
missing pass row, missing discovery-interface row, or missing command-run tuple.
Theorem~\ref{thm:finite-certificate-obstruction-skeleton} gives
inclusion-minimality.  Hence the original row is rigid at its skeleton lesson and
path-verified snapshot level, while fresh reproducibility or semantic-validation
claims require a separately paid finite extension budget.\end{proof}
