\section{Evidence boundary}

\subsection{Primary--supplement interface}

\paragraph{Submission-interface checklist.}
For this presentation-only route, the primary reviewed artifact is
\path{submission_abstract.tex} and the long record is \path{main.tex}.  The short
artifact must expose the following five interface components so that reviewers
can check it against the supplement without reading an abstraction proof:
\begin{enumerate}
\item an abbreviated publication-safety rule: agent output is advisory unless a
typed evidence surface and deterministic gate permit the corresponding claim;
\item an explicit pointer to
the finite-record interface theorem, Theorem~\ref{thm:publication-safety-interface}, and to the pinned discovery-gate
source interface at commit \path{3fb3d6a0641767388a401883062aa522ea0b397b};
\item the four case-row names from Table~\ref{tab:case-witnesses}: Newmath
intake isolation, upper-fibers overlap block, fake-extension block, and Rule110
limitation gate;
\item the evidence-level boundary that path-verified rows support reviewability
and path completeness, not command-run validation, fresh full-source rebuilds,
daemon execution, or Rule110 dynamic reruns; and
\item the supplemental-upload pointer to \path{review_bundle/}, which carries
the source snapshot, source-interface record, case-evidence manifest, and longer
technical account.
\end{enumerate}
The current short source satisfies this checklist in its structured-source,
publication-safety, and review-artifact paragraphs.  The checklist is the
submission contract; it replaces any need to treat this supplement's metadata
lemmas as part of the two-page presentation claim.

The venue facts used for the following local-readiness gate were checked on
2026-06-04 and rerun on 2026-06-07 local time against the official CICM 2026 call for papers at
\url{https://cicm-conference.org/2026/cicm.php?event=&menu=cfp}, the EasyChair
submission page named by the call at
\url{https://easychair.org/conferences/?conf=cicm2026}, and the Springer LNCS
author-guideline page linked from that call at
\url{https://www.springer.com/gp/computer-science/lncs/conference-proceedings-guidelines}.
The local checks recorded HTTP status 200 for all three pages in the run witness
\path{VENUE_CHECK_RUN_2026-06-04.log} and in the later live-check witness
\path{review_bundle/VENUE_BIBLIOGRAPHY_LIVE_CHECK_2026-06-07.log}, with exit code 0, and observed the same route
facts as the 2026-06-03 near-submission check: presentation-only papers are
work-in-progress submissions of two pages plus bibliography, receive a
light-weight review, do not appear in the Springer proceedings, have a
2026-06-15 submission deadline, and use the EasyChair/LNCS submission interface
unless the final form gives a different instruction.  These facts are inputs to
a local submission-pack readiness check only; if upload occurs after this check
or the form shows a conflicting instruction, the finite venue gate must be run
again and, if venue compliance is to be certified inside the submitted package,
the updated venue record and log must be included in the submitted support
surface.  Otherwise these file names record historical local readiness only.

\begin{proposition}[Fresh venue-record criterion]
\label{audit:fresh-venue-record-criterion}
Assume that, immediately before the actual upload action or at a time explicitly
recorded as unchanged for that action, the final submitted support surface
contains either the venue-check record \path{VENUE_CHECK.md} and log
\path{VENUE_CHECK_RUN_2026-06-04.log}, the later local-date rerun log
\path{review_bundle/VENUE_BIBLIOGRAPHY_LIVE_CHECK_2026-06-07.log}, or an
archive-equivalent replacement naming the same checked URLs, observation time,
route rules, command result, source state, environment, exit code, and log path.
Then the venue row of Proposition~\ref{audit:venue-submission-pack-gate} is
accepted for the current package only when the upload is made on that checked
rule state or after a later record with the same fields has replaced it.  If
that support surface is not submitted, or if an upload occurs after a venue-page
change without a replacement record, the package retains only a dated local
readiness observation and cannot certify upload-time venue compliance.

If the local venue-check files are not part of the submitted support surface,
mentions of \path{VENUE_CHECK.md}, \path{VENUE_CHECK_RUN_2026-06-04.log}, and
\path{review_bundle/VENUE_BIBLIOGRAPHY_LIVE_CHECK_2026-06-07.log} in
this supplement are historical/path-verified diagnostic context only, not finite
package coordinates for a venue certificate.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
The venue gate uses facts that are external to the manuscript: route class,
length, review status, proceedings boundary, deadline, submission form, style
interface, and software/data-access instruction.  These facts can change outside
the submitted source, so the gate cannot be discharged by a stale table row.
The stated support-surface hypothesis supplies the finite certificate surface:
three official URLs, a date and time, command-result fields, an HTTP-status run
log, observed route facts, and the boundary that a later upload or conflicting
form instruction triggers re-verification.  If the
upload uses that recorded rule state, each venue-dependent row of
Table~\ref{tab:venue-submission-pack-gate} is checked against a dated official
surface.  If the upload occurs after a change, or without a replacement record,
at least one external premise of Proposition~\ref{audit:venue-submission-pack-gate}
is no longer certified by the package; the venue row is therefore not accepted.
This proves the criterion and connects the fresh record to the local readiness
gate.  Without the support-surface hypothesis, the named local files are not
reviewer-available finite coordinates, so the same text can witness only a dated
historical observation.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Venue-rule freshness non-escalation]
\label{thm:venue-rule-freshness-non-escalation}
Let $U$ be the finite dated venue record consisting of the official call
URL, the submission-form URL, the style-guideline URL, the observation date and
time, the HTTP-status run witness \path{VENUE_CHECK_RUN_2026-06-04.log} or the later rerun witness
\path{review_bundle/VENUE_BIBLIOGRAPHY_LIVE_CHECK_2026-06-07.log}, the
observed route class, page limit, proceedings boundary, deadline, supplement
access rule, and the local witness rows in Table~\ref{tab:venue-submission-pack-gate}.
The venue component of the current finite-record package is accepted exactly for
uploads whose venue-rule state is represented by $U$ or by a later replacement
record with the same fields and with observation time immediately before upload,
or with a finite unchanged-rule bridge from the recorded check to the upload
action.  If an upload is made after a venue-page change, or if no upload-time or
unchanged-rule replacement record is supplied, then the package retains only a
historical venue observation and cannot certify submission-pack readiness,
EasyChair compliance, acceptance, or proceedings status.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
The venue rows used by the package are not internal mathematical definitions;
they are external facts read from the official call, submission, and style
surfaces.  Therefore the finite information available to the package about the
venue is precisely the tuple $U$ displayed in the statement: three source URLs,
one date and time, the HTTP-status run log, the finite route facts observed at
that check, and the corresponding local witness rows.  On the class of uploads
whose venue-rule state is represented by $U$, each premise of the local readiness gate is a component of that tuple, so
Proposition~\ref{audit:fresh-venue-record-criterion} and
Table~\ref{tab:venue-submission-pack-gate} determine the bounded readiness
conclusion.

Now suppose the venue page changes before upload or the upload is made without a
replacement record.  At least one external premise--route class, page limit,
deadline, submission interface, proceedings boundary, or supplement rule--is then
not determined by $U$.  Two possible upload states can agree on the historical
record $U$ while differing on that changed premise.  A certificate that is a
function only of $U$ cannot distinguish those two states, so it cannot certify the
stronger upload-time claim.  A later replacement record with the same fields is
exactly the finite enlargement needed to restore determinacy.  The excluded
claims of EasyChair compliance, acceptance, and proceedings status require still
more external records and are not fields of $U$.  Hence stale venue observations
remain historical only, while the accepted venue gate is the fresh finite-record
one stated above.
\end{proof}

\begin{corollary}[Historical venue record non-escalation]
\label{cor:historical-venue-record-non-escalation}
The venue check recorded at \path{VENUE_CHECK_RUN_2026-06-04.log}, with local
observation time \path{2026-06-04 10:15 Asia/Singapore}, is a historical venue
record.  For the present post-2026-06-04 source state it is superseded, for local
readiness only, by the rerun log
\path{review_bundle/VENUE_BIBLIOGRAPHY_LIVE_CHECK_2026-06-07.log} and its companion record
\path{review_bundle/VENUE_BIBLIOGRAPHY_LIVE_CHECK_2026-06-07.md}, whose local
observation time and exit code are the fields printed in those files.  Neither
dated record certifies actual upload-time EasyChair
compliance, acceptance, or proceedings status unless the final submitted support
surface also contains either an upload-time record with the same fields or an
explicit statement that the upload used the unchanged venue-rule state represented
by the latest dated record.
\end{corollary}

\begin{proof}
Apply Theorem~\ref{thm:venue-rule-freshness-non-escalation} first to the dated
tuple whose HTTP-status witness is \path{VENUE_CHECK_RUN_2026-06-04.log}.  The
2026-06-07 live-check log has the same required fields and is therefore the
later replacement record for local readiness after the source has moved past
2026-06-04.  Applying the theorem to that latest tuple gives exactly the stated
boundary: the package has a fresh local-readiness observation, but if the final
support surface contains neither an upload-time replacement nor a statement tying
the upload to the unchanged rule state represented by the latest tuple, then the
upload-time venue-rule state is not a finite coordinate of the package.
EasyChair compliance, acceptance, and proceedings status still require external
records not present in either dated tuple.
\end{proof}

\begin{corollary}[Upload-time venue-compliance record requirement]
\label{cor:upload-time-venue-compliance-record-requirement}
The logs \path{VENUE_CHECK_RUN_2026-06-04.log} and
\path{review_bundle/VENUE_BIBLIOGRAPHY_LIVE_CHECK_2026-06-07.log} are dated
local-readiness records.  For the current package they certify only the venue
rule state observed at their recorded observation times.  The current source
package contains no EasyChair upload receipt, no completed-upload timestamp, and
no finite statement tying a completed upload to the unchanged venue-rule state;
the public branch/path archive-equivalent locator is a support-surface locator,
not an upload-time venue-compliance record.  Consequently these logs do not
certify upload-time EasyChair compliance.  Such a certification would require a
future submitted support surface to contain either
\begin{enumerate}
\item a later upload-time venue/bibliography record with the same URL,
observation-time, command-result, source-state, environment, exit-code, and
log-path fields; or
\item a finite statement that the completed upload used the unchanged venue-rule
state represented by the latest included dated record.
\end{enumerate}
In the present package neither record is present, so all venue-dependent conclusions in
Proposition~\ref{audit:venue-submission-pack-gate} and
Table~\ref{tab:venue-submission-pack-gate} remain historical local-readiness
claims and cannot be escalated to upload-time compliance, acceptance, or
proceedings-status claims.
\end{corollary}

\begin{proof}
Theorem~\ref{thm:venue-rule-freshness-non-escalation} identifies the finite venue
record $U$ by its official URLs, observation time, run witness, observed route
facts, and witness rows.  The two named logs instantiate such records at their
own dated observation times.  A final upload-time compliance claim, however,
depends on an external state not contained in those dated logs unless the upload
is explicitly tied to the same unchanged rule state or a later replacement record
is supplied.

The present source package includes only the two dated local-readiness logs.  It
does not include a later upload-time tuple and does not include a statement that
a completed upload used the unchanged rule state represented by the latest dated
tuple.  Therefore two upload states can agree with all bytes of the current
package while differing on the live venue rule, submission form, deadline, or
supplement-access instruction.  A certificate depending only on the present
package cannot distinguish those states.  By the finite-coordinate boundary, the
stronger upload-time, acceptance, and proceedings conclusions therefore do not
follow from the current logs.  If a future package adds one of the two listed
records, that added finite coordinate is precisely the new premise needed to
re-run Proposition~\ref{audit:venue-submission-pack-gate}; it is not a coordinate
claimed here.
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[Round-five venue-record instantiation]
\label{prop:round-five-venue-record-instantiation}
The files
\path{review_bundle/VENUE_BIBLIOGRAPHY_LIVE_CHECK_2026-06-07.log} and
\path{review_bundle/VENUE_BIBLIOGRAPHY_LIVE_CHECK_2026-06-07.md}
instantiate the dated local-readiness branch required by
Corollary~\ref{cor:upload-time-venue-compliance-record-requirement}.  Their finite
fields are the command \path{PowerShell Invoke-WebRequest against CICM route pages
and bibliography authority pages}, environment \path{Windows PowerShell 5.1 from
paper directory; network enabled}, source state \path{current working tree for
main.tex, submission_abstract.tex, references.bib, theorem_inventory.json,
theorem_inventory.md, review_bundle/REVIEW_BUNDLE_MANIFEST.json, and
review_bundle/FINAL_DIGESTS_SHA256.md at the recorded 2026-06-07 local-readiness packaging}, exit code \path{0}, log path
\path{review_bundle/VENUE_BIBLIOGRAPHY_LIVE_CHECK_2026-06-07.log}, and record path
\path{review_bundle/VENUE_BIBLIOGRAPHY_LIVE_CHECK_2026-06-07.md}.  This is
not an upload-time venue-compliance record, EasyChair upload receipt, archive
locator, venue acceptance record, or proceedings-status certificate.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
The named log and record contain the five command-run fields required in
Definition~\ref{def:operational-acceptance-interface}: command, source state,
environment, exit code, and log path.  The URL rows record status \path{200} for
the CICM call page, EasyChair interface, LNCS guidance page, DOI or arXiv
locators, and public source repository, and its token rows record the required
presentation-only, two-page, bibliography, EasyChair, Springer/LNCS, and source
markers.  The final boundary line of the log states that the run is only a live
route and bibliography freshness check and excludes venue acceptance, upload
receipt, public archive locator, Lean rebuild, and artifact reruns.  Therefore it
is exactly a dated local-readiness record in the sense of
Corollary~\ref{cor:upload-time-venue-compliance-record-requirement}, and it does
not supply the stronger external records explicitly excluded in the statement.
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[Venue-submission pack gate]
\label{audit:venue-submission-pack-gate}
Assume only the dated venue-rule records currently named in this source package,
with no upload receipt, no completed-upload timestamp, no archive-equivalent
locator, and no unchanged-rule statement for a completed upload.  Then the
current package is submission-pack ready for this route only as a local
pre-upload checklist exactly when the finite gate in
Table~\ref{tab:venue-submission-pack-gate} passes: the primary artifact is the
two-page-plus-bibliography source \path{submission_abstract.tex}, the LNCS-style
source and bibliography files are present, the selected exact support surface
\path{review_bundle/} is supplied with
\path{review_bundle/FINAL_DIGESTS_SHA256.md} and the root
\path{theorem_inventory.json} coordinate named there, and the title/author
metadata remains in the primary source.  This gate is a local readiness
certificate only; it does not assert final-upload-time venue compliance,
EasyChair upload, reviewer approval, acceptance, camera-ready approval, or
proceedings publication.  A future stronger reading requires the additional
upload-time finite record specified in
Corollary~\ref{cor:upload-time-venue-compliance-record-requirement} and
Theorem~\ref{thm:venue-rule-freshness-non-escalation}.
\end{proposition}


\medskip
\begin{proof}
The table has five rows, so the check is finite.  The route-class row compares
the venue's presentation-only rule with the local role separation already fixed
by Proposition~\ref{prop:final-package-shape-convention}:
\path{submission_abstract.tex} is primary, while \path{main.tex},
\path{theorem_inventory.json}, and exact \path{review_bundle/} inclusion are
support artifacts.  If the primary artifact is not the short source, if those
support artifacts are presented as a second primary proceedings paper, or if the
bundle branch is silently changed to an unrecorded archive replacement, this row
fails.  If it passes, the row proves only compatibility with the
presentation-only artifact split, because its
boundary column expressly excludes acceptance and proceedings-publication
claims.

The formatting row is a syntactic source check.  The manuscript begins with the
LNCS class \path{llncs}; the package uses \path{splncs04} for the bibliography;
and the local compiled short artifact and bibliography files are present as
\path{submission_abstract.pdf} and \path{submission_abstract.bbl}.  These are
finite path and token checks.  They do not inspect Springer production systems,
nor do they prove that the venue will accept the formatting; hence the row gives
local formatting readiness only.  The supplement-access row is finite because it
requires the selected exact bundle files, the final digest manifest, and the
root theorem-inventory coordinate named by the manifest and certificate ledger.
Audit record~\ref{audit:review-bundle-archive-equivalence} remains only the
criterion that would have to be satisfied by a future archive replacement; it is
not the branch selected by the current source package.  The metadata and
bibliography row checks the title, author block, institute/email block,
bibliography command, and \path{references.bib}; it does not certify external
metadata entry.  The deadline row records only that the package can be checked
against the venue date before upload and does not assert that an upload was made.

If all rows pass, each venue-facing pre-upload checklist claim used by this
manuscript has a finite local witness and an explicit negative boundary, so the
package is ready in the limited local sense stated in the proposition.
Conversely, if any row fails, one of the finite route-class, formatting,
supplement-access, metadata, bibliography, or deadline witnesses required by the
table is absent or stale; there is then no local certificate for submission-pack
readiness.  Because the hypothesis deliberately omits an upload receipt,
completed-upload timestamp, archive-equivalent locator, and unchanged-rule
statement for a completed upload, no row can identify the venue-rule state at
the actual upload event.  The final sentence follows from the boundary column,
Corollary~\ref{cor:upload-time-venue-compliance-record-requirement}, and
Definition~\ref{def:operational-acceptance-interface}: external upload,
upload-time compliance, acceptance, camera-ready approval, and publication are
editorial or venue events, not local finite-record consequences of this gate.
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[Submission artifact role separation]
\label{audit:submission-artifact-role-separation}
For the current presentation-only route, the primary artifact, long technical
supplement, and review bundle have distinct review roles.  The primary source
\path{submission_abstract.tex} states the presentation claim, \path{main.tex}
proves the long-form interface theorems and records the case tables, and
\path{review_bundle/} supplies the supplemental source snapshots, case snapshots,
and machine-readable manifest rows.  None of these roles converts the support
bundle into a second primary submission or converts a primary-artifact claim into
an uncited supplement claim.
\end{proposition}


\medskip
\begin{proof}
The manifest field \path{submission_artifacts} names the short source, intended
short PDF, long technical source, long technical PDF, and supplemental bundle as
separate artifacts.  The checklist above fixes how the short artifact points to
the supplement: it names the safety theorem, the case rows, the evidence-level
boundary, and \path{review_bundle/}.  The earlier source-snapshot and
case-snapshot propositions identify the two support functions of the bundle: a
pinned discovery-gate source snapshot and bounded case-evidence snapshots.  Since
the primary note states only the interface and each support claim is tied to a
specific theorem, table, or manifest field, the artifacts remain role-separated:
the support materials make the primary note reviewable but do not become hidden
primary claims.\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[Formal-source gate instantiation]
\label{prop:formal-source-gate-instantiation}
For the current presentation package, the Lean-facing assertions in rows
\path{pc3} and \path{pc4} of Table~\ref{tab:primary-claim-inventory} are accepted
only as bounded contextual interfaces, not as accepted formal-source claims.  The
accepted certificate surface is the traceability row $a_1$, the fake-extension
boundary row $a_5$, Table~\ref{tab:source-interface-record},
\path{source_interface_record.json},
\path{review_bundle/primary_claim_inventory.json}, and
\path{review_bundle/certificate_schema.json}.  These records certify that the
short note may mention the mathlib-free design, marker-audit, and axiom-purity
audit vocabulary only as source-interface context and only with the evidence
boundary stated in rows \path{pc3} and \path{pc4}.  They do not certify a fresh
Lean build, a current full-source marker audit, an absence-of-axioms theorem, or
an accepted axiom-purity pass for the whole newmath tree.  Conversely, a
Lean-facing sentence in this package can become a current accepted formal-source
claim exactly by adding an accepted formal-source certificate row with build-gate
and axiom-purity-gate pass records; if the sentence asserts freshness or currency
of an execution, the row must also include the command, source commit or local
state, environment, exit code, and log path required for command-run evidence.
With the present records, every stronger reading of rows \path{pc3} and
\path{pc4} has a minimal bad subrecord of missing formal-source gates or missing
command-run fields.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
By the dispatch rule in the operational interface, an accepted formal-source
claim would have claim kind \emph{formal-source} and therefore would require
pass records for both the build gate and the axiom-purity gate.  The current
acceptance ledger has exactly the six rows $a_1,\ldots,a_6$ displayed in
Table~\ref{tab:current-acceptance-ledger}; none has claim kind
\emph{formal-source}.  The machine-readable schema mirrors this fact: its
\path{claim_kind_domain} includes \path{formal-source}, and its
\path{gate_dispatch.formal-source} field names \path{build} and
\path{axiom-purity}, but the current pass-record ledger uses $a_1$ for
traceability, $a_5$ for rejected stronger discovery/theorem-content claims, and
contains no accepted formal-source row with build and axiom-purity pass records.

It follows that the only positive certificate available for rows \path{pc3} and
\path{pc4} is contextual.  Row $a_1$ supplies inventory and marker-existence
records for the pinned source-interface snapshot, so the paper may say which
Lean-related source-interface vocabulary is being imported.  Row $a_5$ supplies
the boundary that file churn, compilation, or a fake extension is not theorem
growth or discovery, so the paper may explain why formal-looking material is not
promoted without its gate records.  The certificate schema and primary-claim
inventory make the boundary checkable by naming the absent stronger level: a
formal-source acceptance would need build and axiom-purity pass records, and a
command-run version would additionally need command, source commit, environment,
exit code, and log path.  Since those fields are absent from the current package,
Proposition~\ref{prop:finite-certificate-schema-verifier} rejects the stronger
reading by a finite missing-gate or evidence-level subrecord.  The proposition
therefore certifies exactly the bounded contextual Lean interface and no fresh
Lean build or full-source axiom audit.

For the converse boundary, suppose a revised package did add a formal-source
certificate row for one of these sentences.  By the deterministic dispatch rule,
the necessary gate set for the formal-source component is precisely
\(\{\texttt{build},\texttt{axiom-purity}\}\), so pass records for those two gates are
sufficient for the finite schema verifier as far as the formal-source component is
concerned, provided the other coordinates of the record meet the same evidence,
promotion, discovery, and boundary checks as any accepted claim.  If the revised
sentence also says that the build or audit was freshly executed for the current
package, Definition~\ref{def:operational-acceptance-interface} adds exactly the
five command-run fields.  Therefore the present package is not merely silent
about currency: it is a complete negative instantiation of the formal-source
gate, and currency can be claimed only by supplying the missing finite rows.\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Formal-source promotion obstruction]
\label{thm:formal-source-promotion-obstruction}
In the current presentation package, no manuscript sentence about the Lean/BEDC
source interface can be promoted from bounded contextual interface evidence to an
accepted formal-source claim unless the certificate is enlarged by an accepted
formal-source row containing build-gate and axiom-purity-gate pass records.  If a
fresh execution reading is also asserted, the certificate must additionally
contain the command, source commit or final package digest manifest, environment, exit code, and log
path required for command-run evidence.  With the present six-row package ledger,
every such promotion attempt has a finite bad subrecord and is rejected by the
schema verifier.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
The dispatch table sends a formal-source claim to the two gates named in
Proposition~\ref{prop:formal-source-gate-instantiation}: build and
axiom-purity.  Therefore any accepted formal-source version of rows \path{pc3}
or \path{pc4} would need a certificate whose kind set contains
\emph{formal-source} and whose pass-record field $R$ contains one pass record for
each of these two gates.  By
Theorem~\ref{thm:current-package-certificate-exactness}, the current package has
exactly the accepted certificates $a_1,\ldots,a_6$.  None has claim kind
\emph{formal-source}; none contains build-gate and axiom-purity-gate pass records;
and the only positive Lean-facing surface is the bounded traceability and
source-interface context identified in
Proposition~\ref{prop:formal-source-gate-instantiation}.

If the promoted reading also asserts a fresh Lean build, current full-source
marker audit, full-source axiom audit, or command-run theorem-source check, then
Definition~\ref{def:operational-acceptance-interface} requires the command-run
tuple: command, source commit or final package digest manifest, environment, exit code, and log
path.  The boundary fields in Tables~\ref{tab:current-certificate-ledger}
and~\ref{tab:command-boundary-inventory} state that no such command-run tuple is
present for the Lean/BEDC source theorem semantics.  Thus a formal-source
promotion fails check~(iii) of the finite verifier by missing required gate pass
records; a fresh-execution promotion fails check~(vi) by unsupported command-run
escalation.  In either case Theorem~\ref{thm:finite-certificate-obstruction-skeleton}
extracts the failed fields as a finite bad subrecord.  Hence the current package
keeps the Lean/BEDC material at the declared bounded contextual level unless new
formal-source and command-run evidence rows are actually added.\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Evidence-level overclaim obstruction]
\label{thm:evidence-level-overclaim-obstruction}
Fix the certificate schema, dispatch function, and evidence-level vocabulary of
this paper.  Let $c$ be a load-bearing claim whose accepted certificate has
level path-verified and whose boundary field $B$ records that no command-run
assertion is made.  Then every stronger reading of $c$ that asserts any of the
following additional conclusions is rejected by the finite schema verifier:
\begin{enumerate}
\item external gate soundness for an arbitrary implementation of the workflow;
\item Lean correctness, full-source rebuild success, or axiom-purity beyond the
recorded formal-source gate fields;
\item end-to-end external semantic validity of artifact data beyond the finite
manifest and evaluator surfaces named in $E$ and $R$;
\item a fresh daemon run, full-source rerun, Rule110 or GroundCompiler rerun, or
journal or workshop acceptance; or
\item bundle-backed reviewability when neither \path{review_bundle/} nor an
archival equivalent carrying the same finite records is in the submitted
artifact boundary.
\end{enumerate}
More precisely, such a stronger reading has an inclusion-minimal bad subrecord of
one of the five types in
Theorem~\ref{thm:finite-certificate-obstruction-skeleton}.  Hence the accepted
claim is rigid at its declared evidence level: path-verified certificate records
cannot be promoted by prose into command-run evidence, semantic validation,
formal-source correctness, or external acceptance.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
Let $c^+$ be one of the stronger readings.  It has the same displayed manuscript
sentence as $c$ but a larger claim-kind set, a stronger evidence level, or a
larger submitted-artifact boundary requirement.  The verifier does not inspect
rhetorical emphasis; by Proposition~\ref{prop:finite-certificate-schema-verifier}
it inspects only the finite tuple
$\mathcal C=(K,\ell,E,H,R,D,B)$ and the finite dispatch table.

If $c^+$ asserts external gate soundness for an arbitrary implementation, then
it is not merely the finite certificate-construction claim accepted by
the finite-record interface theorem, Theorem~\ref{thm:publication-safety-interface}.  It needs pass records showing
that the relevant implementation ran every gate in
$\bigcup_{k\in K}\Gamma(k)$.  Those records are not supplied by the
path-verified certificate for $c$, so the minimal bad subrecord is a missing
pass-record obstruction of type~2.  If $c^+$ asserts Lean correctness,
full-source rebuild success, or axiom-purity, then it is a formal-source or
command-run claim.  Proposition~\ref{prop:formal-source-gate-instantiation} and
Theorem~\ref{thm:formal-source-promotion-obstruction} identify the required
build, axiom-purity, and command-run fields; when they are absent, the minimal
bad subrecord is type~2 for missing formal-source gates or type~5 for command-run
escalation.

If $c^+$ asserts end-to-end semantic validity of artifacts beyond the named
manifest and evaluator surfaces, then it has an artifact component not covered
by the finite status and limitation-ledger records in $R$.  The current
acceptance and certificate ledgers display only the bounded artifact row $a_6$:
its evidence surface is the Rule110 status file, limitation ledgers,
paper-data reference, and review-bundle manifest, and its boundary field says
that no fresh artifact rerun is asserted.  A stronger artifact claim therefore
lacks an assigned artifact pass record, giving type~2,
or asserts a fresh evaluator run without command-run fields, giving type~5.  The
same argument covers a fresh daemon run, full-source rerun, and Rule110 or
GroundCompiler rerun: each is a command-run or artifact-strength escalation and
is rejected by the missing gate or command-run boundary fields.

If $c^+$ asserts journal or workshop acceptance, then its acceptance-entry field
would have to contain an editorial or venue acceptance record.  The operational
notion of acceptance entry in this paper is internal and submission-local; the
current ledger contains no external editorial acceptance row.  Thus the stronger
reading either lacks the required route/submission pass record, giving type~2,
or lacks the relevant external evidence surface, giving type~1.  Finally, if a
bundle-backed claim is made while no review bundle or archival equivalent lies
inside the submitted artifact boundary, the evidence item named by the
certificate is absent from $E$ at the declared level, so the minimal bad
subrecord is type~1.  These cases exhaust the displayed stronger readings.
Theorem~\ref{thm:finite-certificate-obstruction-skeleton} then gives the stated
classification and proves rigidity at the declared evidence level.\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Finite-witness manifest-bounded record interface]
\label{thm:finite-witness-manifest-record-interface}
This theorem is a manifest-bounded finite-record interface instance of
Theorem~\ref{thm:evidence-level-overclaim-obstruction}: it records only the
bounded manifest/evaluator coordinates that may be checked, and it rejects
stronger artifact readings unless the package supplies the additional finite
records required for them, such as semantic-validation pass records or fresh
rerun logs.  The interface domain is the finite set
\[
\mathcal M_{a_6}=\{\text{manifest entry},\text{local paths},
\text{reviewable snapshots},\text{boundary field},
\text{status pass row},\text{limitation-ledger pass row}\}.
\]
No Rule110 or GroundCompiler property outside $\mathcal M_{a_6}$ is asserted or certified; semantic validation outside this finite set requires a new artifact or evaluator-command certificate.

For the current presentation package, assuming the named review-bundle records
are supplied as supplemental material or by an accepted archive-equivalent
surface, the finite-witness assertions in rows
\path{pc5}, \path{pc6}, and \path{pc11} of
Table~\ref{tab:primary-claim-inventory} have the following bounded interface.
The assertions printed in this source are manuscript-source assertions about the
allowed finite-record reading; the path checks against the status files,
limitation ledgers, and machine-readable manifest are supplemental-record
assertions conditional on the named \path{review_bundle/} records being submitted
and matching those paths.
Row \path{pc5} is contextual: it states the evidence discipline that finite
witnesses are bound by manifest rows, evaluator outcomes, and substrate
contracts rather than by Lean alone.  Rows \path{pc6} and \path{pc11} are
accepted only as the bounded artifact lesson $a_6$.  Their named manifest and
evaluator surfaces are \path{manifest:case_evidence[3]}, its
\path{local_paths}, \path{reviewable_snapshots}, and \path{boundary} fields,
the \path{status} and \path{limitation-ledger} pass records for $a_6$ in
\path{review_bundle/current_package_pass_records.json},
\path{review_bundle/case_snapshots/rule110_STATUS.md},
\path{review_bundle/case_snapshots/rule110_collision_audit_findings.md},
\path{review_bundle/case_snapshots/rule110_exhaustiveness_audit_findings.md},
and \path{review_bundle/case_snapshots/rule110_papers_research_report.md}.
Thus the Rule110/GroundCompiler statement is a finite manifest-bounded artifact
status and limitation-ledger claim, not a claim of Rule110 universality, a fresh
evaluator run, a dynamic behavior theorem, or an unbounded artifact validation.
Its classification is path-complete only for the named finite records in
$\mathcal M_{a_6}$.  Moreover this bounded interface is invariant under
manifest-record equivalence: any two review states
that agree on the named manifest entry, reviewable snapshots, pass-record rows,
and boundary field receive the same publication certificate from this package.
Therefore no additional Rule110/GroundCompiler artifact property can be derived
from the current certificate unless it is already expressible as one of those
finite manifest-bounded fields or a new artifact/command-run certificate is
added.  In particular, any stronger Rule110 or GroundCompiler reading asserting a
fresh evaluator outcome, semantic validation, dynamic behavior, collision-audit
rerun, exhaustiveness rerun, or universality-level artifact fact requires fresh
evaluator logs with command, source state, environment, exit code, and log path;
manifest rows and historical snapshots alone do not supply such a claim.  The
theorem is exactly a finite-record boundary theorem for the artifact
row $a_6$ in the finite-record interface theorem, Theorem~\ref{thm:publication-safety-interface}: it preserves the
bounded artifact-limitation lesson and classifies all readings requiring further
artifact evidence as outside the current finite certificate.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
The finite-witness vocabulary in \path{pc5} is not itself an artifact pass: it
describes which objects would bind a finite substrate claim.  Under the schema
verifier, such a statement becomes load-bearing only when a finite artifact
certificate supplies the assigned artifact gates.  The dispatch function sends
artifact claims to the status and limitation-ledger gates.  In the current
ledger, the only artifact entry is $a_6$, and
Table~\ref{tab:current-certificate-ledger} gives its evidence surface: the Rule110
status file, collision and exhaustiveness ledgers, paper-data reference, and the
review-bundle manifest.  The same row records the boundary excluding a fresh
\path{make test-collision-audit} or dynamic artifact rerun.  Therefore the only
coordinates available to the artifact certificate are precisely the finite
coordinates collected in $\mathcal M_{a_6}$.

The review bundle supplies a finite external-review surface for precisely that
artifact row.  Proposition~\ref{prop:review-bundle-case-snapshot-coverage}
identifies the Rule110 case manifest entry with four reviewable snapshots: the
status file, the collision-audit findings, the exhaustiveness-audit findings,
and the papers research report.  The current pass-record ledger names the two
gate records used by $a_6$: a \path{status} pass record whose input surface is
the Rule110 status file and paper-data reference, and a
\path{limitation-ledger} pass record whose input surface is the collision and
exhaustiveness audit ledgers.  These are the manifest and evaluator-facing fields
available to the current package, and they are exactly the concrete
representatives of $\mathcal M_{a_6}$.  They are finite strings and bundled
snapshots, so Proposition~\ref{prop:finite-certificate-schema-verifier} can check
them without interpreting generated prose, rerunning an evaluator, or assigning
semantics to Rule110 or GroundCompiler beyond the recorded manifest/evaluator
boundary.

No other artifact entry is present.  Therefore any stronger reading of
\path{pc6} or \path{pc11}---for example, that the Rule110 artifact suite was
freshly rerun, that GroundCompiler is semantically validated beyond the manifest
family, or that Lean supplies an unbounded semantic universe for the artifact
boundary---would require a further artifact certificate or command-run fields.
Those fields are absent, and the schema verifier rejects the stronger reading by
a finite missing-record obstruction.  The accepted finite-witness conclusion is
exactly the bounded $a_6$ artifact lesson.

It remains to prove the manifest-record invariance assertion.  Let $W$ and $W'$
be two possible review states, for example two submitted bundles or two local
working trees, that agree on the finite coordinates named in the statement: the
manifest entry, the four reviewable snapshots, the two pass-record rows for
$a_6$, and the boundary excluding fresh reruns.  The certificate constructor and
the schema verifier never query any other coordinate of a review state;
equivalently, these are exactly the artifact coordinates of the finite set $F$
from
Theorem~\ref{thm:finite-coordinate-determination-boundary}.  They therefore build the same tuple
\((K,\ell,E,H,R,D,B)\) for $W$ and for $W'$, and
Proposition~\ref{prop:finite-certificate-schema-verifier} returns the same
accepted bounded-artifact decision in both cases.  A dynamic semantic assertion
that could hold in $W$ and fail in $W'$ is not a function of this finite tuple.
It cannot be a consequence of the current publication certificate.  To make such
an assertion load-bearing, the package would have to add a new finite coordinate:
an artifact pass record for the asserted semantic property, or a command-run tuple
recording the relevant evaluator execution.  This proves manifest-record
invariance for the finite-witness manifest interface and shows that the theorem is
only a finite-record boundary result for row $a_6$ and the coordinate set
$\mathcal M_{a_6}$, not an artifact-validation
theorem.\end{proof}

\begin{corollary}[Artifact semantic-validity boundary for finite witnesses]
\label{cor:artifact-semantic-validity-boundary}
Let $c$ be a finite-witness artifact claim in the current package whose accepted
certificate uses the artifact row $a_6$, and let
$\mathcal M_{a_6}$ be the manifest-bounded coordinate set of
Theorem~\ref{thm:finite-witness-manifest-record-interface}.  If a proposed
stronger reading $c^{\mathrm{sem}}$ asserts semantic validity of the unbounded
Rule110/GroundCompiler artifact, semantic correctness of a dynamic evaluator, or
artifact validity beyond the exact evaluator contract recorded in
$\mathcal M_{a_6}$, then $c^{\mathrm{sem}}$ is not certified by the present
manuscript package.  It becomes a certifiable reading only after the package adds
one of the following finite coordinates:
\begin{enumerate}
\item an artifact pass record whose input surface is the exact evaluator
contract and whose boundary field states the semantic property being checked; or
\item a command-run tuple for the evaluator, including source state, environment,
command, exit code, and log path, together with the manifest row tying that run to
the submitted artifact.
\end{enumerate}
Consequently, the assertions in primary rows \path{pc5}, \path{pc6}, and
\path{pc11} that finite substrates are bounded by manifest rows, evaluator
outcomes, and substrate contracts are valid only as finite-record interface
assertions.  For the Rule110/GroundCompiler case they do not assert unbounded
semantic validity unless the exact evaluator contract and its assigned pass or
command-run record are present on the submitted support surface.
\end{corollary}

\begin{proof}
Theorem~\ref{thm:finite-witness-manifest-record-interface} identifies the complete
finite coordinate set available to row $a_6$: the manifest entry, local paths,
reviewable snapshots, boundary field, status pass row, and limitation-ledger pass
row.  Its proof also shows manifest-record invariance: two review states agreeing
on those coordinates produce the same artifact certificate even if they differ in
unrecorded dynamic or semantic facts.  Therefore semantic validity of an
unbounded Rule110/GroundCompiler artifact is not a function of the accepted tuple
unless the semantic evaluator contract itself appears as one of the submitted
finite coordinates.

Equivalently, compare the accepted artifact reading $c$ with the stronger reading
$c^{\mathrm{sem}}$ in the preorder of
Definition~\ref{def:stronger-reading-preorder}.  The stronger reading enlarges at
least one of the typed fields: it adds an artifact-semantic evidence surface, a
new pass record for the evaluator contract, or a command-run evidence level for a
fresh evaluator execution.  By Theorem~\ref{thm:record-acceptance-exactness}, an
accepted stronger reading must satisfy the same finite schema after that
enlargement.  If the package supplies neither an artifact pass record for the
semantic evaluator contract nor a command-run tuple with source state,
environment, command, exit code, and log path, then the enlarged certificate has
a finite bad subrecord: either the required evaluator pass row is absent from
$R$, or the command-run coordinate required at evidence level $\mathsf{command}$
is absent from $B$.  Theorem~\ref{thm:mixed-claim-projection-obstruction} then
prevents the status and limitation-ledger rows from certifying the omitted
semantic component.

For the current package, rows \path{pc5}, \path{pc6}, and \path{pc11} are tied to
the bounded artifact lesson $a_6$ and to the finite surfaces listed in
$\mathcal M_{a_6}$.  Those surfaces include manifest and evaluator-facing status
or limitation records, but the command-boundary inventory expressly lacks a fresh
Rule110/GroundCompiler semantic rerun or dynamic evaluator log.  Hence the only
certified conclusion is the finite-record one stated in the corollary.  Any
unbounded semantic-validity claim requires one of the additional finite
coordinates enumerated above, so it lies outside the current certificate.\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[Primary-claim conditional inventory-audit classification]
\label{prop:primary-claim-row-classification}
Assume the current primary source \path{submission_abstract.tex} is the submitted
primary artifact, the twelve-row inventory
\path{review_bundle/primary_claim_inventory.json} is supplied with the review
bundle or an accepted archive-equivalent support surface, and the finite manual
inventory audit has identified \path{pc1},\ldots,\path{pc12} as the load-bearing
primary assertions.  Then the current primary-claim inventory is complete for
the short presentation note in the following conditional inventory-audit sense.
Rows \path{pc1}, \path{pc8}, \path{pc9}, \path{pc10}, \path{pc11}, and
\path{pc12} are accepted certificate rows at the evidence level named in
Table~\ref{tab:primary-claim-inventory}.  Rows \path{pc2}, \path{pc3},
\path{pc4}, \path{pc5}, and \path{pc7} are bounded contextual interface rows.
Row \path{pc6} is accepted only through the bounded artifact certificate $a_6$.
Relative to that finite audit, there is no remaining non-classified or hidden
load-bearing primary assertion.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
The classification is a finite inspection of Table~\ref{tab:primary-claim-inventory}
and, under the bundle-availability hypothesis, the machine-readable inventory
\path{review_bundle/primary_claim_inventory.json}.
Rows \path{pc1}, \path{pc8}, and \path{pc9} are the theorem-interface rows: their
certificate surfaces are the finite-record interface theorem, Theorem~\ref{thm:publication-safety-interface},
Theorem~\ref{thm:current-package-certificate-exactness},
Proposition~\ref{prop:finite-certificate-schema-verifier},
Proposition~\ref{prop:machine-readable-certificate-records}, the acceptance
entries in Table~\ref{tab:current-acceptance-ledger}, and the current pass-record
ledger.  Rows \path{pc10} and \path{pc12} are submission-interface rows, certified
by Table~\ref{tab:submission-interface-map}, the primary--supplement interface map
Proposition~\ref{prop:primary-supplement-interface-map},
and the submission-artifact manifest fields.  Row \path{pc11} is the case-row
summary and is certified by Proposition~\ref{prop:review-bundle-case-snapshot-coverage}
and Table~\ref{tab:case-witnesses} at the path-verified snapshot level; its
fresh-rerun and semantic-validation boundaries are classified by
Theorem~\ref{thm:case-witness-skeleton-rigidity}.

Rows \path{pc2} and \path{pc7} do not reprove BEDC closure, the proof-obligation
system, implementation contracts, or the whole layer table as independent
mathematics; they are bounded imports of the pinned source-interface and are
certified by Lemma~\ref{lem:source-interface-extraction-correctness},
Propositions~\ref{prop:reviewable-source-snapshot-interface} and
\ref{prop:source-interface-record-file-consistency}, and
Table~\ref{tab:source-interface-record}.  Rows \path{pc3} and \path{pc4} are
classified by Proposition~\ref{prop:formal-source-gate-instantiation} and
Theorem~\ref{thm:formal-source-promotion-obstruction}: they are Lean-facing
verification-record context, not accepted formal-source build, axiom-purity, or
object-language closure claims.
Row \path{pc5} is classified by Theorem~\ref{thm:finite-witness-manifest-record-interface}
as finite-witness interface vocabulary.  Row \path{pc6} is the only concrete
Rule110/GroundCompiler boundary row and is accepted only through $a_6$, the
bounded artifact-status and limitation-ledger certificate.

The rows listed in the preceding two paragraphs are exactly
\path{pc1},\ldots,\path{pc12}.  Each row has one of the three displayed classes:
accepted certificate, bounded contextual interface, or accepted bounded artifact
certificate.  A row that attempted to carry more than its class permits would
need an additional acceptance entry and pass records under the dispatch function
\(\Gamma\).  Proposition~\ref{prop:current-package-acceptance-certificate-ledger}
states that no load-bearing claim outside the seven acceptance entries is accepted
by the current package.  Hence, under the finite manual audit and the supplied
inventory, the row classification is complete and no
contextual BEDC, Lean, finite-witness, compiler-layer, or review-bundle sentence
remains hidden as an uncertified load-bearing claim.\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[Primary-inventory freshness criterion]
\label{prop:primary-inventory-freshness-criterion}
Let $S$ be a later edited version of \path{submission_abstract.tex}, and let
$I$ be a primary-claim inventory with rows of the same form as
\path{review_bundle/primary_claim_inventory.json}: each row gives a location in
$S$, a claim text or bounded paraphrase, a row status, a certificate surface, and
an evidence level.  Suppose a manual claim audit of $S$ produces a finite list of
load-bearing assertions and verifies the following three conditions:
\begin{enumerate}
\item every load-bearing assertion in $S$ appears in exactly one row of $I$;
\item each row of $I$ points to a supplement theorem, table row, manifest row, or
bounded-context status that exists in the submitted package; and
\item for every row, the status and evidence level do not exceed what its cited
certificate surface proves under the finite record semantics of
the schema-relative finite-record exactness theorem, Theorem~\ref{thm:record-acceptance-exactness}.
\end{enumerate}
Then the row-classification conclusion of
Proposition~\ref{prop:primary-claim-row-classification} holds for the edited
primary artifact $S$ with $I$ in place of the current inventory.  Conversely, if
one of the three audit conditions fails, the edited primary artifact has a finite
review obstruction: an unclassified load-bearing assertion, a stale certificate
pointer, or an evidence-level overclaim.

For the present source state after the Stage--A theoremization round--2 edits on
2026-06-07, the current inventory is additionally witnessed at command-run freshness level by
\path{review_bundle/primary_claim_inventory_freshness_2026-06-07.log}.  The
recorded command is
\path{python review_bundle/verify_primary_claim_inventory.py}; the recorded
environment is Python 3.10.11 on Windows 10 in this paper directory; the recorded
exit code is 0; and the log records 161 primary-source lines, 12 inventory rows,
and 0 freshness errors.  The regenerated inventory rows are locked to the current
primary-source spans \path{pc1}:30--47, \path{pc2}:53--64,
\path{pc3}:66--69, \path{pc4}:68--69, \path{pc5}:70--72,
\path{pc6}:72--74, \path{pc7}:82--96, \path{pc8}:98--106,
\path{pc9}:108--117, \path{pc10}:119--132, \path{pc11}:134--154, and
\path{pc12}:143--154.  This command-run claim is limited to row-span and
certificate-field freshness for the primary inventory; it is not a full semantic
proof of every primary sentence.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
Assume the three audit conditions.  By condition~1, the audit list is a finite
partition of the load-bearing assertions of $S$ by inventory row: no assertion is
omitted and no assertion is assigned to two incompatible rows.  By condition~2,
each row has a concrete certificate surface inside the submitted package, so the
row is not supported by prose memory or by an absent local file.  By condition~3,
the certificate surface licenses exactly the row's declared status and evidence
level in the sense of the schema-relative finite-record exactness theorem, Theorem~\ref{thm:record-acceptance-exactness}.  Hence each
load-bearing assertion of $S$ is either an accepted certificate row, a bounded
contextual interface row, or an accepted bounded artifact row, according to the
status permitted by its cited certificate.  Since the finite audit list exhausts
all load-bearing assertions, there is no hidden primary assertion left to
classify.

For the converse, if condition~1 fails, some load-bearing assertion is missing
from the inventory or is assigned inconsistently, giving an unclassified-row
obstruction.  If condition~2 fails, a row points outside the submitted theorem,
table, manifest, or bounded-context surfaces, giving a stale-pointer obstruction.
If condition~3 fails, the row asserts a stronger status or evidence level than
its certificate permits; the schema-relative finite-record exactness theorem, Theorem~\ref{thm:record-acceptance-exactness}, supplies a
finite bad subrecord, and Theorem~\ref{thm:evidence-level-overclaim-obstruction}
classifies it as an evidence-level overclaim when the excess is a stronger
verification or command-run reading.  These are finite obstructions read from the
edited source, the inventory row, and the cited certificate surface.

For the current command-run instantiation, the cited freshness log is itself a
finite submitted record: it names the command class, environment, primary source,
inventory source, line count, row count, error count, and exit code.  An exit code
0 with error count 0 is exactly the success condition of the comparison command,
so the command-run freshness statement is licensed at the limited evidence level
declared in the proposition.\end{proof}

\begin{proof}
The primary artifact has four load-bearing interfaces.  First, its

maps this claim to the finite-record interface theorem, Theorem~\ref{thm:publication-safety-interface}, to the current
acceptance ledger proposition, to

Table~\ref{tab:current-acceptance-ledger}.  Those cited results prove finite

as recorded in the evidence-level column.

Second, the short paper says that the discovery-gate calculus is imported

Lemma~\ref{lem:source-interface-extraction-correctness},

Proposition~\ref{prop:source-interface-record-file-consistency}, and

Third, the short paper compresses the four case rows and states that they certify

proves the external-review surface for the local case paths and preserves the

Fourth, the short paper identifies the review bundle and long technical account

Proposition~\ref{audit:submission-artifact-role-separation}, to

\paragraph{Artifact roles.}
For the current presentation-only route, \path{submission_abstract.tex} is the
primary two-page CICM-style presentation source, \path{submission_abstract.pdf}
is its intended compiled form, \path{main.tex} is this long technical
supplement, and \path{review_bundle/} is the supplemental review directory.  The
submission package therefore has one primary artifact and one supplemental
artifact role: the short note states the interface, while this supplement and the
bundle provide the bounded source, case-path, and bibliography evidence.

As of the current 2026-06-07 dated local-readiness source state, this manuscript does not claim that
\path{review_bundle/} has been replaced by a DOI-bearing or institutional
archive.  The accepted support surface is therefore the exact directory
\path{review_bundle/} supplied with the submission.  A later archive replacement
is permitted only if it carries a final per-file SHA-256 digest manifest for the
certificate files below and satisfies Audit record~\ref{audit:review-bundle-archive-equivalence}.

\subsection{Review-bundle and archive-equivalence condition}

The archive-equivalence material in this subsection is deliberately bounded to
this package: it only decides when the finite review-bundle records cited above
are available to reviewers.  A general calculus of support-surface replacement,
archive locators, and digest-preserving mirrors is a split-candidate
infrastructure note rather than an additional theorem package for the present
CICM presentation route.

\begin{table}
\caption{Finite archive-equivalence criterion for replacing the review bundle.}
\label{tab:review-bundle-archive-equivalence}
\scriptsize
\begin{tabularx}{\linewidth}{p{0.25\linewidth}X p{0.25\linewidth}}
\toprule
Criterion & Required data in the archive replacement & Failure effect \\
\midrule
Manifest identity & a root record byte-identical to \path{review_bundle/REVIEW_BUNDLE_MANIFEST.json}, except for an added archive URL or DOI field that is not used by the verifier & replacement rejected \\
Bundle version & the identity tuple \path{(supplemental_review_bundle, 2026-06-01, 3fb3d6a0641767388a401883062aa522ea0b397b)} from the manifest fields \path{bundle_kind}, \path{created}, and \path{public_interface.public_source_commit} & replacement rejected \\
Finite file list & exactly the regular files named by the manifest, submission-interface map, source-snapshot record, case-snapshot rows, verifier-script fields, and verifier-log fields; generated cache files such as \path{__pycache__/} are excluded & replacement rejected \\
Content hashes & a SHA-256 digest for each file in that finite file list, equal to the digest of the corresponding file in the submitted \path{review_bundle/} at final packaging time & replacement rejected \\
Path semantics & every path in the archive preserves the same relative name below \path{review_bundle/} and no certificate row is retargeted to a different file & replacement rejected \\
\bottomrule
\end{tabularx}
\end{table}

\begin{auditrecord}[Review-bundle archive-equivalence criterion]
\label{audit:review-bundle-archive-equivalence}
An archival replacement for \path{review_bundle/} is accepted by this manuscript
only if it satisfies all rows of
Table~\ref{tab:review-bundle-archive-equivalence}.  Equivalently, it must have
the same manifest identity, the same bundle-version tuple, the same finite
certificate file list, the same SHA-256 content digests at final packaging time,
and the same relative path semantics as the submitted \path{review_bundle/}.
If no such hash-and-manifest archive is supplied, the only accepted support
surface is the exact \path{review_bundle/} directory uploaded with the
submission.
\medskip
\noindent\emph{Verification.} The manifest identity criterion fixes the root record that all bundle-dependent
claims cite: \path{review_bundle/REVIEW_BUNDLE_MANIFEST.json}.  Allowing only an
additional archive locator field prevents the archive from silently changing the
certificate schema, pass-record ledger, submission-interface map,
primary-claim inventory, verifier scripts, verifier logs, pinned-source
snapshot, case-evidence rows, or submission-artifact declaration.  The bundle
version is finite because the current manifest carries the three displayed
fields \path{bundle_kind}, \path{created}, and
\path{public_interface.public_source_commit}; equality of the tuple rules out
substituting a different support bundle or a different pinned source commit.

The finite file-list criterion is obtained by following only the manifest and
the machine-readable interface records already used in the proofs: certificate
schema, current pass records, primary-claim inventory, submission-interface map,
verifier scripts and logs, source snapshot, and the case snapshots named in the
case-evidence rows.  This traversal is finite because each record is a finite
JSON or manuscript table row and because generated interpreter caches are not
certificate surfaces.  The content-hash criterion then gives a byte-level test
for every file on that finite list.  If a file is omitted, added as a replacement
for a named certificate surface, or changed bytewise, either the file-list row or
the SHA-256 row fails.  The path-semantics row prevents a hash-correct file from
being moved under a new relative name that would make manuscript paths point to a
different object.

When all five rows pass, every path used by a bundle-dependent theorem,
proposition, table row, verifier record, or manifest row denotes the same bytes
under the same relative name and under the same root manifest identity as in the
submitted \path{review_bundle/}.  Thus the archive is equivalent for exactly the
finite certificate checks made in this paper.  When any row fails, a reviewer has
a finite bad subrecord: the changed manifest identity, changed version tuple,
file-list discrepancy, hash mismatch, or path-retargeting witness.  In that case
the archive is not accepted, and the support surface must be exact inclusion of
\path{review_bundle/} in the completed submission.
\end{auditrecord}
