records; the source-interface rows use the pinned source snapshot,
\path{submission_interface_map.json}, and the source-interface verifier log; and
the bundle role itself uses \path{README.md} and
\path{REVIEW_BUNDLE_MANIFEST.json}.  Thus including that directory as the final
supplemental surface supplies every regular certificate surface on which the
bundle-backed claims depend.

The directory listing may contain generated interpreter caches, but the table
criterion excludes such cache files and no theorem, proposition, table row, or
verifier record in this supplement cites them as evidence.  They therefore do
not enlarge the accepted support witness.  If the directory is not supplied,
relative paths such as \path{review_bundle/REVIEW_BUNDLE_MANIFEST.json} and
\path{review_bundle/case_snapshots/rule110_STATUS.md} no longer denote submitted
certificate files.  By Audit record~\ref{audit:review-bundle-archive-equivalence},
the only way to recover those denotations without the exact directory is an
archive with a locator, the same finite file list, matching SHA-256 digests, and
unchanged relative path semantics.  The source-level branch is exact inclusion of
the support tuple or a later archive-equivalent locator with matching final
digest rows; the public branch/path coordinate named here is only a candidate
locator until byte equality is recorded.  Whether the exact-upload branch has actually
been instantiated in a completed venue submission is not proved by this audit
record; it is the external record required by
Corollary~\ref{cor:upload-or-archive-instantiation-record}.
\end{auditrecord}

\begin{theorem}[Exact-inclusion support-surface realization]
\label{thm:exact-inclusion-support-surface-realization}
For the current package the selected support branch is exact supplemental inclusion of \path{review_bundle/}, but this branch remains conditional until an external upload-or-archive instantiation record exists.  If the completed submission contains that directory with the certificate-file digests listed in
\path{review_bundle/FINAL_DIGESTS_SHA256.md}, then every bundle-backed claim in
this supplement has a submitted finite support surface at its stated
path-verified evidence level.  If the exact directory is not submitted, then the
same support conclusion holds only after replacing it by an archive satisfying
the five tests of Table~\ref{tab:review-bundle-archive-equivalence}; no venue upload receipt or public DOI is claimed for the present package, and the public automath branch/path coordinate is only a candidate archive-equivalent locator unless a finite byte-equality record supplies matching digest rows.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
The exact-inclusion hypothesis supplies the directory named in every bundle-local
path and the final digest manifest named in
Audit record~\ref{prop:exact-review-bundle-upload-witness}.  By that proposition,
the manifest traverses the finite certificate surfaces used by the supplement:
the bundle manifest, schema, current pass-record ledger, source-interface map,
primary-claim inventory, verifier scripts, verifier logs, source snapshot, and
case snapshots.  Matching the listed SHA-256 digests fixes the bytes of each of
those certificate files at final packaging time, while preserving the relative
path names by which the manuscript refers to them.  Thus every bundle-backed
claim has the submitted finite evidence surface asserted for it, and the evidence
level remains path-verified because the argument checks file presence and digest
identity rather than rerunning Lean, daemons, or external artifacts.

If the exact directory is absent, the proof of
Audit record~\ref{audit:review-bundle-archive-equivalence} shows that the only
finite replacement accepted by this paper is an archive with the same manifest
identity, bundle-version tuple, finite certificate file list, per-file SHA-256
digests, and relative path semantics.  Those are exactly the five tests in
Table~\ref{tab:review-bundle-archive-equivalence}.  Without such an archive, at
least one bundle-local path or digest row has no submitted representative, so the
corresponding bundle-backed claim lacks its evidence surface.  The statement that
no public DOI or venue-upload receipt is claimed is the support-mode boundary in
Audit record~\ref{prop:exact-review-bundle-upload-witness}; the public branch/path
coordinate is an archive-equivalent locator only under the same byte, digest, and
path-semantics tests.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Public branch archive-equivalent locator criterion]
\label{thm:public-branch-archive-equivalent-realization}
Let $L$ be the public automath branch/path coordinate recorded in
\path{review_bundle/REVIEW_BUNDLE_MANIFEST.json} and in
\cite{auditable-pipeline-review-bundle}.  If $L$ contains
\path{theorem_inventory.json}, \path{theorem_inventory.md}, and the regular files
under \path{review_bundle/} with the same relative paths and the SHA--256 rows
printed in \path{review_bundle/FINAL_DIGESTS_SHA256.md}, and if $L$ is pinned by
commit or immutable release identifier, or else the branch head and
byte-equality record are refreshed together at the final support state, then $L$ is an
archive-equivalent support surface for every bundle-backed and theorem-inventory
claim in this supplement.  If any named file is absent at $L$, has a different
relative path, has a digest different from the final digest row, or lacks the
required immutability or same-state branch-head record, then $L$ does
not instantiate those claims, and the package falls back to exact supplemental
inclusion or to the local/path-verified reading.  The present source package
does not assert that the displayed public coordinate already satisfies these
byte-equality tests; it asserts only this finite criterion and the corresponding
conditional archive-equivalence branch.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
The locator $L$ is finite for the purposes of this paper because the accepted
support surface is not the whole repository: it is the displayed relative
package path, the two theorem-inventory files, the finite regular-file list below
\path{review_bundle/}, and the final digest table.  The bundle manifest and
bibliography entry name the same repository, branch, and package path, so a
reviewer can compare a candidate public surface against the finite table in
\path{review_bundle/FINAL_DIGESTS_SHA256.md}.

Assume first that all listed files are present at $L$ with the displayed relative
paths and digests, and that $L$ is pinned by an immutable identifier or by a
same-state branch-head byte-equality record.  Then every manuscript reference to a theorem-inventory or
bundle-local certificate file denotes the same byte string at $L$ as in the exact
support tuple, with no later branch movement hidden inside the locator.  The manifest identity, file-list, content-hash, and path-semantics
requirements of Audit record~\ref{audit:review-bundle-archive-equivalence} are
therefore satisfied, while Theorem~\ref{thm:exact-inclusion-support-surface-realization}
proves that those same bytes are sufficient for the stated path-verified evidence
level.  Thus $L$ realizes the archive-equivalent branch of
Corollary~\ref{cor:upload-or-archive-instantiation-record}.

Conversely, suppose a named file is missing, moved, digest-mismatched, or not tied
to the same final support state by an immutable identifier or refreshed
branch-head record.  Then at least one of the finite tests in
Audit record~\ref{audit:review-bundle-archive-equivalence} fails: the file-list
row fails for absence, the path-semantics row fails for a move, or the hash row
fails for byte mismatch or mutable-state ambiguity.  The failed row is a finite bad subrecord, so
Theorem~\ref{thm:support-surface-normal-form} rejects the archive-equivalent
reading.  No DOI, venue-upload receipt, Lean rebuild, daemon execution, or
artifact-validation fact follows from the locator comparison, so the only
remaining readings are exact supplemental inclusion when supplied, or the local
path-verified reading already classified above.

Finally, the current package contains no finite byte-equality record witnessing
that the public coordinate has passed the displayed comparison at a pinned commit
or refreshed branch head.  By
Theorem~\ref{thm:finite-coordinate-determination-boundary}, such a witness cannot
be inferred from the local manifest, bibliography entry, or branch/path name
alone.  Hence this theorem is an archive-equivalence criterion rather than a
claim that an external archive has already been instantiated.
\end{proof}

\begin{corollary}[Submission-time support instantiation test]
\label{cor:submission-time-support-instantiation-test}
For a completed presentation-only submission, the bundle-backed and
venue/bibliography-backed claims in this package instantiate external support if
and only if the submitted support surface contains one of the following finite
normal forms:
\begin{enumerate}
\item exact inclusion of \path{theorem_inventory.json},
  \path{theorem_inventory.md}, and \path{review_bundle/}, with
  \path{review_bundle/FINAL_DIGESTS_SHA256.md} regenerated after the inventory,
  extractor, verifier, primary-claim-freshness, and venue/bibliography records
  used by the submission are final;
\item an archive-equivalent replacement carrying the same relative file list,
  path semantics, manifest rows, SHA--256 digests, and upload-or-archive locator;
  or
\item a later replacement support surface with the same fields and a later
  venue/bibliography verification record run immediately before submission.
\end{enumerate}
Without one of these finite records, the same claims remain only local or dated
path-verified observations and do not certify external bundle availability,
upload-time venue compliance, bibliography freshness, DOI availability, archive
availability, Lean/BEDC rebuilds, or dynamic artifact validation.
\end{corollary}

\begin{proof}
Theorem~\ref{thm:exact-inclusion-support-surface-realization} proves the exact
inclusion branch: submitted byte identity for the selected support directory and
its digest manifest gives the finite evidence surface for bundle-backed claims.
The same theorem, through Table~\ref{tab:review-bundle-archive-equivalence},
proves the archive branch, because the archive tests preserve the finite file
list, relative paths, manifest identity, and per-file SHA--256 rows used by the
bundle-local references.  The replacement branch is
Theorem~\ref{thm:workspace-final-package-reconciliation} applied with a new
commit--manifest certificate, together with the freshness condition of
Proposition~\ref{audit:fresh-venue-record-criterion} for venue and bibliography
claims.

It remains only to justify the ``only if'' direction.  By
Theorem~\ref{thm:support-surface-normal-form}, every accepted external support
reading is either the selected directory, an archive-equivalent representative,
or a deliberately promoted replacement state.  The post-inventory ordering is
the fixed-point requirement in
Theorem~\ref{thm:post-inventory-package-fixed-point}: if the digest precedes the
inventory bytes or the post-inventory logs, at least one submitted digest row is
not a digest of the byte string used by the claim.  Venue and bibliography pages
are time-sensitive, so Proposition~\ref{audit:fresh-venue-record-criterion}
allows a current compliance reading only on the checked rule state or after a
later record with the same fields.  If none of the three displayed normal forms
is present, Theorem~\ref{thm:finite-coordinate-determination-boundary} forbids
inserting the missing locator, digest, freshness, or command fields by prose.
The final sentence is exactly the non-escalation boundary established in
Theorem~\ref{thm:submission-support-fixed-point-normal-form}.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Final support-surface closure]
\label{thm:final-support-surface-closure}
For the current package, using
Proposition~\ref{prop:final-package-shape-convention},
Audit record~\ref{prop:exact-review-bundle-upload-witness}, and
Theorem~\ref{thm:exact-inclusion-support-surface-realization}, the bundle-backed
claims in this supplement are admissible at the stated path-verified evidence
level exactly when the submitted supplemental material contains the exact
directory \path{review_bundle/} whose final packaging record is
\path{review_bundle/FINAL_DIGESTS_SHA256.md}.  The exact directory branch also
requires that any post-generation change to a named bundle file be followed by
refreshed SHA-256 rows in that final digest record and by rerunning the finite
verifiers whose records cite the changed coordinate.  A replacement archive is not a
current package branch; it would have to satisfy
Table~\ref{tab:review-bundle-archive-equivalence} and provide a locator before a
future package could select it.  If the exact directory is not present in the
current package submission, the bundle-backed claims are not part of the accepted
support surface, although the short presentation artifact may still cite the
pinned public \path{newmath} repository for the imported source spine.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
Let $B$ denote the set of bundle-dependent claims in this supplement.  By the
definition of \path{review_bundle/} in the problem statement and by the bundle
rows of Table~\ref{tab:review-bundle-archive-equivalence}, every claim in $B$
uses at least one named certificate surface under \path{review_bundle/}: the
manifest, certificate schema, pass-record ledger, source-interface map,
primary-claim inventory, verifier scripts or logs, the pinned source snapshot,
or one of the case snapshots.  Therefore a submitted support surface for $B$
must either contain those named files with their relative path semantics or
replace them by an archive that is finite-file-list and hash equivalent.

If the exact directory \path{review_bundle/} is uploaded, then
Audit record~\ref{prop:exact-review-bundle-upload-witness} identifies it as the
current concrete support witness and all named bundle paths are present in the submitted supplemental boundary.  The file
\path{review_bundle/FINAL_DIGESTS_SHA256.md} records the final SHA-256 digest
surface for those certificate files, so the byte identity of the supplied
directory is checkable without changing the role of the directory into an
archive claim.  If a named bundle file is regenerated after that digest surface
is written, the byte-coordinate selected by the support relation changes; by
Proposition~\ref{prop:regenerated-theorem-inventory-digest-closure} and the same
finite-coordinate argument, the corresponding digest row and verifier logs must
be refreshed before the regenerated file can be the submitted support witness.
Hence all claims in $B$ have the path-verified support surface asserted for this
package exactly in the exact-upload-with-current-digests branch.

The archive criterion remains a finite non-current rejection test.  If a later
package attempted to replace the directory by an archive, Audit
record~\ref{audit:review-bundle-archive-equivalence} would require equality of
the manifest identity, bundle-version tuple, finite certificate file list,
per-file SHA-256 digests, and relative path semantics.  The current package does
not contain the locator or archive digest table needed to enter that branch, so
the theorem cannot treat an archive as an accepted present support surface.

Conversely, suppose the exact directory is not submitted for the current package.
Then at least one named bundle certificate surface is outside the submitted
artifact boundary and has no selected finite replacement.  The finite certificate
schema used in Theorem~\ref{thm:current-package-certificate-exactness}
accepts only submitted paths, explicit historical path records, and the stated
supplemental surfaces.  Hence the missing bundle surface cannot support the
corresponding claim in $B$.  The public \path{newmath} repository citation is a
separate pinned-source citation, so it remains available for the short
architectural note but does not reconstitute the omitted bundle-backed
certificate records.  This proves both admissible cases and the failure of all
other cases.
\end{proof}

\begin{auditrecord}[Final immutable package-state certificate]
\label{prop:final-immutable-package-state-certificate}
This audit record retains the legacy cross-reference label
\path{prop:final-immutable-package-state-certificate} for compatibility with
earlier acceptance-ledger entries; its environment category is \path{auditrecord},
not \path{proposition}.
For the current presentation package, the final source-state record for the three
finite verifier runs is the pair consisting of the source state printed in each
refreshed log and the digest manifest
\path{review_bundle/FINAL_DIGESTS_SHA256.md}; the venue/bibliography
live-check run is a historical 2026-06-07 route-readiness record carrying the same
digest manifest as its package-state boundary.  The selected supplemental support
mode is conditional exact inclusion of \path{review_bundle/}; no DOI-bearing,
institutional, or other archival replacement is claimed.  Consequently a
command-result claim for any of the four logs
\path{review_bundle/certificate_verification_run.log},
\path{review_bundle/source_interface_verification_run.log}, or
\path{review_bundle/primary_claim_inventory_freshness_2026-06-07.log}, and
\path{review_bundle/VENUE_BIBLIOGRAPHY_LIVE_CHECK_2026-06-07.log} is accepted
only when read against the declared package-state boundary and the exact included
bundle once the external instantiation record exists.  If either the digest
manifest is absent or the exact bundle is not submitted and no archive satisfies
Audit record~\ref{audit:review-bundle-archive-equivalence}, the corresponding
command-result and bundle-backed support claims are outside the accepted support
surface of this package.
\medskip
\noindent\emph{Verification.} The three verifier logs and the venue/bibliography live-check log are finite
files in the supplemental bundle, and their commands are the only fresh
command-run surfaces listed in
Table~\ref{tab:command-boundary-inventory}.  Each log records its command,
environment, exit code, and log path.  After the final packaging pass the
verifier logs record the source state as the displayed automath commit together
with \path{review_bundle/FINAL_DIGESTS_SHA256.md}; the live-check log records the
dated 2026-06-07 local-date route and bibliography query tuple.  That digest manifest
records the selected support mode, namely conditional exact supplemental
inclusion of \path{review_bundle/}, and gives SHA-256 digests for the finite
certificate surfaces of the package, including all four command-run logs.  Thus
the byte identity of the files carrying
the command-result claims is not determined by a mutable phrase such as a local
working tree; it is determined by the finite commit--manifest certificate.

By Theorem~\ref{thm:final-support-surface-closure}, bundle-backed claims are
admissible only in the exact-bundle case or in the archive-equivalent case of
Table~\ref{tab:review-bundle-archive-equivalence}.  The final digest manifest
selects the first case and states that no archive replacement is claimed; it is
not itself a venue-upload receipt.  Hence the accepted support surface for this
submission is precisely the exact \path{review_bundle/} inclusion when that inclusion
is actually supplied, with the digest manifest serving as its byte-level
final-packaging record.  If that manifest is absent, the finite source-state
component attached to the logs is missing.  If the bundle is absent and no
archive-equivalent replacement is supplied, Theorem~\ref{thm:final-support-surface-closure}
removes the bundle-backed claims from the accepted support surface.  These are
exactly the failure cases stated in the audit record.
\end{auditrecord}

\begin{theorem}[Workspace-to-final-package reconciliation]
\label{thm:workspace-final-package-reconciliation}
Let $D_0$ be the digest manifest
\path{review_bundle/FINAL_DIGESTS_SHA256.md}, and let $H_i$ denote the source
state printed in verifier log $i$.  The source-state claim made in Audit
record~\ref{prop:final-immutable-package-state-certificate} is the finite package
claim consisting of these recorded pairs $(H_i,D_0)$, not a claim that every
later reviewer checkout, editor workspace, or post-packaging working tree has Git
\path{HEAD} equal to a single historical commit.  If a current workspace reports
a different Git \path{HEAD} from one of the historical rows, then the verifier-log and
bundle-backed claims of this supplement remain admissible exactly in the
following two reconciled cases:
\begin{enumerate}
\item the submitted support surface is exact inclusion of \path{review_bundle/}, or
an archive equivalent to it, whose finite certificate files match the SHA-256
rows of $D_0$; or
\item the package is deliberately advanced to the current workspace state by
rerunning the three verifier commands and replacing the displayed source-state
record, the verifier logs, and the digest manifest by new matching
source-state--manifest certificates.
\end{enumerate}
In all other cases a mismatch between a later workspace \path{HEAD} and a
recorded historical row does not certify a new package state and does not
invalidate the historical finite package claim $(H_i,D_0)$; it only shows that
the later workspace is
outside the submitted support surface.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
The accepted command-result coordinates are finite.  By
Audit record~\ref{prop:final-immutable-package-state-certificate}, the three log
paths, the recorded source-state fields $H_i$, and the manifest $D_0$ form the
source-state record for the verifier runs.  By
Theorem~\ref{thm:final-support-surface-closure}, those log claims are part of the
accepted support surface only when the exact bundle is submitted or when an
archive supplies the same finite file list, digests, and relative path semantics.
Thus case (1) fixes the bytes of the certificate files directly: the reviewer's
ambient checkout may have any Git \path{HEAD}, but the submitted certificate
surface is still the byte-identified finite package $(H_i,D_0)$.

Suppose instead that the authors want the later workspace itself to be the
package state.  Then at least one source-state coordinate has changed, namely
the Git \path{HEAD} coordinate.  The verification rule in the scope contract
requires every command-result claim to record its exact command, source commit,
environment, exit code, and log path.  Therefore a new workspace state cannot be
accepted by merely observing the new \path{HEAD}; the three verifier runs must be
rerun against that state, and their logs and digest manifest must be replaced by
consistent source-state--manifest records.  This is case (2).

If neither case holds, the later workspace contributes no finite coordinate to
the submitted certificate.  It is not named in $D_0$, it is not an
archive-equivalent representative under Table~\ref{tab:review-bundle-archive-equivalence},
and it is not accompanied by fresh verifier logs satisfying the command-result
record rule.  The finite-coordinate determination boundary of
Theorem~\ref{thm:finite-coordinate-determination-boundary} then forbids silently
using that workspace either to certify a new package or to refute the old
byte-identified package.  The only certified statement left is the historical
finite package claim $(H_i,D_0)$, conditional on the exact support-surface branch
already proved above.\end{proof}

\begin{auditrecord}[Mutable-checkout observations are non-certifying]
\label{prop:mutable-checkout-observation-boundary}
This audit record retains the legacy cross-reference label
\path{prop:mutable-checkout-observation-boundary} for compatibility with earlier
acceptance-ledger entries; its environment category is \path{auditrecord}, not
\path{proposition}.
Let $W$ be a review-time working tree whose Git \path{HEAD} is not one of the
source-state rows recorded in the verifier logs named in
Theorem~\ref{thm:workspace-final-package-reconciliation}.  The observation of $W$
is not a certificate coordinate for any verifier-log claim in this package unless
$W$ is promoted by the second branch of that theorem, namely by rerunning the
three verifier commands and replacing the source-state, log, and digest records
by new matching finite package certificates.  Consequently, a reviewer who sees
$W$ with a different \path{HEAD} has exactly one source-level reconciliation
test for the existing package: inspect the exact-bundle or archive-equivalent
surface whose certificate-file bytes match \path{review_bundle/FINAL_DIGESTS_SHA256.md}.
\medskip
\noindent\emph{Verification.} The finite package certificate records three kinds of coordinates relevant here:
the logged source-state rows, the verifier logs, and the per-file digest manifest.  A
bare working-tree observation supplies none of the last two coordinates and, when
its \path{HEAD} differs from a recorded row, supplies a different first coordinate.  By the
command-result rule used in
Audit record~\ref{prop:final-immutable-package-state-certificate}, changing a
source-state coordinate requires fresh logs with their command, environment, exit
code, and log path; this is precisely the promotion branch in
Theorem~\ref{thm:workspace-final-package-reconciliation}.  If that branch is not
performed, the observation of $W$ remains outside the finite package.  The only
remaining reconciliation route is therefore the branch that checks the already
selected support surface: the exact included \path{review_bundle/} directory, or
an archive equivalent under Table~\ref{tab:review-bundle-archive-equivalence},
with certificate bytes fixed by the final digest manifest.
\end{auditrecord}

\begin{theorem}[Support-surface normal form for bundle-backed claims]
\label{thm:support-surface-normal-form}
For the current presentation package, every accepted use of a bundle-backed
certificate coordinate is in exactly one of the following normal forms.
\begin{enumerate}
\item \emph{Exact inclusion}: an external upload or submission receipt records
that the submitted support surface contains the exact \path{review_bundle/}
directory whose certificate-file bytes match
\path{review_bundle/FINAL_DIGESTS_SHA256.md}.
\item \emph{Archive-equivalent inclusion}: a replacement archive satisfies every
row of Table~\ref{tab:review-bundle-archive-equivalence}, including manifest
identity, bundle-version identity, finite file-list identity, SHA-256 content
identity, and relative path semantics.
\item \emph{Promoted rerun}: the package is advanced to a new source state by
rerunning the verifier commands and replacing the displayed source-state record,
verifier logs, and digest manifest by one common finite commit--manifest
certificate.
\end{enumerate}
If none of these three normal forms is present, the attempted bundle-backed
reading has a finite obstruction subrecord: either the external exact-inclusion
receipt is absent, the archive-equivalence table has a failed row, or the rerun
branch lacks the command, source-state, environment, exit-code, log-path, and
digest coordinates required by the command-boundary rule.  Thus the late
support-surface chain is a finite audit-interface classification for this
package, not a theorem about general archive infrastructure, venue upload, or
semantic correctness of the bundled artifacts.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
The candidate support surface for a bundle-backed coordinate can differ from the
source-level package in only three record-visible ways.  First, it can supply an
external receipt for the same directory selected by
Proposition~\ref{prop:final-package-shape-convention}.
Theorem~\ref{thm:exact-inclusion-support-surface-realization} and
Theorem~\ref{thm:final-support-surface-closure} prove that this exact directory,
with the final digest manifest, is the local byte surface that must be supplied;
the additional receipt is what turns that local selection into submitted support.
This is normal form~(1).

Second, the candidate can replace the directory by an archive.  The manuscript
does not accept arbitrary archive availability as a certificate coordinate:
Audit record~\ref{audit:review-bundle-archive-equivalence} gives the finite
hash-and-manifest test, and every failed row of
Table~\ref{tab:review-bundle-archive-equivalence} is a finite bad subrecord.  If
all rows pass, the replacement denotes the same certificate bytes under the same
relative paths and is normal form~(2); if any row fails, the archive cannot carry
the bundle-backed claim.

Third, the candidate can point to a later or different workspace state.
Theorem~\ref{thm:workspace-final-package-reconciliation} proves that such a state
is not the old finite package unless it is deliberately promoted by fresh verifier
runs and by a replacement commit--manifest certificate.
Audit record~\ref{prop:mutable-checkout-observation-boundary} then shows that a
bare mutable-checkout observation is non-certifying.  Therefore a different
workspace is accepted only in normal form~(3), and otherwise has the finite
command-boundary obstruction stated in the theorem.

The three cases are mutually exclusive as record modes for a fixed submitted
claim: the first uses the selected directory, the second uses a byte-identical
archive representative, and the third replaces the package state and digest
record by a new common certificate.  If a proposed support reading is in none of
the modes, it contributes no coordinate read by the verifier.  By
Theorem~\ref{thm:finite-coordinate-determination-boundary} and the obstruction
skeleton of Theorem~\ref{thm:finite-certificate-obstruction-skeleton}, the missing
coordinate is an inclusion-minimal finite bad subrecord rather than a semantic
fact about the underlying artifacts.\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Support-surface invariant compression]
\label{thm:support-surface-invariant-compression}
Let $\mathcal S$ be the family of support-surface and post-inventory claims in
Theorems~\ref{thm:final-support-surface-closure},
\ref{thm:workspace-final-package-reconciliation},
\ref{thm:support-surface-normal-form}, and
\ref{thm:post-inventory-package-fixed-point}, together with
Corollary~\ref{cor:stable-replay-post-inventory-tuple}.  For the current package,
every member of $\mathcal S$ is a projection of the same finite support tuple
\[
\Sigma=(F,M,D,R,Q),
\]
where $F$ is the selected relative file list, $M$ is the review-bundle manifest,
$D$ is the final digest table, $R$ is the retained extractor and verifier log
family, and $Q$ is the post-inventory ordering relation saying that the inventory
bytes precede the retained log bytes and that the digest table is regenerated
after them.  More precisely, each theorem in $\mathcal S$ is obtained from
$\Sigma$ by forgetting all but one of the following projections:
\begin{enumerate}
\item byte closure of the selected support surface $(F,M,D)$;
\item reconciliation of a later workspace through replacement logs $(R,D)$;
\item the three accepted external-support modes of
Theorem~\ref{thm:support-surface-normal-form}; or
\item the post-inventory replay order $(Q,R,D)$.
\end{enumerate}
Hence a presentation version may state this family as one finite-record support
invariant and cite the inventory file, theorem-environment extractor log, finite
record-verifier logs, and final digest table for full label coverage.  Such a
compression loses no mathematical content for the CICM presentation route,
because none of the separated labels in $\mathcal S$ carries an independent
semantic claim beyond these finite projections.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
Each cited result reads only the named finite files, manifests, logs, digests, and
their ordering.  The final support-surface closure theorem reads the selected
file list, manifest rows, and digest rows, so it is the projection $(F,M,D)$.
The workspace reconciliation theorem compares later workspace observations with
the retained source-state and verifier-log records; it therefore reads only the
log family and digest certificate, namely $(R,D)$, together with the rule that a
replacement workspace must supply fresh records of the same form.  The support
surface normal-form theorem classifies the same selected bytes into exact
inclusion, archive-equivalent inclusion, or promoted rerun; this is a case split
on the external-support projection of $(F,M,D,R)$.  The post-inventory fixed
point and its stable replay corollary add no new support object; they add the
ordering projection $Q$ saying which inventory, extractor, verifier, and digest
bytes were produced after which earlier bytes.

Because these projections are all functions of the one tuple $\Sigma$, a reader
who is given $\Sigma$ can reconstruct the truth value and bad-subrecord witness
of every label in $\mathcal S$ by applying the corresponding projection and then
the already proved finite-coordinate determination theorem,
Theorem~\ref{thm:finite-coordinate-determination-boundary}.  Conversely, any
accepted support claim among the cited labels must pass one of the displayed
projection checks: otherwise the normal-form theorem or the post-inventory fixed
point supplies the missing finite coordinate as a bad subrecord.  Thus the labels
are useful audit bookkeeping but not independent semantic theorems about Lean,
artifact behavior, archive infrastructure, venue upload, or journal acceptance.
For a presentation article, replacing the separated labels by the single tuple
statement and pointing to \path{theorem_inventory.json},
\path{review_bundle/theorem_environment_extraction_run.log}, the retained finite
record-verifier logs, and \path{review_bundle/FINAL_DIGESTS_SHA256.md} preserves
exactly the same finite-record content.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Upload instantiation boundary]
\label{thm:upload-or-archive-instantiation-boundary}
Before the bundle-backed claims of this supplement are treated as part of a
completed venue submission, the external submission record must instantiate one
support-surface branch selected by
Proposition~\ref{prop:final-package-shape-convention} and
Theorems~\ref{thm:final-support-surface-closure} and
\ref{thm:support-surface-normal-form}.  Either it must record that
\path{review_bundle/} was uploaded exactly with digest manifest $D_0$ and with
the root \path{theorem_inventory.json} coordinate named by the manifest and
ledger, or it must record an archive-equivalent locator satisfying
Theorem~\ref{thm:public-branch-archive-equivalent-realization}.  The TeX source
and the local digest manifest select those admissible branches but do not
constitute a venue upload receipt or a public byte-equality locator.  In the
present source package no exact-upload receipt and no final-state public
byte-equality locator is recorded; consequently all bundle-backed claims are
proved only as conditional source-level support claims until one of the two
external instantiation records is supplied.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
Proposition~\ref{prop:final-package-shape-convention} and
Theorem~\ref{thm:final-support-surface-closure} prove that the bundle-backed
claims for this package are admissible in either the exact-directory branch or
the public branch/path archive-equivalent branch, subject in both cases to the
same final digest rows.  Audit record~\ref{prop:exact-review-bundle-upload-witness}
and Audit record~\ref{prop:final-immutable-package-state-certificate} give the
same support tuple and explicitly exclude a DOI, venue-upload receipt, and
final-state public byte-equality record.  Those source records determine what
must be submitted or publicly located, but they are produced before the venue's
upload action.  Hence a completed submission needs one additional external
instantiation record: either the venue or packager records the exact supplemental
directory upload with the final digest manifest, or the public branch/path
coordinate is checked at a pinned commit or refreshed branch head and contains
the matching digest-identified tuple.
Without one of those records, the statements proved above remain conditional
source-level support claims rather than a proof that an external support action
has occurred.  Therefore
treating the bundle-backed claims as externally instantiated would add a
coordinate not present in the finite support surface, contradicting the finite
coordinate determination boundary of
Theorem~\ref{thm:finite-coordinate-determination-boundary}.\end{proof}

\begin{corollary}[Upload-or-archive instantiation record]
\label{cor:upload-or-archive-instantiation-record}
The bundle-backed support claims of the current package may be used only under
the external-record condition of
Theorem~\ref{thm:upload-or-archive-instantiation-boundary}.  In particular,
\path{review_bundle/FINAL_DIGESTS_SHA256.md} is a local byte-level packaging
manifest and not, by itself, an upload receipt, DOI, or proof that the public
branch/path locator already contains the matching bytes.  Thus the current
package has a conditional local-support reading unless a later exact-upload
receipt or archive-equivalent byte-equality locator is added to the finite
support surface.
\end{corollary}

\begin{proof}
This is the record-level specialization of
Theorem~\ref{thm:upload-or-archive-instantiation-boundary} to the current digest
manifest.  The added sentence is the contrapositive of the same boundary: when
neither admissible external record is present, the finite support tuple lacks the
coordinate that would certify completed upload or archive-equivalent byte
equality, and Theorem~\ref{thm:finite-coordinate-determination-boundary} forbids
supplying that coordinate by prose.
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[No external instantiation from local bundle records]
\label{prop:no-external-instantiation-from-local-bundle-records}
In the current source package, the rows of
\path{review_bundle/FINAL_DIGESTS_SHA256.md}, the manifest fields of
\path{review_bundle/REVIEW_BUNDLE_MANIFEST.json}, the bibliography entries
\cite{automath-publication-pipeline,auditable-pipeline-review-bundle}, and the
support notes in this supplement instantiate only a local exact-inclusion
support mode and name the public branch/path archive-equivalent coordinate.
They do not by themselves instantiate either admissible external-support branch
of Corollary~\ref{cor:upload-or-archive-instantiation-record}.  Consequently any
claim depending on a file under \path{review_bundle/} or on the root theorem
ledger \path{theorem_inventory.json} remains a conditional local package-support
claim unless a finite update supplies an exact venue-upload receipt or the public
automath branch/path archive-equivalent locator with matching bytes recorded in
the manuscript, manifest, bibliography, and digest surfaces.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
The admissible external-support branches are exactly the two branches isolated by
Theorem~\ref{thm:upload-or-archive-instantiation-boundary} and
Corollary~\ref{cor:upload-or-archive-instantiation-record}: an exact uploaded
\path{review_bundle/} directory with matching final digests, or an
archive-equivalent replacement satisfying the same manifest, file-list, digest,
and relative-path tests.  The present package supplies the local byte manifest
\path{review_bundle/FINAL_DIGESTS_SHA256.md} and manifest text selecting exact
supplemental inclusion, but these are source-package coordinates.  By the same
corollary, a local byte manifest is not an upload receipt, DOI, public archive
identifier, or archive locator.  The bibliography entries cited in the statement
assert no such upload receipt or DOI.  The archive-equivalent branch is
instantiated only when the public automath branch/path coordinate contains bytes
matching the final digest rows.  Hence the local source package alone does not
instantiate either external branch.

If a bundle-backed claim were nevertheless read as externally supported, its
finite support tuple would contain an upload-or-archive coordinate absent from
the local package.  This is exactly the obstruction excluded by
Theorem~\ref{thm:finite-coordinate-determination-boundary}.  Therefore the only
current reading is conditional local package support.  The final sentence is the
contrapositive update condition: leaving this normal form requires adding one of
the missing external coordinates to the common manuscript, manifest,
bibliography, and digest surfaces so that all finite records name the same
submitted support surface.
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[Upload-or-archive instantiation update protocol]
\label{prop:upload-or-archive-instantiation-update-protocol}
Suppose a later venue upload receipt or archive-equivalent locator is to be used
to replace the current no-receipt boundary of
Corollary~\ref{cor:upload-or-archive-instantiation-record} by an externally
instantiated submitted-support claim.  Then the replacement is accepted by the
finite-record interface if and only if one of the two following finite update
protocols is performed.  The manuscript surfaces in the statement are precisely
\path{submission_abstract.tex} and \path{main.tex}; the manifest surface is
\path{review_bundle/REVIEW_BUNDLE_MANIFEST.json}; the bibliography surface is
\path{references.bib}; and the digest surface is
\path{review_bundle/FINAL_DIGESTS_SHA256.md} or a replacing digest manifest named
by both manuscript sources and by the manifest.
\begin{enumerate}
\item \emph{Exact-upload update.}  \path{submission_abstract.tex} and
\path{main.tex} record the receipt or venue
submission identifier for the exact support surface selected in
Proposition~\ref{prop:final-package-shape-convention}; the manifest field
\path{public_interface.distribution_mode} is changed from conditional exact
supplemental upload to completed exact supplemental upload; the manifest field
\path{public_interface.preferred_submission_form} or an adjacent receipt field
names the uploaded \path{submission_abstract.pdf}, \path{main.pdf} or
\path{main.tex}, root \path{theorem_inventory.json}, and \path{review_bundle/};
\path{review_bundle/FINAL_DIGESTS_SHA256.md} still contains matching digest rows
for the submitted bundle files; and the \path{references.bib} note for
\path{auditable-pipeline-review-bundle} is changed from no completed upload to the
same receipt-bound support statement.
\item \emph{Archive-equivalent update.}  \path{submission_abstract.tex} and
\path{main.tex} record the archive
locator; the manifest field \path{public_interface.current_public_archive} and the
\path{references.bib} note for \path{auditable-pipeline-review-bundle} name that same
locator; the manifest keeps or replaces \path{public_interface.archival_update_rule}
with the byte-equivalence rule actually satisfied; and
\path{review_bundle/FINAL_DIGESTS_SHA256.md}, or a replacing digest manifest named
by both the manuscript and the manifest, records the file list and SHA--256 rows
against which the archive is checked, including the coordinates for
\path{review_bundle/}, \path{theorem_inventory.json}, and the manifest itself.
\end{enumerate}
In either protocol, if any digest row, manifest public-interface field,
manuscript support statement, bibliography note, or receipt/locator coordinate is
changed, all four surfaces must name the same support mode and the same finite
receipt-or-locator record.  Without this finite common update, the current
no-receipt normal form of
Proposition~\ref{prop:submission-support-instantiation-field-normal-form} remains
the only accepted submitted-support status.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
The current package has only the source-level exact-inclusion branch by
Proposition~\ref{prop:final-package-shape-convention} and
Theorem~\ref{thm:upload-or-archive-instantiation-boundary}.  Thus an external
instantiation can add exactly one new positive coordinate: either a receipt for
that exact upload or a locator for an archive-equivalent representative.  The
finite-coordinate determination boundary,
Theorem~\ref{thm:finite-coordinate-determination-boundary}, forbids the verifier
from reading any coordinate not present on the submitted support surface.  Hence
the new coordinate must be visible wherever the package declares public support
status: in the manuscript statement that changes the boundary, in the manifest
public-interface fields that describe distribution mode and archive status, in
the digest manifest that identifies the bytes being supplied, and in the
bibliography entry that tells a reviewer whether the bundle is an external
support surface or merely a local note.

For the exact-upload branch, the receipt is meaningful for this package only if
it refers to the support surface selected by
Proposition~\ref{prop:final-package-shape-convention}: the primary presentation
artifact, the technical supplement, the root theorem inventory, and the exact
\path{review_bundle/} directory with matching final digests.  Updating the
manuscript, the manifest distribution fields, the digest record, and the
bibliography note with the same receipt is therefore sufficient for the finite
record checker to read the external-upload coordinate; if one of those records is
missing or names a different support mode, the checker sees a bad subrecord of
the kind classified by Theorem~\ref{thm:support-surface-normal-form}.

For the archive-equivalent branch, Table~\ref{tab:review-bundle-archive-equivalence}
and Theorem~\ref{thm:support-surface-normal-form} require the archive to preserve
the same file list, digest rows, manifest semantics, and relative-path meanings.
The stated archive update records exactly those finite data: the common locator,
the byte-equivalence rule, and the digest manifest identifying the archive bytes.
Again, if the manuscript, manifest, bibliography, and digest surface do not name a
single common locator and byte-equivalence certificate, the proposed archive is
not a finite coordinate accepted by the verifier.

The two branches are exhaustive because
Corollary~\ref{cor:upload-or-archive-instantiation-record} allows only an exact
upload receipt or an archive-equivalent replacement to instantiate the support
surface.  Therefore a future support claim is accepted exactly after one of the
two finite common updates above, and otherwise the no-receipt normal form remains
the accepted status.
\end{proof}

\begin{corollary}[Current no-receipt support-surface non-escalation]
\label{cor:current-no-receipt-support-surface-non-escalation}
In the current source package the records
\path{review_bundle/FINAL_DIGESTS_SHA256.md},
\path{review_bundle/REVIEW_BUNDLE_MANIFEST.json},
\path{review_bundle/current_package_pass_records.json}, and
\path{theorem_inventory.json} contain no venue upload receipt, public DOI, or
institutional archive identifier for \path{review_bundle/}.  They do record the
public automath branch/path coordinate as an archive-equivalent locator, but only
under matching file-list, digest, manifest, and path-semantics tests.  Hence every
claim whose evidence surface is a bundle-local path is accepted only as a
conditional exact-supplemental-inclusion or matching archive-equivalent claim.
Such a claim becomes an externally instantiated submitted-support claim only
after one of the two finite records below is present on the submitted support
surface:
\begin{enumerate}
\item an upload receipt naming the exact \path{review_bundle/} directory and the
final digest manifest; or
\item an archive-equivalent locator satisfying the file-list, digest, manifest,
and path-semantics tests of
Audit record~\ref{audit:review-bundle-archive-equivalence}.
\end{enumerate}
Until then, the theorem inventory, verifier logs, case snapshots, source
snapshot, and primary-claim inventory remain local/path-verified review-bundle
coordinates rather than unconditional public or venue-submitted coordinates.
\end{corollary}

\begin{proof}
The four named files are the only finite records in the present source package
that select the support branch for \path{review_bundle/}: the digest manifest
gives the byte-level package identity, the review-bundle manifest gives the
submission strategy, the pass-record ledger exposes the accepted finite gates,
and \path{theorem_inventory.json} is the theorem-ledger coordinate.  Their
recorded branch is exact supplemental inclusion and their archive fields state
that no archive replacement or locator is claimed.  Therefore they determine the
bytes and paths that must be supplied, but they do not determine that a venue
upload, DOI minting event, institutional archiving event, or archive-equivalent
publication event has occurred.

By Theorem~\ref{thm:upload-or-archive-instantiation-boundary}, an externally
instantiated bundle-backed claim needs exactly the missing external coordinate:
either an upload record for the exact directory and final digest manifest, or a
replacement archive record passing
Audit record~\ref{audit:review-bundle-archive-equivalence}.  By
Corollary~\ref{cor:upload-or-archive-instantiation-record}, the digest manifest
alone is not such a coordinate.  If neither finite record is present, promoting a
bundle-local path to an unconditional submitted-support coordinate would add an
evidence coordinate not contained in the support surface, contrary to
Theorem~\ref{thm:finite-coordinate-determination-boundary}.  The stated
conditional boundary follows for every bundle-backed surface named in the
corollary.
\end{proof}

\begin{corollary}[Fresh inventory digest instance for this source state]
\label{cor:fresh-inventory-digest-instance-this-source-state}
In the present source state, the regenerated files
\path{theorem_inventory.json} and \path{theorem_inventory.md} are closed as local
exact-inclusion coordinates only after their SHA-256 rows in
\path{review_bundle/FINAL_DIGESTS_SHA256.md} are recomputed from the bytes of
those two files and the finite command-boundary table names that refreshed
manifest state.  This corollary supplies no upload receipt, archive locator,
extractor-completeness certificate, or semantic proof check; it supplies only the
local byte-coordinate instance required by
Corollary~\ref{cor:current-theorem-inventory-digest-instance}.
\end{corollary}

\begin{proof}
There are two relevant file coordinates, namely
\path{theorem_inventory.json} and \path{theorem_inventory.md}.  For each
coordinate the SHA-256 computation is a deterministic function of the finite byte
string stored at that relative path.  Replacing the rows in
\path{review_bundle/FINAL_DIGESTS_SHA256.md} by the values computed after the
inventory regeneration therefore makes the digest manifest name the regenerated
bytes rather than a prior or anticipated inventory state.  The command-boundary
table is the only place in the manuscript where command-run and freshness claims
are promoted beyond path names, so updating that table to the same refreshed
state prevents a stale 2026-06-05 package description from being read as the
current digest instance.  The argument uses only byte equality at the two named
paths and the finite-record boundary of
Theorem~\ref{thm:digest-closure-refresh-criterion}; it does not add an extractor
run, external support-surface instantiation, upload event, archive event, or
semantic validation.  Hence exactly the local digest-closure claim stated above
is obtained.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Digest-closure refresh criterion for final package claims]
\label{thm:digest-closure-refresh-criterion}
For the current presentation package, any claim that a submitted support surface
has exact final digests is accepted only for a closed finite state $D$ satisfying
all of the following conditions.
\begin{enumerate}
\item The digest manifest \path{review_bundle/FINAL_DIGESTS_SHA256.md} contains
SHA-256 rows for \path{main.tex}, \path{submission_abstract.tex},
\path{references.bib}, \path{source_interface_record.json},
\path{theorem_inventory.json}, \path{theorem_inventory.md}, and every file in
the selected \path{review_bundle/} support directory.
\item The digest rows are computed after the accepted versions of
\path{theorem_inventory.json}, \path{theorem_inventory.md},
\path{review_bundle/primary_claim_inventory.json}, and every verifier log named
by a command-run claim have been written.
\item If \path{submission_abstract.tex} is edited after the primary-claim
inventory was checked, then
\path{review_bundle/verify_primary_claim_inventory.py} is rerun and its fresh
log replaces the old primary-inventory freshness coordinate before the final
digest rows are recomputed.
\item If an upload-time venue-compliance claim, rather than a dated local
readiness observation, is made, the venue and bibliography check is rerun at the
upload rule state and the fresh venue record is included before the final digest
rows are recomputed.
\end{enumerate}
If any one of these conditions fails, the package still has the local
path-verified records printed in the manuscript, but it does not have an exact
final-digest certificate for the regenerated inventory, primary-claim, or
upload-time venue component that failed the condition.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
The support-surface certificate is byte-level: by
Theorem~\ref{thm:support-surface-normal-form}, exact inclusion and
archive-equivalent inclusion both read the finite file list, relative paths, and
SHA-256 content rows rather than prose descriptions of those files.  Therefore a
file written after the digest manifest was computed cannot be covered by that
manifest; its byte string was not one of the inputs to the listed hash values.
This proves the necessity of recomputing the digest rows after regenerating the
theorem inventory, primary-claim inventory, and command logs named by the final
package.

The primary-claim inventory is line-span dependent.  Its verifier reads the
current \path{submission_abstract.tex}, checks the row-id set and line ranges,
and prints a finite freshness log.  If the primary source is changed after that
run, at least the source line-span premise of the old log is no longer the
current primary source checked by the verifier.  A fresh verifier run before the
digest pass restores a finite coordinate for the new primary state; omitting it
leaves only the old path-verified inventory observation.

Venue compliance is external-state dependent by
Proposition~\ref{audit:fresh-venue-record-criterion} and
Theorem~\ref{thm:venue-rule-freshness-non-escalation}.  A dated venue log can
witness local readiness at its observation time, but upload-time compliance
requires a record of the rule state at upload or an included replacement record.
Thus a package that claims upload-time venue compliance must write that fresh
record before the digest closure step; otherwise the digest manifest can cover
only the dated local-readiness file, not the missing upload-time premise.

Combining these observations gives a finite fixed-point condition: first write
the accepted inventory files and freshness logs, then compute the manifest over
those bytes, and finally submit either the exact directory or an
archive-equivalent replacement.  If a later edit or upload-time claim changes one
of the inputs, the fixed point is broken precisely at that finite subrecord.  The
remaining local rows remain available at their declared evidence levels, but the
stronger exact final-digest claim is not accepted for the changed component.
\end{proof}


\begin{proposition}[Primary architectural fallback without external support]
\label{prop:primary-architectural-fallback-without-external-support}
Assume the current source package is used without an upload receipt for the exact
\path{review_bundle/} directory and without an archive-equivalent replacement
satisfying Audit record~\ref{audit:review-bundle-archive-equivalence}.  Then the
primary presentation article may assert only the source-level architecture and
path-verified local-package claims whose evidence surfaces are visible outside the
missing bundle.  In particular, the theorem-ledger claim of
Theorem~\ref{thm:theorem-obligation-ledger-boundary}, the case-snapshot claims,
the verifier-log claims, and any claim whose certificate coordinate is
\path{theorem_inventory.json} or a file under \path{review_bundle/} cannot be
read as externally submitted evidence.  If either accepted external instantiation
record is later supplied, the same claims re-enter exactly at the finite evidence
level stated in their certificate rows and not at any stronger semantic or venue
acceptance level.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
Corollary~\ref{cor:current-no-receipt-support-surface-non-escalation} identifies
the only two records that can externally instantiate a bundle-backed support
claim: an exact upload receipt for \path{review_bundle/} with the final digest
manifest, or an archive-equivalent locator passing the finite file-list, digest,
manifest, and path-semantics tests.  Under the present hypothesis neither record
is available.  Hence every bundle-local path is absent from the externally
submitted finite support surface.

The affected claims are precisely those whose certificate coordinate names such a
path.  Table~\ref{tab:submitted-theorem-obligation-ledger} places the manual
theorem inventory at \path{theorem_inventory.json} and exposes it through
\path{review_bundle/current_package_pass_records.json}; the case-snapshot and
verifier-log statements similarly name files under \path{review_bundle/}.  By
Theorem~\ref{thm:finite-coordinate-determination-boundary}, a claim cannot acquire
an evidence coordinate that is absent from the finite support surface.  Therefore
those claims cannot be read as externally submitted evidence while both accepted
instantiation records are missing.

Removing that external reading does not remove the primary article's independent
architectural assertion.  The primary text describes the compiler's typed record
interface and cites the pinned public source surface; those claims do not require
the missing bundle-local coordinate.  If a valid exact-upload or
archive-equivalent record is later supplied, Theorem~\ref{thm:support-surface-normal-form}
and Corollary~\ref{cor:upload-or-archive-instantiation-record} put the
bundle-backed claims back into the finite support surface.  The reinstatement is
only at the recorded evidence levels, because the same corollaries and
Theorem~\ref{thm:upload-or-archive-instantiation-boundary} exclude stronger
readings such as semantic proof checking, fresh dynamic validation, venue
compliance, or acceptance.\end{proof}
