\begin{theorem}[Deterministic theorem-environment extractor boundary]
\label{thm:deterministic-theorem-environment-extractor-boundary}
Let the theorem-like environment domain $\mathcal E$ for this package consist
of the five environment names \path{definition}, \path{lemma},
\path{proposition}, \path{theorem}, and \path{corollary}.  The command
\path{python review_bundle/extract_theorem_environments.py} run on
\path{main.tex} and \path{theorem_inventory.json} is a deterministic
syntactic extractor for the following finite claim only: every labelled
environment in \path{main.tex} whose environment name belongs to $\mathcal E$ is
listed by label in \path{theorem_inventory.json}; every such environment is
labelled; and no such label is duplicated.  When the refreshed run log
\path{review_bundle/theorem_environment_extraction_run.log} records exit code
\path{0}, the package has a deterministic theorem-environment coverage
certificate at this syntactic label level.  The certificate does not assert that
the inventory is a semantic proof checker, that the printed proofs are correct,
that external Lean or BEDC semantics were rebuilt, or that theorem-like material
outside $\mathcal E$ is covered.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
The extractor is a finite program whose inputs are exactly the two byte strings
at \path{main.tex} and \path{theorem_inventory.json}.  It scans the source lines
for beginnings of the five displayed environment names, records the first
\path{\textbackslash label\{...\}} before the matching end of the same
environment, and forms the finite set $T$ of recorded labels together with line
numbers, titles, and environment names.  It also reads the inventory JSON and
forms the finite set $I$ of labels appearing in inventory label fields with one
of the package theorem-label prefixes.  These operations are deterministic
functions of the two input byte strings.

The script exits with code \path{0} exactly when three finite tests pass: there
is no element of $\mathcal E$ without a label, no duplicate label in $T$, and
$T\subseteq I$.  Therefore a refreshed exit-code-zero log proves precisely the
syntactic coverage assertion: every labelled theorem-like environment in the
chosen domain has a corresponding inventory label and the source labels are
well-formed for this comparison.  If any test fails, the printed missing-label,
duplicate-label, or omitted-from-inventory list is the finite bad subrecord.

No step of this computation parses mathematical statements, checks proofs,
invokes Lean, consults the publication daemon, or validates external artifacts.
Thus the certificate is stronger than the earlier manual ledger at the single
point needed by the scope contract---deterministic environment-label coverage---
and no stronger than that.  The semantic and implementation boundaries remain
those of Theorem~\ref{thm:publication-safety-interface} and
Corollary~\ref{cor:finite-record-gate-soundness-boundary}.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Extractor-domain exactness and non-gluing]
\label{thm:extractor-domain-exactness-non-gluing}
For the submitted theorem-inventory surface, the command
\path{python review_bundle/extract_theorem_environments.py} is exact for one
domain and impossible to glue beyond that domain without an added finite
coordinate.  Its exact domain is the syntactic label-coverage predicate on
\[
\mathcal E=\{\texttt{definition},\texttt{lemma},\texttt{proposition},
\texttt{theorem},\texttt{corollary}\}.
\]
An exit-code-zero run proves precisely that the source has no unlabelled
environment in $\mathcal E$, no duplicate label among those environments, and no
labelled environment in $\mathcal E$ omitted from \path{theorem_inventory.json}.
It proves no audit-record coverage, statement-semantics coverage,
proof-correctness coverage, Lean/BEDC rebuild, artifact validation, daemon run,
or venue-state fact.  Any manuscript reading that adds one of those conclusions
is a properly stronger reading of the theorem-inventory coordinate and is
accepted only after the submitted surface adds the corresponding parser domain,
semantic checker, proof checker, build log, artifact log, daemon log, or venue
record with assigned pass rows.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
The script definition fixes the domain: its \path{THEOREM_ENVS} tuple contains
exactly the five displayed names, and its begin-pattern is built from that tuple.
The extraction loop records only occurrences of those begins in \path{main.tex},
then searches within the matched environment for the first \path{\label{...}}
token.  The inventory side reads only label strings from
\path{theorem_inventory.json}.  Thus the finite predicate computed by the script
has exactly three positive clauses: every detected environment in $\mathcal E$
has a label, the detected labels are not duplicated, and the detected label set
is contained in the inventory label set.  Theorem~\ref{thm:deterministic-theorem-environment-extractor-boundary}
therefore gives exactness on this syntactic domain.

Each proposed extension reads a coordinate absent from that computation.  Audit
records use a different LaTeX environment counter and are not in $\mathcal E$;
statement semantics would require a parser and meaning relation for theorem
bodies; proof correctness would require a proof checker or a formal build;
Lean/BEDC rebuilding, artifact validation, daemon execution, and venue state each
require their own command, source state, environment, exit code, and log or
external record.  Two package states can agree on \path{main.tex} labels in
$\mathcal E$ and on \path{theorem_inventory.json} while differing in any of
those absent coordinates.  By Theorem~\ref{thm:finite-coordinate-determination-boundary},
the extractor's accepted finite record cannot determine such a stronger fact.

Finally, in the stronger-reading preorder of
Definition~\ref{def:stronger-reading-preorder}, adding any of the excluded
conclusions enlarges the claim-kind, evidence-surface, command-boundary, or
assigned-gate coordinate.  Theorem~\ref{thm:finite-extension-budget-normal-form}
then supplies the positive direction: once the enlarged parser domain, semantic
checker, proof checker, build log, artifact log, daemon log, or venue record and
its pass rows are included, that stronger coordinate may be checked by the
finite interface.  Without those additions,
Theorem~\ref{thm:finite-certificate-obstruction-skeleton} returns the missing
coordinate as a finite bad subrecord.  Hence the extractor is exact and maximal
for syntactic label coverage over $\mathcal E$, and it cannot be glued into any
semantic, auditrecord, implementation, artifact, daemon, or venue certificate by
prose alone.
\end{proof}


\begin{theorem}[Final-byte extractor inventory inclusion]
\label{thm:final-byte-extractor-inventory-inclusion}
Fix a manuscript source byte string $M$, an inventory byte string $J$, and the
extractor byte string
\path{review_bundle/extract_theorem_environments.py}.  Let $T(M)$ be the finite
set of labels obtained by running that extractor on the five-environment domain
$\mathcal E$ of Theorem~\ref{thm:deterministic-theorem-environment-extractor-boundary},
and let $I(J)$ be the finite set of inventory labels read from $J$ with the
accepted theorem-label prefixes.  For the final bytes of this package, a
zero-exit extractor run is equivalent to the following three finite conditions:
\[
  T(M)\subseteq I(J),
  \qquad \text{no element of }\mathcal E\text{ in }M\text{ is unlabelled},
  \qquad \text{and }T(M)\text{ has no duplicate source label}.
\]
Consequently, after any edit to \path{main.tex} or \path{theorem_inventory.json},
the exact theorem-obligation ledger coordinate used by
Theorems~\ref{thm:theorem-obligation-ledger-boundary} and
\ref{thm:post-inventory-package-fixed-point} is available only from a fresh
zero-exit run on those final bytes.  This inclusion criterion is the complete
bad-example obstruction for missing in-domain labels: an omitted labelled
environment, an unlabelled in-domain environment, or a duplicate in-domain label
is witnessed by a finite failing row printed by the extractor, and no prose
inventory statement can remove that row without changing $M$, $J$, or the
extractor coordinate.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
The first two input byte strings determine the two finite sets.  The extractor
constructs $T(M)$ by a line scan for begins of the five names in $\mathcal E$,
searches each matched environment body for its first label token, and records the
label together with its line and title fields.  It constructs $I(J)$ by reading
the inventory JSON and collecting the finite label fields with the accepted
prefixes.  Since these operations use only fixed regular expressions and JSON
parsing on finite byte strings, $T(M)$, the unlabelled-environment list, the
duplicate-label list, and $I(J)$ are deterministic functions of the final bytes.

The extractor's acceptance test is exactly the conjunction of the three displayed
conditions.  If an in-domain environment has no label, the unlabelled list is
nonempty and the command exits nonzero.  If two in-domain environments have the
same label, the duplicate list is nonempty and the command exits nonzero.  If a
recorded source label is absent from the inventory, then the set difference
$T(M)\setminus I(J)$ is nonempty and the command exits nonzero.  Conversely, when
all three lists are empty and $T(M)\subseteq I(J)$, the extractor has no failing
row for its declared predicate and exits with code zero.  This proves the stated
equivalence.

It remains to connect the equivalence to the paper's support-coordinate chain.
Theorem~\ref{thm:theorem-obligation-ledger-boundary} uses the inventory only as a
finite theorem-obligation ledger plus the syntactic extractor coordinate, and
Theorem~\ref{thm:post-inventory-package-fixed-point} requires that the inventory,
extractor run, verifier logs, and final digest be ordered after the final source
bytes are selected.  Editing either $M$ or $J$ changes one of the deterministic
inputs just described.  The exact ledger coordinate for the edited package
therefore requires the fresh zero-exit run on those edited bytes; otherwise the
package has at most the weaker path-verified or stale-coordinate reading already
classified in Theorem~\ref{thm:post-inventory-support-coordinate-dichotomy}.

The bad-example statement follows from the same finite disjunction.  Each failure
mode is represented by an explicit finite row: an unlabelled environment, a
duplicate label, or a member of $T(M)\setminus I(J)$.  Because
Theorem~\ref{thm:finite-coordinate-determination-boundary} allows the submitted
claim to depend only on recorded finite coordinates, prose cannot erase such a
row.  Only changing the source bytes, the inventory bytes, or the extractor
coordinate can change the computed obstruction.  Thus the theorem gives the
final-byte inclusion gate required for the post-inventory theorem chain and no
semantic proof-checking, Lean/BEDC rebuild, artifact, daemon, upload, or venue
claim beyond that syntactic gate.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Theorem-inventory proof-checker obstruction]
\label{thm:theorem-inventory-proofchecker-obstruction}
Let $\mathcal E$ be the five-environment domain
\{\path{definition}, \path{lemma}, \path{proposition}, \path{theorem},
\path{corollary}\} in \path{main.tex}.  The finite theorem-inventory support
surface consisting of \path{theorem_inventory.json}, \path{theorem_inventory.md},
and the output of \path{python review_bundle/extract_theorem_environments.py}
classifies exactly labelled-environment coverage in $\mathcal E$: every labelled
environment in $\mathcal E$ is present in the inventory, no such label is missing
or duplicated, and no inventory conclusion glues to material outside that domain.
It is not a proof checker.  Any stronger reading that validates proof semantics,
checks theorem statements against Lean or BEDC, covers other TeX environments,
covers \path{submission_abstract.tex}, validates remarks or audit records, or
certifies artifact or venue facts is accepted by this calculus if and only if the
support surface is extended by a nonempty finite checker coordinate for that
additional domain and by an assigned pass row.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
Theorem~\ref{thm:deterministic-theorem-environment-extractor-boundary} identifies
the extractor domain as the five classes in $\mathcal E$ and records that the
script reads \path{main.tex} and \path{theorem_inventory.json}.  Theorem~\ref{thm:extractor-domain-exactness-non-gluing}
then proves non-gluing: the successful extractor run eliminates exactly the bad
example of a labelled environment in $\mathcal E$ missing from the inventory or a
duplicate label in that same syntactic domain, and it says nothing about other
files, environments, proof semantics, or external artifacts.

To see that no proof-checking conclusion follows, fix two TeX states with the
same sequence of labelled $\mathcal E$ environments and the same inventory rows,
but with different proof bodies for one theorem.  For example, replace a proof
body by another body while leaving the surrounding \path{\textbackslash begin}
line, label, environment kind, and inventory row unchanged.  The extractor reads
only the syntactic environment record and the inventory labels, so it returns the
same coverage result on both states.  A semantic proof checker could distinguish
the states, but that checker output is not part of the finite projection.  By
Theorem~\ref{thm:finite-coordinate-determination-boundary}, a semantic theorem
validation claim is therefore not determined by the inventory support surface.

The same argument applies to every listed stronger domain: each requires reading
at least one new file, environment class, proof-assistant state, artifact log, or
venue record that is absent from the extractor projection.  Hence each stronger
reading has a finite bad subrecord unless a new checker coordinate and assigned
pass row are submitted.  Conversely, once that coordinate is submitted, the claim
is no longer a gluing inference from the old inventory; it is a separate finite
extension governed by Theorem~\ref{thm:record-interface-rigidity}.  This proves
both exact syntactic coverage and the proof-checker obstruction.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Theorem-inventory boundary]
\label{prop:manual-theorem-inventory-boundary}
This theorem retains the legacy cross-reference label
\path{prop:manual-theorem-inventory-boundary} for compatibility with earlier
acceptance-ledger entries; its environment category is \path{theorem}, not
\path{proposition}.
The file \path{theorem_inventory.json} is a theorem-obligation inventory record whose
current support has two finite parts.  First, it is an obligation ledger selected
by finite-record review and connected to the manifest and acceptance ledger.  Second,
Theorem~\ref{thm:deterministic-theorem-environment-extractor-boundary} supplies a
deterministic syntactic comparison between the labelled theorem-like environments
in the five-environment domain $\mathcal E$ and the labels appearing in the
inventory.  The inventory indexes obligations; the evidence for those obligations
is supplied by the corresponding TeX statements and proofs in this source,
together with external records only where a statement explicitly cites them.  It
is not a semantic proof checker and it is not an executable completeness
certificate for theorem-like material outside $\mathcal E$.  Consequently any
claim that the ledger itself semantically checks the printed proofs, reruns Lean
or BEDC, validates artifacts, or certifies an external venue state is rejected by
the current finite-record interface, while the bounded obligation-ledger and
syntactic environment-label coverage claims are accepted.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
The support records named in Table~\ref{tab:submitted-theorem-obligation-ledger}
are finite manifest and ledger records.  The root manifest exposes the path
\path{theorem_inventory.json}; acceptance-ledger entry \path{a7} records the
inventory, marker-existence, theorem-environment extraction, and submission-pack
gates for the bounded theorem-ledger claim; and the certificate verifier checks
only record shape, gate coverage, evidence pointers, and file availability.  The
new extractor coordinates add a command, source state, environment, exit code,
and log path for syntactic label coverage in the domain $\mathcal E$.  By
Theorem~\ref{thm:deterministic-theorem-environment-extractor-boundary}, those
coordinates prove only that all labelled environments in the chosen domain are
represented by labels in the inventory and that no source label is missing or
duplicated.

The remaining boundary is semantic, not syntactic.  A finite inventory can still
be useful because it fixes the Stage-A obligation surface that the submission
chooses to expose and lets the certificate ledger prevent hidden prose-only
references to that surface.  The extractor strengthens this by eliminating one
bad example: an environment in $\mathcal E$ whose label exists in \path{main.tex}
but is absent from the inventory.  Once the extractor log has exit code
\path{0}, that bad example cannot occur.  Finite-coordinate determination is
still exact, however.  The accepted records are manifest, ledger, table, and
syntactic extractor coordinates; they cannot imply a nonrecorded semantic
theorem checker, nonrecorded Lean rebuild, nonrecorded daemon run, or nonrecorded
artifact validation.  Hence the finite interface accepts the obligation ledger
together with deterministic syntactic environment-label coverage and rejects
every stronger proof-checking or implementation-soundness reading.
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[Regenerated theorem-inventory digest closure]
\label{prop:regenerated-theorem-inventory-digest-closure}
Suppose a theorem-inventory pass regenerates either \path{theorem_inventory.json} or
\path{theorem_inventory.md} after the package has selected the exact-inclusion
support branch.  Then the final support-surface claims of
Theorem~\ref{thm:final-support-surface-closure}, the submitted theorem-ledger
claim of Theorem~\ref{thm:theorem-obligation-ledger-boundary}, and the command
boundary of Proposition~\ref{audit:command-run-boundary-inventory} remain closed
only under the fixed-point conditions of
Theorem~\ref{thm:digest-closure-refresh-criterion}: the package refreshes the
corresponding SHA-256 rows in \path{review_bundle/FINAL_DIGESTS_SHA256.md},
reruns the finite record verifiers and the theorem-environment extractor whose
records name that digest manifest or inventory coordinate, and does not promote
primary-claim or venue-compliance rows beyond the fresh records actually
included.  Once those rows and logs are refreshed, the regeneration creates no
new mathematical, Lean, daemon, or artifact claim: it only changes the
byte-coordinate of the theorem-obligation ledger and its syntactic label-coverage
certificate inside the already selected finite support surface.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
The selected support branch is byte-level exact inclusion.  In that branch,
Theorem~\ref{thm:final-support-surface-closure} identifies the submitted support
surface by relative paths together with the digest rows in
\path{review_bundle/FINAL_DIGESTS_SHA256.md}.  The theorem inventory is one of
those paths: Table~\ref{tab:submitted-theorem-obligation-ledger} exposes
\path{theorem_inventory.json} through the root manifest and acceptance-ledger
entry \path{a7}, while the final digest record also lists the human-readable
companion \path{theorem_inventory.md}.  Therefore regenerating either file after
the digest record was written changes a finite coordinate used by the exact
support-surface relation.  Until the SHA-256 rows are recomputed, the digest
record denotes the old bytes, and the regenerated inventory is not the same
finite certificate file as the one named by the final packaging record.

The required reruns are exactly the finite record verifiers and extractor at the
package boundary.  The certificate-record verifier checks that the
manifest-required paths, including the theorem-inventory coordinate, are present
and covered by the accepted gate records; the source-interface and primary-claim
verifiers record their command boundary against the same final digest manifest;
and the theorem-environment extractor compares labelled environments in
$\mathcal E$ with the inventory labels.  Rerunning those commands after the
digest refresh supplies fresh source state, environment, exit code, and log-path
coordinates for the command-boundary table.  No verifier in this list proves
theorem semantics.  Hence the refreshed state is closed precisely as a finite
record-and-digest package with deterministic syntactic label coverage, and by
Proposition~\ref{prop:manual-theorem-inventory-boundary} it remains a
non-semantic theorem-obligation ledger rather than a proof-checking certificate.
\end{proof}

\begin{corollary}[Current theorem-inventory digest instance]
\label{cor:current-theorem-inventory-digest-instance}
For the present local support package, after \path{theorem_inventory.json} and
\path{theorem_inventory.md} have been regenerated, the submitted
theorem-obligation ledger may be cited in
Theorem~\ref{thm:theorem-obligation-ledger-boundary} only through the refreshed
digest rows for those two files in \path{review_bundle/FINAL_DIGESTS_SHA256.md}
and through fresh finite-record verifier logs produced after the regenerated
inventory files are present.  If either the two digest rows or the
post-regeneration verifier logs are absent, the
inventory remains a local index but is not closed as an exact-inclusion
support coordinate for the bundle-backed theorem-ledger claim.
\end{corollary}

\begin{proof}
Apply Proposition~\ref{prop:regenerated-theorem-inventory-digest-closure} to the
two regenerated inventory files in this package.  The support branch selected by
Proposition~\ref{prop:final-package-shape-convention} is exact inclusion, so the
identity of each submitted support file is read from the corresponding
relative-path and SHA-256 row in \path{review_bundle/FINAL_DIGESTS_SHA256.md}.
Theorem~\ref{thm:theorem-obligation-ledger-boundary} uses
\path{theorem_inventory.json} as the theorem-ledger coordinate and uses
Theorem~\ref{thm:deterministic-theorem-environment-extractor-boundary} only for
syntactic label coverage.  Proposition~\ref{prop:manual-theorem-inventory-boundary}
excludes every stronger semantic-proof reading.  Therefore the only new fact
needed after regeneration is byte-coordinate and command-coordinate closure: the
digest rows must name the new bytes, and the finite verifiers and extractor whose
records mention the digest or inventory coordinate must be rerun against the
package state in which the regenerated inventory files exist.  Without those rows
and logs, the ledger is still a local file, but the exact-inclusion support
relation points either to stale bytes or to an unverifiable command-boundary
state.  With them, no additional mathematical, Lean, daemon, or artifact claim is
introduced; the refreshed package supplies exactly the finite support coordinate
used by the submitted theorem-obligation ledger.
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[Post-inventory fixed-point support certificate]
\label{prop:post-inventory-fixed-point-support-certificate}
In the present package state, the two regenerated theorem-inventory files are
accepted by the exact-inclusion theorem-ledger chain only at the following
finite fixed point: the rows of
\path{review_bundle/FINAL_DIGESTS_SHA256.md} name
\path{theorem_inventory.json} and \path{theorem_inventory.md}; the same digest
manifest names the refreshed logs
\path{review_bundle/certificate_verification_run.log},
\path{review_bundle/source_interface_verification_run.log},
\path{review_bundle/primary_claim_inventory_freshness_2026-06-07.log}, and
\path{review_bundle/theorem_environment_extraction_run.log}; and
Table~\ref{tab:command-boundary-inventory} records those four invocations as
finite checks with exit code \path{0}.  Under these hypotheses,
Corollary~\ref{cor:current-theorem-inventory-digest-instance} applies to the
theorem-ledger coordinate used by
Theorem~\ref{thm:theorem-obligation-ledger-boundary}.  If any one of the two
inventory digest rows or four refreshed command-log rows is replaced by a
pre-regeneration row, the exact-inclusion support claim fails and only the local
historical index remains.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
The statement is a finite-coordinate instance of
Theorem~\ref{thm:digest-closure-refresh-criterion}.  The first named coordinate
is \path{theorem_inventory.json}, and the second is
\path{theorem_inventory.md}; after regeneration, each coordinate is identified
inside the exact-inclusion surface only by the SHA-256 row attached to its
current byte string in \path{review_bundle/FINAL_DIGESTS_SHA256.md}.  The four
command-run coordinates are exactly the finite verifier and extractor logs listed
in Table~\ref{tab:command-boundary-inventory}: the certificate-record verifier,
the source-interface record verifier, the primary-claim inventory verifier, and
the theorem-environment extractor.  The same table records command, source state,
environment, exit code, and log path, and its boundary column states that these
are record or syntactic label-coverage checks rather than Lean, daemon, Rule110,
upload, or semantic validation runs.

If the five rows are current, the hypotheses of
Proposition~\ref{prop:regenerated-theorem-inventory-digest-closure} are met for
the regenerated inventory files and for the verifier and extractor records whose
logs name the digest or inventory boundary.
Corollary~\ref{cor:current-theorem-inventory-digest-instance} then supplies
exactly the local fixed-point instance needed by the theorem-obligation ledger in
Theorem~\ref{thm:theorem-obligation-ledger-boundary}.  Conversely, suppose that
one of the two inventory digest rows is stale.  Then the digest manifest denotes
old bytes at one of the two inventory paths, so the regenerated file is not the
file certified by the exact-inclusion row.  Suppose instead that one of the
four command-log rows is stale.  Then the command-boundary table promotes a
fresh verifier invocation while the digest surface still names an earlier log
file, so the command-run coordinate is not closed in the selected byte surface.
In either case the bad subrecord is finite: it is the stale row together with
its relative path.  The exact-inclusion claim therefore fails by
Theorem~\ref{thm:finite-coordinate-determination-boundary}, while
Proposition~\ref{prop:manual-theorem-inventory-boundary} still permits the
weaker reading of the inventory as a local historical manual index.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Post-inventory finite-record realization]
\label{thm:post-inventory-finite-record-realization}
Suppose the following finite records are present in the current source package:
\begin{enumerate}
\item \path{theorem_inventory.json} and \path{theorem_inventory.md};
\item a zero-exit log for
\path{python review_bundle/extract_theorem_environments.py} computed after those
two files were written;
\item zero-exit logs for
\path{python review_bundle/verify_certificate_records.py},
\path{python review_bundle/verify_source_interface_record.py}, and
\path{python review_bundle/verify_primary_claim_inventory.py} computed against the
same post-inventory package state; and
\item a regenerated \path{review_bundle/FINAL_DIGESTS_SHA256.md} whose rows name
the two inventory files and the four log files just listed.
\end{enumerate}
Then the first coordinate in
Theorem~\ref{thm:submission-support-fixed-point-normal-form} is instantiated as
a local exact-inclusion theorem-ledger support coordinate.  The instantiated
coordinate is exactly syntactic inventory coverage plus finite-record verifier
closure: it is not a semantic proof check, a Lean rebuild, a daemon run, an
artifact rerun, a venue upload receipt, or an archive-equivalent locator.  If one
of the four listed conditions is absent, the theorem-inventory files remain at
most local historical indices and cannot support the exact-inclusion
theorem-ledger claim.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
The proof is by finite-coordinate determination.  The first condition supplies
the two byte strings that are to be accepted as the theorem-ledger inventory.
The second condition supplies the only syntactic coverage test used by
Theorem~\ref{thm:deterministic-theorem-environment-extractor-boundary}: the
extractor reads \path{main.tex}, extracts theorem-like labels, and compares them
with labels present in \path{theorem_inventory.json}.  The third condition
supplies the companion finite-record checks for the certificate ledger, the
source-interface record, and the primary-claim inventory.  The fourth condition
places the resulting bytes and logs into the selected exact-inclusion digest
surface.

Under all four conditions, every finite coordinate named in
Proposition~\ref{prop:post-inventory-fixed-point-support-certificate} is present:
the inventory-file coordinates are identified by SHA-256 rows, and the extractor
and verifier coordinates are identified by their zero-exit command logs and
digest rows.  Therefore
Corollary~\ref{cor:current-theorem-inventory-digest-instance} applies, giving the
local exact-inclusion support coordinate used in the first clause of
Theorem~\ref{thm:submission-support-fixed-point-normal-form}.  The boundary of
the coordinate is the intersection of the boundaries of its inputs: the
extractor is syntactic label coverage only, the three verifiers are finite-record
checks only, and the digest manifest is a byte-coordinate manifest only.  None of
these records contains a proof-assistant rebuild, publication-daemon run,
artifact validation rerun, external upload receipt, or archive locator, so no
such stronger coordinate can be inferred.

Conversely, omit any one condition.  If the inventory files are absent, there is
no byte string for the theorem-ledger row.  If the extractor log is absent or
pre-inventory, syntactic coverage is not recorded for the current inventory
bytes.  If any finite-record verifier log is absent or pre-inventory, the
corresponding support record is not closed against the same package state.  If
the digest manifest is not regenerated over the inventory files and logs, the
exact-inclusion surface names stale or missing byte coordinates.  In each case
Theorem~\ref{thm:finite-coordinate-determination-boundary} rejects the stronger
reading and leaves only whatever local historical index was actually recorded.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Seven-row submitted theorem-obligation ledger integration]
\label{thm:theorem-obligation-ledger-boundary}
Let $P^{+}_{7}$ be the seven-row support state obtained from $P^{+}_{6}$
by the conservative finite-record extension of
Theorem~\ref{thm:nested-package-states}, namely by adding exactly the submitted theorem-ledger coordinate \path{a7}.  In
$P^{+}_{7}$, the theorem-obligation ledger is no longer a detached review-only
artifact: the submitted finite support surface contains
\path{theorem_inventory.json} through the manifest field \path{theorem_inventory}
and through acceptance-ledger entry \path{a7}.  The earlier six-row state
$P^{+}_{6}$ of Theorems~\ref{thm:nested-package-states}
and~\ref{thm:current-package-certificate-exactness} remains
the row-frozen certificate state for \path{a1}--\path{a6}; the present theorem
adds exactly the theorem-ledger coordinate \path{a7}.  Consequently the
package may make
the following bounded theorem-ledger claim: every theorem-obligation assertion
made by the submission is supported only through the finite coordinates in
Table~\ref{tab:submitted-theorem-obligation-ledger}, namely the integrated
theorem inventory, the primary-claim inventory, the certificate ledger, and the
manifest exposure records.  By
Proposition~\ref{prop:manual-theorem-inventory-boundary} and
Theorem~\ref{thm:manual-ledger-dependence-boundary}, this is a theorem-obligation ledger claim with deterministic syntactic
theorem-environment label coverage, not a semantic proof-checking or
implementation-soundness claim.  The claim does not assert that
\path{theorem_inventory.json} proves the mathematical semantics of each theorem,
does not replace the proofs printed in this source, and does not certify a Lean
rebuild, publication-daemon run, Rule110 artifact rerun, semantic extractor
completeness beyond $\mathcal E$, or external upload.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
The support surface is finite and can be inspected coordinate by coordinate.
The root manifest now contains the key \path{theorem_inventory} with value
\path{theorem_inventory.json}.  The certificate schema includes the same key in
its required manifest-file map.  Therefore a certificate-record verification of
the enlarged package must check that the manifest exposes this root-level
inventory path and that the file is present.  This removes the previous
possibility of silently citing a root-level theorem-inventory artifact outside the support
surface.

The acceptance ledger contains the additional entry \path{a7}.  Its accepted
claim is exactly the bounded submitted theorem-obligation ledger claim; its
claim kinds are \path{traceability} and \path{submission}.  Under the schema's
gate dispatch, those kinds require the inventory, marker-existence, and
submission-pack gates; the theorem-extractor check is an additional command
boundary attached to the marker-existence gate.  Entry \path{a7} supplies the
required pass records: the inventory gate points to \path{theorem_inventory.json},
the marker-existence gate points to
Table~\ref{tab:submitted-theorem-obligation-ledger} and the extractor log, and
the submission-pack gate points to \path{review_bundle/REVIEW_BUNDLE_MANIFEST.json}.  Thus the
ledger supplies a finite route from the theorem-inventory file to the submitted
certificate records rather than a prose-only reference.

It remains to identify the boundary.  The schema-level verifier checks finite
record shape, required manifest paths, gate coverage, evidence pointers, and
evidence levels.  None of those checks is a semantic proof of the theorem-like
environments in this source, and none reruns Lean, the publication daemon, or the
Rule110/GroundCompiler artifacts.  The independent theorem-environment extractor
only supplies the syntactic coverage certificate of
Theorem~\ref{thm:deterministic-theorem-environment-extractor-boundary}.
Proposition~\ref{prop:manual-theorem-inventory-boundary} and
Theorem~\ref{thm:manual-ledger-dependence-boundary} supply the remaining
classification: the inventory is an accepted Stage-A ledger with syntactic label
coverage and not an executable semantic completeness certificate.  The theorem-ledger claim is
therefore admissible only at the path-verified evidence level stated in
\path{a7}.  The mathematical content of the article remains carried by the
printed definitions, propositions, lemmas, theorems, and proofs, and external
records carry only the finite evidence claims that cite them.  The integrated
inventory gives the submitted finite obligation surface against which those
theorem-like claims are enumerated; it indexes the obligations and does not prove
them.  This is exactly the bounded claim stated in the theorem.
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[Post-inventory support-tuple extraction certificate]
\label{prop:post-inventory-support-tuple-extraction-certificate}
For the current source package, the theorem-ledger support tuple is accepted only
in the post-inventory form consisting of
\path{theorem_inventory.json}, \path{theorem_inventory.md},
\path{review_bundle/theorem_environment_extraction_run.log},
\path{review_bundle/current_package_pass_records.json}, and
\path{review_bundle/FINAL_DIGESTS_SHA256.md}.  In that form the extraction log must be generated after the two inventory files
exist, the pass-record ledger must contain the theorem-ledger certificate entry,
and the digest manifest must be regenerated after those three records are in
place.  A digest manifest whose inventory rows predate any member of this tuple
does not certify the current theorem-ledger support coordinate; it certifies only
the earlier local byte state named by its rows.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
The tuple has five finite coordinates.  The first two are the regenerated manual
inventory files.  The third is the syntactic extractor output: by
Theorem~\ref{thm:deterministic-theorem-environment-extractor-boundary}, the
extractor reads \path{main.tex} and \path{theorem_inventory.json} and records
only labelled theorem-like environment coverage.  The fourth is the finite
certificate ledger entry for the theorem-obligation surface described in
Theorem~\ref{thm:theorem-obligation-ledger-boundary}.  The fifth is the digest
manifest used by Theorem~\ref{thm:final-support-surface-closure} to identify the
bytes available under exact supplemental inclusion.

If the five coordinates are produced in the stated order, every support claim
about the regenerated theorem ledger is a function of byte strings already fixed
before the digest pass: the inventory bytes are read by the extractor, the
theorem-ledger certificate entry records the finite support route, and the digest
manifest hashes the resulting files and log.  The support tuple is therefore a
finite coordinate of the package.  If, however, the digest rows for
\path{theorem_inventory.json} or \path{theorem_inventory.md} are older than the
accepted inventory, or if the extractor or pass-record ledger is written after
the digest manifest, then one of the bytes used by the theorem-ledger claim is
not among the bytes hashed by the manifest.  By
Theorem~\ref{thm:finite-coordinate-determination-boundary}, the stronger current
support claim cannot be inferred from a finite record that omits that coordinate.
Thus the old digest remains evidence only for the earlier local byte state and
not for the post-inventory support tuple stated here.
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[Submitted theorem-inventory availability]
\label{prop:submitted-theorem-inventory-availability}
If the external support-surface condition of
Corollary~\ref{cor:upload-or-archive-instantiation-record} is satisfied, then
\path{theorem_inventory.json} is an available submitted certificate coordinate
for the bounded theorem-obligation ledger claim of
Theorem~\ref{thm:theorem-obligation-ledger-boundary}.  If the exact bundle is not
uploaded with matching final digests, the theorem inventory has only local
diagnostic force and cannot support a submitted theorem-ledger claim in the
current package.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
The first sentence is the exact-bundle branch of
Theorem~\ref{thm:final-support-surface-closure} applied to the manifest-exposed
coordinate \path{theorem_inventory.json} and the ledger-exposed coordinate
\path{a7}.  In that branch, the file named by the manifest is available to the
reviewer as part of the submitted finite support surface, so it may be used for
the bounded theorem-ledger claim.  If the exact bundle is not supplied with
matching final digests, Corollary~\ref{cor:upload-or-archive-instantiation-record}
prevents all bundle-backed support claims from being externally instantiated for
this package.  The theorem inventory then remains a local file rather than a
submitted support coordinate, so it cannot carry the submitted theorem-ledger
claim.
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[Submission-support instantiation field normal form]
\label{prop:submission-support-instantiation-field-normal-form}
For the current source package, the upload-or-archive support coordinate has the
following complete finite field normal form:
\begin{center}
\small
\begin{tabularx}{\textwidth}{p{0.29\textwidth}X}
\toprule
field & current value\\
\midrule
support branch & conditional exact supplemental inclusion of \path{theorem_inventory.json}, \path{theorem_inventory.md}, and \path{review_bundle/}\\
upload receipt & absent\\
archive-equivalent locator & absent; public automath branch/path coordinate is candidate-only until byte equality is recorded\\
accepted external support status & instantiated only after exact upload or later archive-equivalent byte-equality record\\
\bottomrule
\end{tabularx}
\end{center}
Consequently the support-surface fields required by
Corollary~\ref{cor:upload-or-archive-instantiation-record} are not externally
instantiated in this package.  The public branch/path coordinate remains a
candidate-only locator; it satisfies the archive-equivalent branch only after a
finite byte-equality record shows that the public coordinate contains bytes
matching the final digest rows.  Until an exact upload receipt or such an archive
record is supplied under
Proposition~\ref{prop:upload-or-archive-instantiation-update-protocol},
bundle-backed claims may be read only as conditional local package-support
claims.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
The support branch is fixed by
Proposition~\ref{prop:final-package-shape-convention}: the selected supplement is
\path{main.tex}, the selected theorem ledger is \path{theorem_inventory.json},
and the selected review surface is exact inclusion of \path{review_bundle/} with
\path{review_bundle/FINAL_DIGESTS_SHA256.md}.  Theorem~\ref{thm:upload-or-archive-instantiation-boundary}
then identifies the extra coordinate needed to turn that local support surface
into an externally submitted one, namely either an exact upload receipt or an
archive-equivalent locator with byte equality.  Corollary~\ref{cor:current-no-receipt-support-surface-non-escalation}
and Proposition~\ref{prop:no-external-instantiation-from-local-bundle-records}
record that neither coordinate is present in the current source package.  The
bibliography and manifest name a candidate branch/path coordinate, but no finite
record in the current package proves that the public bytes equal the final digest
rows.  These finite statements determine all four fields in the displayed table, while
Proposition~\ref{prop:upload-or-archive-instantiation-update-protocol} specifies
the finite manuscript, manifest, bibliography, and digest updates needed to leave
this normal form in a later package.

If the last field were instead marked externally instantiated, the record would
contain neither of the two coordinates that Theorem~\ref{thm:upload-or-archive-instantiation-boundary}
requires.  That would add a support coordinate not present in the package,
contradicting Theorem~\ref{thm:finite-coordinate-determination-boundary}.  Hence
the only compatible normal form is the one displayed, and its consequence for
bundle-backed claims follows from
Corollary~\ref{cor:upload-or-archive-instantiation-record}.
\end{proof}

\begin{corollary}[Current local-support normal form]
\label{cor:current-local-support-normal-form}
For the current presentation package, the finite support state has exactly the
following normal form.  The theorem-inventory coordinate is accepted only through
the post-regeneration digest-and-verifier fixed point of
Proposition~\ref{prop:post-inventory-fixed-point-support-certificate}; the
review-bundle coordinate is externally instantiated only under exact upload or
matching public branch/path bytes, because
Proposition~\ref{prop:submission-support-instantiation-field-normal-form}
records no upload receipt and makes the archive-equivalent locator
byte-conditioned; and the venue and
bibliography coordinate is only the dated local-readiness record of
Proposition~\ref{prop:round-five-venue-record-instantiation}, not an upload-time
venue-compliance certificate.  Consequently every bundle-backed theorem-ledger,
case-evidence, verifier-log, source-interface, and primary-claim-inventory claim
in this package is a local/path-verified exact-inclusion claim unless a later
submitted support surface adds both an accepted upload-or-archive instantiation
record and, for venue compliance, an upload-time or unchanged-rule venue record.
\end{corollary}

\begin{proof}
The three named coordinates are disjoint finite fields of the support record.
For the theorem-inventory field,
Proposition~\ref{prop:post-inventory-fixed-point-support-certificate} gives the
necessary and sufficient local fixed point after the regenerated inventory files:
the current byte rows for \path{theorem_inventory.json} and
\path{theorem_inventory.md}, together with the three current finite-record
verifier logs, are the only support coordinates read by the exact-inclusion
ledger.  Replacing any of those rows by a pre-regeneration row leaves only the
manual local index and fails the exact-inclusion theorem-ledger claim.

For the submission-instantiation field,
Proposition~\ref{prop:submission-support-instantiation-field-normal-form} lists
the complete current values: conditional exact supplemental inclusion, absent
upload receipt, a digest-conditioned public branch/path locator, and external
support status only when exact upload or matching branch bytes are present.  By
Corollary~\ref{cor:current-no-receipt-support-surface-non-escalation}, no
bundle-local path can therefore be promoted to an unconditional public or
venue-submitted coordinate in this package.  For the venue field,
Proposition~\ref{prop:round-five-venue-record-instantiation} supplies only the
dated 2026-06-07 live route and bibliography check and explicitly excludes
venue upload, acceptance, proceedings status, archive minting, Lean rebuilds,
and artifact reruns.  Corollary~\ref{cor:historical-venue-record-non-escalation}
then prevents that dated record from being read as upload-time compliance unless
a later upload-time tuple or unchanged-rule statement is included.

The finite-coordinate determination theorem applies to the product of these
fields.  A support reading that adds external submission support without the
receipt-or-locator field, or adds upload-time venue compliance without the
required venue field, introduces a coordinate absent from the current record.
Thus the only current normal form is the local/path-verified exact-inclusion one
stated above; leaving it requires the finite update protocols already named in
the statement.
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[Review-bundle availability condition]
\label{audit:review-bundle-availability-condition}
The final support mode selected for the current presentation package is exact
supplemental inclusion of \path{review_bundle/}, conditional on the external
instantiation record of Corollary~\ref{cor:upload-or-archive-instantiation-record}, as recorded in
\path{review_bundle/FINAL_DIGESTS_SHA256.md} and in
Audit record~\ref{prop:final-immutable-package-state-certificate}.  This is a
conditional support-mode selection, not a completed venue-upload record or public
archive locator.  The source-interface, case-snapshot, primary-claim inventory,
submission-interface map, theorem-inventory, and verifier-record claims are
therefore reviewable at the path-verified evidence level stated in this
supplement only under the exact included bundle once the external instantiation
record exists.  A future archival equivalent would have to satisfy
Audit record~\ref{audit:review-bundle-archive-equivalence} and the common-field
update protocol of
Proposition~\ref{prop:upload-or-archive-instantiation-update-protocol}, but it is
not the current package branch.  If the bundle is not uploaded exactly with matching
final digests, then the primary note retains only the public-source citation to
the pinned newmath repository and the architectural claim in the short text; the
supplemental support claims depending on bundle-local files are not accepted by
this package.
\end{proposition}


\medskip
\begin{proof}
The bundle-dependent support claims are exactly the claims whose certificate
surface names files under \path{review_bundle/}.  Proposition~\ref{prop:machine-readable-certificate-records}
lists the schema, current pass-record ledger, submission-interface map,
primary-claim inventory, verifier script, and verifier log as bundle files.
Proposition~\ref{prop:reviewable-source-snapshot-interface} uses the bundled
source snapshot, and Proposition~\ref{prop:review-bundle-case-snapshot-coverage}
uses the bundled case snapshots.  Therefore, when \path{review_bundle/} is
actually included as exact supplemental material with matching final digests,
each such certificate surface lies in the submitted artifact boundary and is
locally checkable in the sense of the finite schema verifier.  Until the external
upload record specified in
Corollary~\ref{cor:upload-or-archive-instantiation-record} exists, these
bundle-backed statements are conditional support claims rather than independent
public-access claims.

If the bundle is absent, those bundle-local paths are no longer in the submitted
artifact boundary.  The definition of local checkability requires every named
path, manifest row, or log to lie in the submitted artifact, the supplemental
review bundle, or an explicit path-verified historical boundary.  No DOI or
institutional archive identifier is claimed in this package, so an omitted bundle
has no replacement surface unless an archival equivalent satisfying
Audit record~\ref{audit:review-bundle-archive-equivalence} is provided.  If neither
surface is present, the affected claims fail the evidence-surface check of
Proposition~\ref{prop:finite-certificate-schema-verifier}.  What remains is only
the public repository citation at the pinned commit and the short note's
architectural thesis, not the bundle-backed support certification.
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[Case-snapshot non-rerun boundary]
\label{audit:case-snapshot-non-rerun-boundary}
Under the hypotheses of
Propositions~\ref{prop:path-complete-case-witnesses} and
\ref{prop:review-bundle-case-snapshot-coverage}, the four case-study rows cannot
be read as fresh dynamic-behavior claims.  Any stronger reading asserting a fresh
publication-daemon execution, full-source rebuild, theorem-content rerun,
Rule110 dynamic rerun, or GroundCompiler semantic rerun requires command, source
state, environment, exit-code, and log-path fields in addition to the bundled
snapshots.  Without those fields the stronger reading is rejected by the finite
record verifier at its evidence-strength boundary.
\end{proposition}


\medskip
\begin{proof}
The propositions identify the complete evidence surface of the case rows:
thirteen local-path strings, thirteen reviewable bundle snapshots, and manifest
boundaries recording historical or path-verified evidence.  None of those case
rows contains the five command-run fields required by
Definition~\ref{def:operational-acceptance-interface}.  A fresh daemon,
full-source, theorem-content, Rule110, or GroundCompiler rerun is therefore a
strictly stronger evidence-level assertion than the accepted row supplies.  By
Proposition~\ref{prop:finite-certificate-schema-verifier} and
the schema-relative finite-record exactness theorem, Theorem~\ref{thm:record-acceptance-exactness}, the same certificate cannot accept
that stronger assertion: its boundary field $B$ records no command-run assertion,
and the missing command tuple is a finite bad subrecord.  Thus the snapshots are
rigidly limited to path-verified reviewability unless a separate rerun log is
added.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Finite-row closure of case evidence]
\label{thm:finite-row-case-evidence-closure}
Let $C$ be the finite set of four case-study rows in
Table~\ref{tab:case-witnesses}.  Under
Propositions~\ref{prop:path-complete-case-witnesses} and
\ref{prop:review-bundle-case-snapshot-coverage}, the publication-safety interface
accepts the case-study contribution exactly as the image of $C$ under the
skeleton map of Theorem~\ref{thm:case-witness-skeleton-rigidity}.  The image has
four possible values only: promotion, route/submission blocking, theorem-content
non-growth, and artifact-limitation disclosure.  For each row, the accepted
claim is finite-record evidence for the corresponding software-workflow lesson;
it is not evidence that the historical daemon, overlap checker, fake-extension
pipeline, Rule110 artifact system, or GroundCompiler evaluator has been rerun or
semantically validated.  A proposed upgrade of any row to such a rerun or
semantic-validation claim is accepted if and only if it adds the positive
extension coordinates required by
Theorem~\ref{thm:finite-extension-budget-normal-form}, including command, source
state, environment, exit code, and log path whenever fresh execution is asserted.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
Proposition~\ref{prop:path-complete-case-witnesses} gives, for every row in $C$,
the gate name, observed issue, safe lesson, and cited path surface.  Proposition~\ref{prop:review-bundle-case-snapshot-coverage}
places those cited path surfaces inside the reviewable snapshot boundary and
records that the boundary is historical or path-verified.  Applying
Theorem~\ref{thm:case-witness-skeleton-rigidity} therefore maps each row to one
of the four skeleton values listed in the statement, and no fifth skeleton is
available because the table contains only those four gate/lesson forms.

The finite-record interface theorem, Theorem~\ref{thm:publication-safety-interface},
then accepts only the certificate tuple carried by the row: its claim kind, path
surface, assigned pass rows, and boundary field.  By
Proposition~\ref{audit:case-snapshot-non-rerun-boundary}, that boundary field has
no command-run tuple for a fresh daemon execution, full-source rebuild,
theorem-content rerun, Rule110 rerun, or GroundCompiler semantic rerun.  Hence the
accepted image of $C$ is exactly finite-row evidence for the four safe workflow
lessons and not an empirical rerun or full artifact-validation claim.

Conversely, suppose a stronger reading of a case row is accepted.  It changes at
least one finite coordinate: the claim-kind coordinate for a semantic or artifact
validation assertion, the evidence-surface coordinate for a new run or semantic
property, the assigned-gate coordinate for the enlarged gate set, or the boundary
coordinate for command-run strength.  The finite extension-budget normal form,
Theorem~\ref{thm:finite-extension-budget-normal-form}, says that exactly these
positive coordinates must be added, and clause A6 of
Definition~\ref{def:certificate-schema-acceptance-table} supplies the command,
source-state, environment, exit-code, and log-path requirement for fresh
execution.  If any required coordinate is absent, the schema exactness theorem,
Theorem~\ref{thm:record-acceptance-exactness}, returns a finite bad subrecord
instead of accepting the upgrade.  This proves both finite-row closure and the
upgrade criterion.
\end{proof}
