\subsection{Command-run boundary}

\begin{proposition}[Command-run boundary inventory]
\label{audit:command-run-boundary-inventory}
For the current presentation package, Table~\ref{tab:command-boundary-inventory}
is complete for the command-boundary claims used by
Proposition~\ref{prop:machine-readable-certificate-records},
Proposition~\ref{audit:review-bundle-availability-condition},
Theorem~\ref{thm:theorem-obligation-ledger-boundary},
Theorem~\ref{thm:post-inventory-package-fixed-point}, and the finite-record
interface theorem, Theorem~\ref{thm:publication-safety-interface}.  The accepted
command-boundary claims in the package are exactly the three refreshed finite record-verifier invocations recorded in
\path{review_bundle/certificate_verification_run.log},
\path{review_bundle/source_interface_verification_run.log}, and
\path{review_bundle/primary_claim_inventory_freshness_2026-06-07.log}, the
refreshed theorem-environment extractor invocation recorded in
\path{review_bundle/theorem_environment_extraction_run.log}, the byte-level final
digest generation recorded in \path{review_bundle/final_digest_generation_run.log},
together with the dated live venue and bibliography freshness check recorded in
\path{review_bundle/VENUE_BIBLIOGRAPHY_LIVE_CHECK_2026-06-07.log}.  The
Lean/BEDC source theorem semantics, publication-daemon or route-state surfaces,
and Rule110/\allowbreak GroundCompiler artifact surfaces remain path-verified,
historical, or bounded-artifact evidence and are not command-run validation
claims.
\end{proposition}


\medskip
\begin{proof}
The evidence-level definition in
Definition~\ref{def:operational-acceptance-interface} requires a command-run row
to record command, immutable source state, environment, exit code, and log
path; otherwise the boundary field must say that no command-run assertion is
made.  The first six rows of Table~\ref{tab:command-boundary-inventory} contain
all five required command-run fields, read at the source-state level explicitly
stated in each row.  The first log records command
\path{python review_bundle/verify_certificate_records.py}, current local
checkout source state
\path{current local automath checkout plus review_bundle/FINAL_DIGESTS_SHA256.md},
\path{Python 3.10.11 on Windows 10}, exit code \path{0}, and refreshed log path
\path{review_bundle/certificate_verification_run.log}.  Its negative boundary is
schema-level certificate-record verification only, with no Lean, daemon, or
Rule110 rerun.  The second log records command
\path{python review_bundle/verify_source_interface_record.py}, current local
checkout source state and Python environment, exit code \path{0}, and log path
\path{review_bundle/source_interface_verification_run.log}.  Its negative
boundary is finite source-record comparison only: it compares table rows, JSON
records, manifest ranges, labels, digest, and line count, without validating the
external theorem semantics.  The third log records command
\path{python review_bundle/verify_primary_claim_inventory.py}, current local
checkout source state and Python environment, exit code \path{0}, and log path
\path{review_bundle/primary_claim_inventory_freshness_2026-06-07.log}.  Its
negative boundary is finite primary-inventory freshness only: it checks source
line spans, row shape, the \path{pc1}--\path{pc12} row id set, and
certificate-like pointers, without proving the semantics of the primary prose.
The fourth log records command
\path{python review_bundle/extract_theorem_environments.py}, current local
checkout source state and Python environment, exit code \path{0}, and log path
\path{review_bundle/theorem_environment_extraction_run.log}.  Its negative
boundary is syntactic theorem-label coverage only: it extracts the five
environment classes in $\mathcal E$ from \path{main.tex} and checks that their
labels occur in \path{theorem_inventory.json}, without proving theorem semantics
or validating external artifacts.  The fifth log records command
\path{powershell -NoProfile -Command regenerate review_bundle/FINAL_DIGESTS_SHA256.md from selected path table},
current local checkout after the inventory and retained verifier logs exist,
environment \path{PowerShell 5.1 on Windows; Python 3.10.11}, exit code
\path{0}, and log path \path{review_bundle/final_digest_generation_run.log}.
Its negative boundary is byte-level hashing only: it recomputes SHA--256 rows for
selected package paths, without certifying upload, archive minting, venue
acceptance, Lean rebuilds, daemon execution, or artifact validation.  The sixth
log records command \path{PowerShell Invoke-WebRequest against CICM
route pages and bibliography authority pages}, source state
\path{main.tex, submission_abstract.tex, references.bib, and review_bundle/FINAL_DIGESTS_SHA256.md at the recorded 2026-06-07 venue-check state},
environment \path{Windows PowerShell 5.1 on Windows; network enabled}, exit code
\path{0}, and log path
\path{review_bundle/VENUE_BIBLIOGRAPHY_LIVE_CHECK_2026-06-07.log}.  Its negative
boundary is live route and bibliography freshness only: it confirms reachability
of the CICM presentation-only call, EasyChair/LNCS interface pages, DOI or arXiv
locators, and the public source repository on 2026-06-07 local date, without certifying
venue acceptance, venue upload, archive minting, Lean rebuilds, or artifact
reruns.

The remaining three rows have no command field, no environment field, no exit
code, and no rerun log.  Their accepted surfaces are instead the bounded source
theorem interface at the pinned commit, the route and case-witness tables, and
the bounded artifact certificate $a_6$ with its manifest and review-bundle
snapshots.  Those are precisely the evidence surfaces used in the cited
propositions and in the finite certificate-construction theorem.  By the verifier rule, a row
lacking command-run fields can only be accepted at its declared path-verified,
historical, or bounded-artifact level.  Thus the table exhausts the
command-boundary inventory and prevents the non-rerun Lean, daemon, Rule110, and
external source-theorem semantics surfaces from being promoted by prose into
fresh execution claims.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Post-edit rerun metadata gate]
\label{thm:post-edit-rerun-metadata-gate}
Let $I$ be the pair consisting of \path{theorem_inventory.json} and
\path{theorem_inventory.md}, let $L$ be the tuple of retained extractor and
finite-record verifier logs named in Table~\ref{tab:command-boundary-inventory},
and let $D$ be \path{review_bundle/FINAL_DIGESTS_SHA256.md}.  After any edit to
\path{main.tex}, $I$, or a retained support record, a claim that the package has
a post-inventory fixed point for the new bytes is accepted only if the submitted
surface also contains, for each newly claimed rerun, its command, source commit
or source-state description, environment, exit code, and log path, and if $D$ is
regenerated after those logs exist.  If those metadata rows are absent, the same
source text has only the conditional reading of
Theorem~\ref{thm:post-inventory-package-fixed-point}: the fixed point is the
finite coordinate that would be obtained by the ordered rerun, not a current-byte
execution certificate for the edited package.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
The operational evidence interface,
Definition~\ref{def:operational-acceptance-interface}, makes command-run status
a finite coordinate: the command, source state, environment, exit code, and log
path are part of the accepted record.  The digest-closure refresh criterion,
Theorem~\ref{thm:digest-closure-refresh-criterion}, makes the byte digest another
finite coordinate: after an inventory or support-record byte changes, the old
digest row names the old byte string and cannot identify the edited state.

The tuple $(I,L,D)$ is therefore ordered data, not a self-updating invariant.  If
the edited package includes fresh rows for the extractor, the retained
finite-record verifiers, any retained dated venue check, and the final digest
generator, then Theorem~\ref{thm:post-inventory-package-fixed-point} applies to
those rows and supplies the fixed point.  If one of the metadata fields or the
post-log digest regeneration is missing, two packages can agree on the manuscript
prose while differing on the command actually run, the source state used, the
environment, the exit status, the log bytes, or the final digest bytes.  By
Theorem~\ref{thm:finite-coordinate-determination-boundary}, the stronger
current-byte rerun claim is then not determined by the submitted surface; the
missing command or digest field is the finite bad subrecord.  What remains is
only the conditional finite-record statement, namely that such a fixed point is
available once the ordered rerun metadata and final digest coordinate are
supplied.
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[Final four-interface maximality and support-state reconciliation]
\label{prop:final-local-support-state-reconciliation}
For the source package after the present manuscript edits, the finite support
state used by the publication-safety theorem chain is the following reconciled
local state and no stronger one.  First, the files
\path{theorem_inventory.json} and \path{theorem_inventory.md} are accepted as a
post-inventory fixed point only under the ordered metadata condition of
Theorem~\ref{thm:post-edit-rerun-metadata-gate}; absent such post-edit rows, the
fixed-point statements are conditional rerun criteria rather than current-byte
execution certificates.  Second, each command-run source state is the state
recorded in the corresponding log row of Table~\ref{tab:command-boundary-inventory};
historical rows are not claims that every later editor checkout has the same Git
\path{HEAD} or digest bytes.  Third, because the source package records neither
an upload receipt nor an archive-equivalent locator, bundle-backed support
remains conditional on the external instantiation record of
Corollary~\ref{cor:upload-or-archive-instantiation-record}.

Moreover the same finite state is maximal simultaneously for the four boundary
interfaces on which the presentation route depends:
\begin{enumerate}
\item the imported BEDC/newmath discovery-gate source interface is exactly the
bounded path, commit, line-range, label, and summary interface of
Lemma~\ref{lem:source-interface-extraction-correctness} and
Theorem~\ref{thm:bounded-source-interface-maximality}; a full source-verification
reading requires fresh Lean or BEDC build records, axiom-purity records, and
source-marker or labelled-statement existence logs;
\item the publication-safety and current-package claims are exactly the
finite-record certificates of Theorems~\ref{thm:publication-safety-interface}
and~\ref{thm:current-package-certificate-exactness} under the explicitly supplied
record-gate soundness interface, not implementation soundness of the gates;
\item the four case-study rows are exactly the path-complete historical snapshot
lessons classified by Theorem~\ref{thm:case-witness-skeleton-rigidity} and closed
by Theorem~\ref{thm:finite-row-case-evidence-closure}; current operational
reproducibility of any daemon, overlap checker, Rule110 system, GroundCompiler
evaluator, or artifact suite requires the corresponding rerun log; and
\item the theorem-obligation inventory is exactly the syntactic labelled
environment coordinate on \path{main.tex} proved by
Theorem~\ref{thm:deterministic-theorem-environment-extractor-boundary} and
Theorem~\ref{thm:extractor-domain-exactness-non-gluing}; non-main \TeX{} files,
snapshot excerpts, and auxiliary tables are supporting or background material
unless separately brought into the extractor domain, and the coordinate is not a
proof-correctness, semantic-theorem-completeness, Lean/BEDC, daemon, artifact, or
venue certificate.
\end{enumerate}
Any stronger reading of one of these four interfaces is accepted by the
publication-safety theorem chain if and only if the submitted support state adds
the positive finite coordinates named in the relevant item and the assigned pass
records required by Theorem~\ref{thm:finite-extension-budget-normal-form}; if a
required coordinate is absent, the attempted upgrade has an inclusion-minimal
finite bad subrecord in the sense of
Theorem~\ref{thm:finite-certificate-obstruction-skeleton}.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
The first assertion is exactly the fixed-point criterion specialized in
Proposition~\ref{prop:post-inventory-fixed-point-support-certificate} and
realized by Theorem~\ref{thm:post-inventory-finite-record-realization}: the two
inventory paths and the verifier-log paths are finite byte coordinates of the
selected exact-inclusion support surface.  If any of those rows is stale,
Theorem~\ref{thm:finite-coordinate-determination-boundary} rejects the stronger
exact-inclusion claim and leaves only the local index reading.

For the second assertion, Proposition~\ref{audit:command-run-boundary-inventory}
classifies the command-boundary table.  A row with command, source state,
environment, exit code, and log path is accepted only at the evidence level
stated in that row.  Hence a historical source-state field certifies the logged
finite command result, not an equality between the final editor workspace and the
historical commit.  This is the reconciliation theorem
Theorem~\ref{thm:workspace-final-package-reconciliation} applied to the refreshed
log rows.

For the third assertion,
Proposition~\ref{prop:submission-support-instantiation-field-normal-form} lists
the upload receipt and archive-equivalent locator fields as absent in the current
package.  Corollary~\ref{cor:current-no-receipt-support-surface-non-escalation}
therefore forbids promoting a bundle-local path into an unconditional venue or
public support coordinate.  Combining the three finite fields gives precisely
the local reconciled support state stated above, and no additional source-state,
upload, archive, Lean, daemon, or artifact-rerun coordinate is introduced.

It remains to prove simultaneous maximality for the four interfaces.  For the
source interface, Lemma~\ref{lem:source-interface-extraction-correctness} fixes
the manual record fields, Proposition~\ref{prop:source-interface-evidence-level-separation}
separates those fields from stronger formal-source evidence levels, and
Theorem~\ref{thm:bounded-source-interface-maximality} proves maximality for the
submitted source surface.  A fresh Lean or BEDC rebuild, axiom-purity assertion,
or source-marker existence assertion reads a command, formal-build, axiom-audit,
or marker-coordinate not present in the bounded line-range interface.  Hence the
finite-coordinate theorem,
Theorem~\ref{thm:finite-coordinate-determination-boundary}, forbids deriving it
from the submitted fields alone, while
Theorem~\ref{thm:finite-extension-budget-normal-form} gives the positive
direction after the missing records and pass rows are added.

For the safety interface, Definition~\ref{def:record-gate-soundness-external-interface}
and Proposition~\ref{prop:record-gate-soundness-external-interface-exactness}
fix record-gate soundness as an external finite input/output promise.  The finite
publication theorem, Theorem~\ref{thm:publication-safety-interface}, and the
current package theorem, Theorem~\ref{thm:current-package-certificate-exactness},
use that promise only to construct and identify finite publication certificates.
Theorems~\ref{thm:external-interface-projection-no-free-upgrade} and
\ref{thm:current-package-extension-coordinate-rigidity} classify an
implementation-soundness reading as a proper extension requiring gate-source,
command, environment, exit-code, and log coordinates.  Thus the stated finite
safety chain is not demoted: it remains the maximal accepted theorem chain at the
record level, and the stronger software-verification theorem is accepted exactly
when its additional executable gate records are supplied.

For the case rows, Proposition~\ref{prop:path-complete-case-witnesses} provides
the finite path-complete row surface, Theorem~\ref{thm:case-witness-skeleton-rigidity}
classifies each row into one of the four skeleton lessons, and
Theorem~\ref{thm:finite-row-case-evidence-closure} proves closure under the
finite-record interface.  A current operational reproduction claim changes the
command-boundary coordinate: it asks for a fresh daemon, overlap-checker,
Rule110, GroundCompiler, or artifact-suite run rather than for the historical
snapshot lesson.  Proposition~\ref{audit:case-snapshot-non-rerun-boundary} shows
that the required command tuple is absent, so the obstruction skeleton returns
that missing tuple as a finite bad subrecord unless the rerun log and assigned
pass rows are added.

For the theorem-inventory coordinate,
Theorem~\ref{thm:deterministic-theorem-environment-extractor-boundary} proves the
deterministic labelled-environment extraction predicate, and
Theorem~\ref{thm:extractor-domain-exactness-non-gluing} proves that the domain is
exactly the five displayed theorem-like environments in \path{main.tex}.  The
manual boundary proposition,
Proposition~\ref{prop:manual-theorem-inventory-boundary}, separately identifies
non-main \TeX{} material as manual supporting or background material.  Therefore
semantic theorem completeness, proof correctness, non-main-file coverage, Lean or
BEDC rebuilding, daemon execution, artifact validation, and venue facts all read
coordinates outside the extractor predicate.  The same finite-coordinate and
extension-budget theorems then give non-escalation when those coordinates are
absent and acceptance when they are explicitly added with assigned pass rows.

In all four cases the stronger reading preorder of
Definition~\ref{def:stronger-reading-preorder} is strict exactly when the reading
adds one of the named coordinates.  If the coordinate is absent,
Theorem~\ref{thm:finite-certificate-obstruction-skeleton} supplies an
inclusion-minimal finite bad subrecord; if it is present with the required pass
rows, Theorem~\ref{thm:finite-extension-budget-normal-form} supplies the upgraded
finite certificate.  This proves the advertised simultaneous maximality and the
``if and only if'' upgrade criterion.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Machine-checkable support-coordinate fixed-point normal form]
\label{thm:submission-support-fixed-point-normal-form}
For the current source package, the support state for the three externalized
claims in the publication-safety theorem chain is exactly the following finite
normal form.  This is a bookkeeping theorem for the machine-checkable support
coordinates used by the bounded audit interface: it records when byte strings,
logs, and digest rows are mutually current, and it is not an independent
semantic invariant of the paper, the artifacts, or the venue process.
\begin{enumerate}
\item The theorem-inventory coordinate is accepted only after
\path{theorem_inventory.json} and \path{theorem_inventory.md} have been written,
the theorem-environment extractor and finite-record verifiers have been rerun on
that post-inventory state, and
\path{review_bundle/FINAL_DIGESTS_SHA256.md} has been regenerated over those
bytes and logs.
\item Bundle-backed support is local/path-verified only unless the exact
\path{review_bundle/} directory is uploaded with matching final digests or an
archive-equivalent locator satisfying
Audit record~\ref{audit:review-bundle-archive-equivalence} is supplied.
\item Venue/page-limit compliance is only a dated local-readiness observation
unless a near-submission upload-time venue record, or an included unchanged-rule
statement tying the upload to the latest dated venue record, is supplied.
\end{enumerate}
Consequently no theorem-ledger, bundle-availability, or upload-time venue claim
may be read in the stronger submitted-support sense unless the corresponding
finite coordinate in this normal form is present.  The only mathematical role of
this theorem in the article is to close the support-coordinate input required by
Theorems~\ref{thm:publication-safety-interface} and
\ref{thm:current-package-certificate-exactness}; later corollaries in this
subsection are projections of this same finite byte-level closure.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
For the theorem-inventory coordinate, the digest table is a function of finite
bytes.  If \path{theorem_inventory.json} or \path{theorem_inventory.md} is
created or changed after that table is computed, its byte string is not an input
to the recorded hash row.  The fixed-point closure theorem,
Theorem~\ref{thm:final-support-surface-closure}, the post-inventory certificate
Proposition~\ref{prop:post-inventory-fixed-point-support-certificate}, and the
realization theorem
Theorem~\ref{thm:post-inventory-finite-record-realization}, therefore accept the
inventory coordinate exactly after the inventory files and their verifier logs
precede the final digest pass.  This proves the first clause and prevents stale
digest rows from supporting the exact-inclusion theorem-ledger claim.

For bundle-backed support,
Proposition~\ref{prop:submission-support-instantiation-field-normal-form}
records no current upload receipt and records the archive-equivalent locator only
as a digest-conditioned public branch/path coordinate.  By
Corollary~\ref{cor:current-no-receipt-support-surface-non-escalation} and
Proposition~\ref{audit:review-bundle-availability-condition}, a local path under
\path{review_bundle/} is therefore not an external reviewer-available coordinate
unless the exact-upload record is present or the public coordinate contains the
matching digest-identified bytes.  This proves the second clause.

For venue compliance, the venue facts are external facts rather than internal
source definitions.  Theorem~\ref{thm:venue-rule-freshness-non-escalation} and
Corollary~\ref{cor:upload-time-venue-compliance-record-requirement} classify the
current venue logs as dated readiness records and require either a later
upload-time tuple or an unchanged-rule statement before the stronger upload-time
claim is available.  This proves the third clause.  The three fields are
independent coordinates of the finite package state, so adding a stronger
reading in any one field without its named coordinate is exactly the prohibited
coordinate extension ruled out by
Theorem~\ref{thm:finite-coordinate-determination-boundary}.  Hence the displayed
normal form is exact.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Post-inventory support-coordinate dichotomy]
\label{thm:post-inventory-support-coordinate-dichotomy}
For the current presentation package there are exactly two possible support
readings for any claim whose evidence surface names \path{theorem_inventory.json},
\path{theorem_inventory.md}, or a file under \path{review_bundle/}.  If the
ordered post-inventory pass of Theorem~\ref{thm:post-inventory-package-fixed-point}
has been run and the exact resulting surface is externally instantiated by the
upload-or-archive record of
Corollary~\ref{cor:upload-or-archive-instantiation-record}, then the claim is a
submitted bundle-backed support claim at the finite-record evidence level stated
in its certificate.  In every other case it is only a local/path-verified or
dated-readiness claim, with the further fallback of
Theorem~\ref{thm:submission-support-fixed-point-normal-form} if the
post-inventory pass is not closed.  No intermediate reading is available in
which refreshed local digests alone supply an external support coordinate, or in
which an upload-or-archive locator alone supplies post-inventory verifier and
digest closure.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
Let $c$ be a claim whose evidence surface names one of the stated paths.  The
finite-coordinate determination boundary,
Theorem~\ref{thm:finite-coordinate-determination-boundary}, allows $c$ to be
read only from finite fields present in the current package state.  The first
field is the post-inventory closure field.  By
Theorem~\ref{thm:post-inventory-package-fixed-point}, that field is present
exactly when the inventory files are written, the theorem-environment extractor
and finite-record verifiers are rerun on that post-inventory state, any retained
dated route-readiness log is fixed, and the final SHA--256 manifest is generated
after those bytes exist.  If this field is absent, the first clause of
Theorem~\ref{thm:submission-support-fixed-point-normal-form} permits only the
local index or dated-readiness fallback and forbids the exact theorem-ledger
support reading.

The second field is external instantiation of the selected support surface.  By
Corollary~\ref{cor:upload-or-archive-instantiation-record} and
Proposition~\ref{audit:review-bundle-availability-condition}, this field is
present only when either the exact supplemental directory with the matching final
digest manifest is uploaded, or an archive-equivalent replacement satisfying
Audit record~\ref{audit:review-bundle-archive-equivalence} is supplied.  If this
field is absent, Corollary~\ref{cor:current-no-receipt-support-surface-non-escalation}
keeps every bundle-backed support claim local/path-verified and prevents it from
becoming an unconditional public or venue-submitted support coordinate.

Thus the support state is the product of two independent finite fields: local
post-inventory closure and external support instantiation.  When both are
present, the submitted bundle-backed reading follows from the exact support
surface closure theorem, Theorem~\ref{thm:final-support-surface-closure}.  If the
first field is missing, the verifier/digest fixed point is not closed; if the
second is missing, the closed local surface has not been externally instantiated.
In either missing-field case, promoting $c$ to a submitted bundle-backed claim
would add a finite coordinate not contained in the package.  Theorem~\ref{thm:finite-coordinate-determination-boundary}
rejects that promotion, and the only remaining readings are precisely the
local/path-verified or dated-readiness readings already classified above.
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[Final refreshed-coordinate instantiation]
\label{prop:final-refreshed-coordinate-instantiation}
For the current dated local-readiness package, the first coordinate of
Theorem~\ref{thm:submission-support-fixed-point-normal-form} is instantiated by
the finite tuple consisting of \path{theorem_inventory.json},
\path{theorem_inventory.md}, \path{review_bundle/theorem_environment_extraction_run.log},
\path{review_bundle/certificate_verification_run.log},
\path{review_bundle/source_interface_verification_run.log},
\path{review_bundle/primary_claim_inventory_freshness_2026-06-07.log},
\path{review_bundle/VENUE_BIBLIOGRAPHY_LIVE_CHECK_2026-06-07.log}, and
\path{review_bundle/FINAL_DIGESTS_SHA256.md}, provided these files are submitted
with the exact SHA-256 rows printed in the digest manifest.  This instantiation
does not add an upload receipt, DOI, venue acceptance record, fresh Lean rebuild,
daemon run, or artifact rerun; the archive-equivalent locator remains the public
branch/path coordinate and is effective only with matching digest rows.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
The tuple is finite, and each named component is one of the coordinates already
classified by Proposition~\ref{prop:post-inventory-fixed-point-support-certificate},
Theorem~\ref{thm:post-inventory-finite-record-realization},
Proposition~\ref{audit:command-run-boundary-inventory}, and
Theorem~\ref{thm:submission-support-fixed-point-normal-form}.  The two theorem
inventory files are the post-inventory byte coordinates.  The theorem-environment
log is the syntactic coverage coordinate for those files.  The two finite-record
verifier logs and the primary-claim verifier log are the schema-relative,
source-interface, and primary-surface coordinates.  The
venue/bibliography log is the dated external-route locator coordinate.  The
digest manifest is the finite hash table selecting the bytes of the preceding
coordinates.

If the submitted files have the printed hashes, then equality of SHA-256 rows is
exactly the package-state equality relation used by
Theorem~\ref{thm:workspace-final-package-reconciliation}; hence the first normal
form coordinate is present.  If any named file is omitted, changed after the
digest pass, or replaced by a file with a different row, then the finite tuple no
longer equals the selected coordinate and the post-inventory claim falls back to
the weaker local or historical reading described in
Proposition~\ref{prop:regenerated-theorem-inventory-digest-closure}.  The final
sentence follows from Corollary~\ref{cor:current-no-receipt-support-surface-non-escalation}
and Corollary~\ref{cor:upload-time-venue-compliance-record-requirement}: none of
the listed files is an upload receipt, DOI, venue acceptance, Lean rebuild,
daemon execution, or artifact-rerun record; the branch/path locator is already
classified separately by the support-surface normal form.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Post-inventory support-coordinate fixed point]
\label{thm:post-inventory-package-fixed-point}
Let $P$ be the current local package state consisting of the manuscript source,
\path{submission_abstract.tex}, \path{theorem_inventory.json},
\path{theorem_inventory.md}, and \path{review_bundle/}.  Suppose that the following
finite sequence is executed in the displayed order:
\begin{enumerate}
\item write the theorem-inventory files for the manuscript source state;
\item rerun \path{python review_bundle/extract_theorem_environments.py};
\item rerun the finite-record verifiers named in
Table~\ref{tab:command-boundary-inventory};
\item rerun the dated venue/bibliography locator check, if a dated route-readiness
claim is retained; and
\item write \path{review_bundle/final_digest_generation_run.log} as the command
metadata for the hash pass and regenerate
\path{review_bundle/FINAL_DIGESTS_SHA256.md} from the resulting bytes of the
inventory files, verifier logs, dated venue log, and selected review-bundle files
other than the digest manifest and this generation log.
\end{enumerate}
Then, at the same bookkeeping level as
Theorem~\ref{thm:submission-support-fixed-point-normal-form}, the support coordinate selected by
Proposition~\ref{prop:final-refreshed-coordinate-instantiation} is a fixed point
of the finite post-inventory verifier/digest pass: every file used to justify the
theorem-ledger, finite-verifier, and dated-readiness claims is an input to the
final digest manifest, the digest-generation command has its own acyclic command,
source-state, environment, exit-code, and log-path row in
Table~\ref{tab:command-boundary-inventory}, and the manifest is produced after
those support inputs exist.
Conversely, if any one of the five ordered steps is omitted or performed after the
digest regeneration, the stronger exact-inclusion theorem-ledger claim is not
available; the manuscript retains only the local path-verified or dated-readiness
reading classified in
Theorem~\ref{thm:submission-support-fixed-point-normal-form}.  In either case the
fixed point supplies no external upload receipt, archive-equivalent locator,
upload-time venue-compliance record, fresh Lean/BEDC rebuild, marker-resolution
audit, axiom-purity audit, daemon run, or artifact-semantic rerun.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
The package state $P$ is finite because it is a finite list of relative paths and
finite byte strings.  The extractor in the second step reads
\path{main.tex} and \path{theorem_inventory.json}; its stated boundary is
syntactic theorem-like environment extraction and inventory-label coverage only.
The finite-record verifiers in the third step read the schema, pass-record,
primary-claim, submission-interface, source-interface, manifest, and source
snapshot records named in Table~\ref{tab:command-boundary-inventory}.  The dated
venue/bibliography check, when retained, reads external route and locator pages
and writes a dated log with the explicit boundary that it is not acceptance,
upload, archive minting, Lean rebuild, daemon execution, or artifact rerun
evidence.  The final digest pass records the command, source state, environment,
exit code, generated manifest, and log path in
\path{review_bundle/final_digest_generation_run.log}, and computes the SHA--256
table from the already-existing support inputs.  The table deliberately excludes
\path{review_bundle/FINAL_DIGESTS_SHA256.md} and
\path{review_bundle/final_digest_generation_run.log} as hashed inputs, avoiding a
self-digest recursion; those two files are instead the manifest and the command
metadata for producing it.

For the forward implication, the ordering makes every load-bearing finite record
that supports the theorem-ledger coordinate an already-existing byte string before
the digest table is computed.  Hence the digest rows name the same inventory,
extractor-log, verifier-log, dated-venue-log, and selected bundle bytes that are cited in
Proposition~\ref{prop:final-refreshed-coordinate-instantiation}.  By
Theorem~\ref{thm:final-support-surface-closure}, exact finite support is precisely
equality of the selected paths and their final digest rows.  The digest-generator
row in Table~\ref{tab:command-boundary-inventory} supplies the command-result
ledger required for the final hash pass itself without requiring the log to be a
hashed input to its own output, so the first coordinate of
Theorem~\ref{thm:submission-support-fixed-point-normal-form} is closed under the
post-inventory verifier/digest pass without borrowing a pre-inventory or
unlogged command result.

For the converse, suppose a step is omitted or run after digest regeneration.  If
the inventory files are missing or stale, the extractor and inventory coverage
claim has no post-inventory input.  If the extractor or verifier logs are missing
or stale, the package has no command-result record with command, source state,
environment, exit code, and log path for the relevant finite comparison.  If a
dated venue-readiness claim is retained but the dated locator check is missing or
stale, the venue statement has no current dated-readiness coordinate.  If the
digest manifest is produced before any of these inputs exists or after they later
change, at least one cited byte string is not the byte string selected by its
final SHA--256 row.  In every case the stronger exact-inclusion coordinate would
require adding a finite record not selected by the manifest, which is forbidden by
Theorem~\ref{thm:finite-coordinate-determination-boundary}.  The fallbacks are
exactly the local/path-verified and dated-readiness readings stated in
Theorem~\ref{thm:submission-support-fixed-point-normal-form}.

Finally, none of the five steps contains the finite fields required for the other
evidence axes.  Upload and archive support require the records of
Corollary~\ref{cor:upload-or-archive-instantiation-record}; upload-time venue
compliance requires the tuple of
Corollary~\ref{cor:upload-time-venue-compliance-record-requirement}; and fresh
formal-source, daemon, and artifact-semantic claims require the command/source,
environment, exit-code, and log coordinates isolated in
Proposition~\ref{audit:command-run-boundary-inventory}.  Since those coordinates
are not created by the post-inventory fixed-point pass, no such stronger claim is
proved.
\end{proof}

\begin{corollary}[Current post-inventory realization]
\label{cor:current-post-inventory-realization}
Assume that the present source state contains the two submitted
inventory files \path{theorem_inventory.json} and \path{theorem_inventory.md}.
If, after those two files exist, the theorem-environment extractor, the retained
finite-record verifiers, the retained dated venue/bibliography check, and the
final SHA--256 manifest are run in the order displayed in
Theorem~\ref{thm:post-inventory-package-fixed-point}, then the theorem-ledger
row $a_7$ of Table~\ref{tab:current-acceptance-ledger} is instantiated at exactly
the finite support level of
Theorem~\ref{thm:submission-support-fixed-point-normal-form}.  In particular the
instantiated row certifies only syntactic inventory coverage, finite-record
schema acceptance, retained dated-readiness logging, and byte-level digest
closure; it does not certify theorem semantics, upload, archive replacement,
venue acceptance, fresh source-theory rebuilds, or dynamic artifact validation.
\end{corollary}

\begin{proof}
The first hypothesis supplies the missing finite coordinates selected in
Proposition~\ref{prop:final-package-shape-convention} and in the added row
$a_7$ of Theorem~\ref{thm:nested-package-states}.  The ordered post-write command
sequence is precisely the sequence required in
Theorem~\ref{thm:post-inventory-package-fixed-point}.  Applying that theorem
therefore makes the resulting digest table a fixed point over the inventory
bytes and the verifier/log bytes that are used by the theorem-ledger claim.

It remains to identify the strength of the instantiated support row.  The
normal-form theorem, Theorem~\ref{thm:submission-support-fixed-point-normal-form},
classifies the post-inventory coordinate as a finite local support coordinate:
inventory coverage is syntactic, verifier acceptance is schema-relative, dated
venue evidence is local-readiness evidence only, and digest closure is a
byte-string equality surface.  The exclusions listed in the last sentence are
exactly the non-escalation exclusions in that theorem and in
Theorem~\ref{thm:post-inventory-package-fixed-point}.  Hence the current
inventory row is realized, but only at the stated finite support level.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Current inventory support-coordinate realization]
\label{thm:current-inventory-artifact-realization}
For the present Stage--A source state, after the final theorem-inventory bytes
for this manuscript have been accepted, suppose that the files
\path{theorem_inventory.json} and \path{theorem_inventory.md} are present as
finite package coordinates and that, after those bytes exist, the following
records are freshly regenerated from the same source state: the syntactic
theorem-environment extraction log
\path{review_bundle/theorem_environment_extraction_run.log}, the finite
certificate verifier log \path{review_bundle/certificate_verification_run.log},
the source-interface verifier log
\path{review_bundle/source_interface_verification_run.log}, the primary-claim
inventory freshness log
\path{review_bundle/primary_claim_inventory_freshness_2026-06-07.log}, any retained dated
venue/bibliography check log, and the final digest table
\path{review_bundle/FINAL_DIGESTS_SHA256.md}.  Then the two missing support-coordinate
obligations labelled \path{post-inventory-artifact-realization} and
\path{fresh-post-inventory-verifier-and-digest-logs} are discharged exactly at
the support level of
Theorem~\ref{thm:post-inventory-package-fixed-point}: the inventory files are
selected finite coordinates, the extractor and finite-record verifiers are
post-inventory command records, and the digest table names the resulting bytes.
This realization is only a finite byte/log/digest realization and does not assert extractor semantic completeness, theorem-proof
correctness, venue acceptance, upload, archive replacement, fresh Lean/BEDC
rebuilds, daemon execution, or dynamic artifact validation.
If the inventory files are changed after the extractor or digest pass, the same
statement is no longer current; it reverts to the conditional/historical reading
until the displayed rerun sequence is repeated against the changed bytes.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
The first hypothesis supplies the two finite byte coordinates that were absent
from the pre-run support surface.  The theorem-environment extractor then reads
\path{main.tex} together with \path{theorem_inventory.json}; by
Theorem~\ref{thm:deterministic-theorem-environment-extractor-boundary}, an
exit-code-zero refreshed log proves precisely syntactic label coverage in the
five-environment domain and proves no semantic theorem property.  The certificate
and source-interface verifiers are finite programs of the kind isolated in
Theorem~\ref{thm:concrete-finite-script-implementation-soundness} and
Proposition~\ref{audit:command-run-boundary-inventory}; rerunning them after the
inventory bytes exist makes their command, environment, exit-code, and log-path
records post-inventory records rather than stale pre-inventory records.  The
retained dated venue/bibliography check, if used, contributes only the dated
readiness coordinate classified in
Theorem~\ref{thm:submission-support-fixed-point-normal-form}.

Finally, regenerating \path{review_bundle/FINAL_DIGESTS_SHA256.md} after those
files and logs exist makes the digest table a deterministic SHA--256 function of
the same finite byte strings used by the inventory and verifier claims.  This is
exactly the ordered fixed-point construction of
Theorem~\ref{thm:post-inventory-package-fixed-point}, so the two named issue
obligations are realized as local finite-record support coordinates.  The
negative part follows from the same fixed-point theorem and from
Theorem~\ref{thm:finite-coordinate-determination-boundary}: none of the listed
finite rows contains the fields required for semantic proof checking, external
publication, source-theory rebuilds, daemon runs, or artifact-semantic reruns.
If an inventory byte changes later, the extractor log and final digest table no
longer name the changed byte string.  The fixed-point theorem then applies only
to the earlier byte tuple, so the current claim is recovered only by rerunning
the sequence and regenerating the digest rows.
\end{proof}

\begin{corollary}[Current post-inventory support tuple instantiation]
\label{cor:current-post-inventory-support-tuple-instantiation}
In the current source package, after \path{theorem_inventory.json} and
\path{theorem_inventory.md} have been written, the ordered tuple consisting of \path{theorem_inventory.json},
\path{theorem_inventory.md},
\path{review_bundle/theorem_environment_extraction_run.log},
\path{review_bundle/certificate_verification_run.log},
\path{review_bundle/source_interface_verification_run.log},
\path{review_bundle/primary_claim_inventory_freshness_2026-06-07.log}, and
\path{review_bundle/FINAL_DIGESTS_SHA256.md} instantiates the first coordinate of
Theorem~\ref{thm:submission-support-fixed-point-normal-form} exactly when the
extractor and finite-record verifiers are rerun after the inventory files exist
and the final digest table is regenerated after those logs exist.  The resulting
coordinate is a finite local support tuple only; it is not theorem semantics,
venue submission, archive replacement, Lean/BEDC rebuild evidence, daemon
evidence, or dynamic artifact-validation evidence.
\end{corollary}

\begin{proof}
The two inventory files are the finite byte coordinates whose absence blocks the
post-inventory fixed-point claim.  Once they exist, the theorem-environment
extractor reads \path{main.tex} and \path{theorem_inventory.json}, and the
certificate and source-interface verifiers read the finite records specified in
their scripts and in Proposition~\ref{audit:command-run-boundary-inventory}.
Running these commands after the inventory write therefore produces command-log
bytes whose input side includes the inventory state rather than a pre-inventory
state.

If \path{review_bundle/FINAL_DIGESTS_SHA256.md} is then regenerated after those
logs exist, each member of the displayed tuple is selected by a post-inventory
byte row.  Theorem~\ref{thm:post-inventory-package-fixed-point} says precisely
that this ordered construction closes the first coordinate of the normal form in
Theorem~\ref{thm:submission-support-fixed-point-normal-form}, and
Proposition~\ref{prop:final-refreshed-coordinate-instantiation} identifies the
same paths as the refreshed finite support tuple.  Hence the tuple instantiates
the current theorem-ledger support coordinate.

Conversely, if the inventory write, either verifier rerun, or digest
regeneration is missing or out of order, at least one tuple component is not a
post-inventory byte selected by the final digest manifest.  The exact-inclusion
coordinate would then require an unstated finite row, which is excluded by
Theorem~\ref{thm:finite-coordinate-determination-boundary}.  The final sentence
is the non-escalation boundary already proved in
Theorem~\ref{thm:post-inventory-package-fixed-point} and
Theorem~\ref{thm:submission-support-fixed-point-normal-form}.
\end{proof}

\begin{corollary}[Byte-level stable replay criterion for the post-inventory tuple]
\label{cor:stable-replay-post-inventory-tuple}
Let $S$ be a source state of the present presentation package in which
the final accepted \path{theorem_inventory.json} and \path{theorem_inventory.md}
exist.  Suppose
that the theorem-environment extractor, certificate-record verifier,
source-interface verifier, and retained primary-claim verifier are run from $S$,
that their logs have exit code zero, and that
\path{review_bundle/FINAL_DIGESTS_SHA256.md} is regenerated after those logs are
written.  Then every later support claim for the first coordinate of
Theorem~\ref{thm:submission-support-fixed-point-normal-form} factors through the
finite tuple of Corollary~\ref{cor:current-post-inventory-support-tuple-instantiation}:
the claim is valid for $S$ exactly as a byte-level replay of that tuple, and any
stronger claim must add a new command-result coordinate of the kind listed in
Proposition~\ref{audit:command-run-boundary-inventory}.  If either inventory file
is later rewritten, this replay criterion applies only historically to the old
tuple until the extractor/verifier/digest sequence is run again from the new
source state.
\end{corollary}

\begin{proof}
The hypotheses reproduce the ordered construction required by
Theorem~\ref{thm:post-inventory-package-fixed-point}.  Since the inventory files
exist before the four retained commands are run, the resulting logs are
post-inventory command records.  Since the digest table is regenerated after the
logs are written, the same finite byte strings are also selected by the final
SHA--256 surface.  Corollary~\ref{cor:current-post-inventory-support-tuple-instantiation}
therefore identifies exactly the support coordinate available in $S$.

Now consider any later claim that cites the first coordinate of
Theorem~\ref{thm:submission-support-fixed-point-normal-form} without adding new
records.  By Theorem~\ref{thm:finite-coordinate-determination-boundary}, a local
support claim is determined only by its displayed finite coordinates.  The only
displayed coordinates under the hypotheses are the two inventory files, the four
post-inventory logs, and the regenerated digest table.  Hence the later claim
can use no support surface beyond the tuple just identified.  To assert theorem
semantics, upload, archive replacement, fresh Lean/BEDC rebuilding, daemon
execution, or dynamic artifact validation would require additional command,
source, environment, exit-code, and log data; those are precisely the missing
coordinates isolated in Proposition~\ref{audit:command-run-boundary-inventory}.
Thus the replay criterion is stable and non-escalating.
A later inventory rewrite changes one of the byte strings in the tuple.  Since
the regenerated digest table is part of the tuple and records hashes of the old
bytes, the later state has a different finite coordinate.  The criterion is
therefore stable under byte-level replay of the recorded tuple, but it is not an
automatic certificate for a changed inventory state.
\end{proof}

\begin{corollary}[Round-four local fixed point and external-support boundary]
\label{cor:round-four-local-fixed-point-external-boundary}
Assume that the current round-four source state contains
\path{theorem_inventory.json} and \path{theorem_inventory.md}, that the
theorem-environment extractor and the three retained finite-record verifiers are
rerun after those inventory files exist, and that
\path{review_bundle/FINAL_DIGESTS_SHA256.md} is regenerated after the resulting
logs are written.  Then the support tuple of
Corollary~\ref{cor:current-post-inventory-support-tuple-instantiation} is a
closed local fixed point for the theorem-inventory coordinate of the present
package.  This fixed point does not instantiate external support unless one of
the two external coordinates of
Corollary~\ref{cor:upload-or-archive-instantiation-record} is also supplied:
either exact supplemental upload with matching final digests, or an
archive-equivalent public locator with matching file list, path semantics,
manifest identity, SHA--256 rows, and pinned-commit or same-state branch-head
byte equality.  In the absence of such an upload receipt, DOI, completed
supplemental upload, or byte-equality locator, all bundle-backed and
theorem-inventory-backed claims remain local/path-verified only.
\end{corollary}

\begin{proof}
The first three hypotheses are exactly the ordered construction required in
Theorem~\ref{thm:post-inventory-package-fixed-point}: the inventory files are
present before the extractor and finite-record verifiers run, their logs are
therefore post-inventory command records, and the final digest table is computed
only after those log bytes exist.  Corollary~\ref{cor:current-post-inventory-support-tuple-instantiation}
then identifies the resulting tuple of inventory files, extractor log, verifier
logs, and digest manifest as the current finite support coordinate.  The replay
criterion of Corollary~\ref{cor:stable-replay-post-inventory-tuple} shows that
later uses of this coordinate factor through the same byte tuple unless a new
inventory write or a new command-result coordinate is added.  Hence the tuple is
closed as a local fixed point for the theorem-inventory coordinate.

The remaining assertion is the external-support separation.  The local tuple
contains byte paths, zero-exit command logs, and SHA--256 rows, but none of the
finite fields that Corollary~\ref{cor:upload-or-archive-instantiation-record}
requires for external instantiation.  Exact upload requires the selected
supplemental surface to be supplied to the venue with matching final digests.
Archive replacement requires the public locator test of
Theorem~\ref{thm:public-branch-archive-equivalent-realization}: same finite file
list, same relative paths, same manifest identity, same digest rows, and an
immutable or same-state branch-head byte-equality record.  By
Proposition~\ref{prop:no-external-instantiation-from-local-bundle-records}, those
external coordinates cannot be inferred from the local bundle, bibliography row,
or digest table alone.  Therefore, unless one of the two external coordinates is
actually supplied, the closed fixed point remains local/path-verified and proves
no upload, DOI, archive replacement, venue acceptance, Lean/BEDC rebuild, daemon
rerun, or dynamic artifact-validation claim.
\end{proof}

This long technical supplement reports path-verified source architecture and case evidence.
It does not claim a fresh rebuild of the full newmath source tree.  A command-run
claim is outside the present evidence level unless the source commit,
environment, command, exit code, and log path are recorded with the submission.
This boundary is deliberate: the workflow is meant to make unsupported claims
visible rather than to hide them behind generated prose.

For the current presentation-only submission route, the public source link is
the newmath repository \cite{bedc-newmath-source} at pinned commit
\path{3fb3d6a0641767388a401883062aa522ea0b397b}.  The automath workspace record
and the review-bundle record are treated as submission-local support notes, not
ordinary external bibliography items.  They provide extracted line-range records,
case-evidence manifests, submission-local provenance, and the technical
supplement only when uploaded exactly with the submission and matching final
digests, as required by
Proposition~\ref{audit:review-bundle-availability-condition}.
No DOI, institutional archive, dynamic validation service, or fresh command-run
evidence is claimed here; the bundle-dependent evidence claim is conditional on
that supplemental surface being available to reviewers.

\section{Conclusion and support-boundary summary}

The supplement's theorem chain proves a finite-record certificate calculus for
publication claims under record-gate soundness.  The core mathematical/software
claim is not that AI output is proof evidence, that the workflow is a theorem
prover, that all BEDC declarations have been freshly rebuilt, or that the Rule110
artifact suite has been rerun for this submission.  It is that a theory-to-paper
pipeline can preserve traceability by separating source evidence, deterministic
gates, agent suggestions, human promotion decisions, and evidence-level
boundaries, and by rejecting stronger readings whose finite certificate fields
are absent.

The closing support records identify what may travel with a presentation-only
submission: the primary two-page article, this technical supplement, the bounded
theorem-obligation ledger, and the review bundle if it is uploaded exactly
with matching final digests or replaced by an archive-equivalent locator.  If
that external support surface is absent, the bundle-backed claims remain local
path-verified records.  In all cases the theorem inventory indexes obligations
only; the printed TeX statements and proofs, not the inventory, discharge the
mathematical obligations of the finite-record safety theorem chain.
